# Build 7 — fleet coordination, PR I: the server core

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A program becomes a live, observable, coordinated thing *server-side*: `~/.ccrc/coord.db` holds runs, work items and mail; a session can POST mail to another session and have it land — idle-timed, verified, replayed until acked — inside that session's own input box; a coordinator can open a run, dispatch a wave under enforced caps, and close it with a done-claim the server **re-measures** rather than believes.

**Architecture:** One new `server/src/coord/` directory (opener, store, prhistory reader, git-ref reader, fingerprint, envelope), one new watcher lane (`MAIL_SWEEP_MS`, `void`-dispatched, never awaited), six new routes, one widened `HookState`, one additive `FleetMsg` frame and two additive `NotifyEvent` kinds. Storage is `node:sqlite`'s `DatabaseSync` — synchronous, zero native deps, `user_version` as the schema key, migrations that **refuse to start** rather than start empty. **Zero new ccd verbs**: every fleet mutation rides `CCD_ARGV` entries that are already minted, already granted and already in `ccd caps`.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript ESM (Node ≥22.13, Fastify), `node:sqlite` (built-in, experimental), vitest. **No new dependencies in any package.json** — only `engines` fields.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-07-build7-fleet-coordination-design.md` (approved 2026-08-08; the three **Operator rulings** at `spec:294-302` are binding).

**Scope:** PR I of two. **PR J is not yours** — the coordinator skill, its four-homes install lane, the `/runs` screen, the mail feed, the session mail strip and every renderer. Where PR I lands a wire type or a store slot that PR J will render, this plan says so and stops at the seam.

**Branch:** one PR, `feat/build7-core`, cut from `docs/build7-spec` (which carries the approved spec and is itself cut from `main` at `00ec0a1`). The orchestrator merges and deploys.

**Baseline for every anchor:** `00ec0a1` (current `main`), which `docs/build7-spec` has not moved code from — the two commits on it are spec documents. Every `file:line` below was re-derived by `grep`/`read` against this tree while writing the plan. **An anchor here is a snapshot at plan-writing time, not a live index**: trust the shipped source's own comments once execution starts moving lines.

---

Each names the minimal faithful adaptation rather than silently redesigning.

### D-1 (blocking) — "dispatches wave N+1 **fresh** into the same workspace" is not reachable with zero new ccd verbs

`spec:214-216` describes the coordinator dispatching a *fresh* session into a held workspace at each wave boundary, and `spec:97-101` forbids any new ccd verb. Those two cannot both hold. Measured: `cmd_ensure` (`ccd/ccd:6851-6858`) picks `mode=resume` whenever `$REG/<id>.started` is `1`, and `cmd_ws_add` sets `started 1` at creation (`ccd/ccd:1115`). The only thing that would make `ensure` spawn `new` is clearing that field — a registry **write**, which the agent's whitelist forbids the server outright (`agent/src/whitelist.ts:78-80`: writes are `.cc-clips` and nothing else). `CCD_ARGV.start(w, p, wd)` is not a substitute: it derives the id as `<wrapper>-<project>` (`ccd/ccd:105`), so pointing it at a workspace workdir would seize the project's **main-checkout** session id, not the workspace's.

**Adaptation (orchestrator-amended):** dispatch for wave N ≥ 2 is `CCD_ARGV.ensure(id)` — the pane comes back with the harness resuming its own transcript — **followed by a `/clear` injected through `sendPrompt`'s full proof discipline** inside the same KeyedQueue slot (the exact move ccd itself makes with `/compact` from `_auto_compact_check`, upgraded from blind send-keys to the verified path). `/clear` discards the resumed context and rotates the harness uuid (`_sync_uuid` refreshes the registry copy within one supervise tick, so mail attribution re-arms itself); the wave brief is then delivered as **mail**, through the delivery lane, into a genuinely fresh context. Freshness is thereby mechanical again; only brief *quality* stays discipline (`docs/superpowers/programs/TEMPLATE.md:22-36`). The dispatch route performs all three steps and records them on the run row (`resumed: 1`, `clearedAt`); a `/clear` whose send is refused (dialog open, draft present) leaves the run in `dispatched` with the refusal recorded, retried by the coordinator like any failed step. The named ccd change (a `--fresh` flag on `ensure`, or `ws-dispatch`) is **still recorded and not invented** — it remains the cleaner eventual path.

**Amended (found in Task 2 review):** this deviation names `clearedAt` on the run row and `RunSummary.clearedAt`'s docstring (Task 3) calls it "the proof the second step ran" — but no task below ever adds the column. Task 2's v1 DDL as drafted stops at `resumed`; `CoordStore.hydrateRun` was then written to hardcode `clearedAt: null` for every run, which is indistinguishable on the wire from D-1's own refusal case (a `/clear` whose send was refused). Fixed in Task 2 directly rather than deferred to a Task 9 migration that was never scheduled: `runs.clearedAt INTEGER` lands in the v1 DDL now — `coord.db` exists on no box yet (`README.md` still says "No database"), so amending v1 before it has ever been observed costs nothing, versus a migration 2 landing mid-PR against a schema no deploy has ever seen. `hydrateRun` reads the real column. Nothing writes it yet — Task 9's dispatch route is still what performs the `/clear` and is still what will call the setter — so every run's `clearedAt` stays honestly `null` until then, exactly as before, but now because nothing has cleared anything rather than because the column doesn't exist.

### D-2 (blocking) — `no-handoff-commit` cannot mean "the commit touches the ledger"

`spec:127-132` wants the coordinator to re-measure `{branchTip, prNumber, prPhase, handoffCommit}`. Three of the four are reachable. The fourth is not, and the reason is structural: `EXEC_COMMANDS` is the closed set `['tmux','ccd']` (`agent/src/whitelist.ts:134`), so the server can never run `git`; and `FleetIO` (`server/src/io.ts:11-24`) reads bytes, so a commit object — a zlib-deflated loose object, or a slice of a packfile — is not readable in any useful sense. **Nothing server-side can tell whether a commit edits `docs/superpowers/programs/<slug>.md`.**

**Adaptation:** `no-handoff-commit` is redefined, in writing, as a **correspondence** check, not a content check: the claim is rejected when `handoffCommit` is absent, is not 40 hex, or is not equal to the `branchTip` the same claim carries. So the worker cannot report a handoff that is not the branch's own tip, and cannot report a tip the branch does not have (`stale-tip` covers that half). Whether the tip commit is a *real* handoff stays exactly where `spec:246-252` already puts it — "briefs are written prose reviewed like code" — i.e. the coordinator's ordinary review of the commit. The route's own docstring says this so nobody later reads the token as a stronger guarantee than it is.

### D-3 (additive, forced by the spec's own requirements) — two tables beyond the six §3 names

`spec:106-117` names six tables. Two of its own sentences need more:

- *"Every transition records who caused it"* (`spec:126`) is a history, not a column. → `run_events`.
- *"Every rejection is itself recorded (a rejected message is a fact about the fleet)"* (`spec:147-148`). A rejected ingress has **no** `mail` row by construction — it may carry an unknown recipient, a stale uuid or a forged sender, and writing it into `mail` would put unattributable rows in the table the delivery lane walks. → `mail_rejections`.

Both are additive and are named in the schema's own comment as the spec's requirements rather than as embellishment.

### D-4 (accepted hazard, stated) — `deploy/ccrc.service` sources no `EnvironmentFile`, and the live server is in remote mode anyway

`deploy/ccrc.service:1-13` has no `EnvironmentFile=` line, while `deploy/deploy.sh:101` ships `deploy/ccrc.env` to `~/.ccrc/ccrc.env` and `deploy.sh:103` copies the repo's unit file over the installed one on **every** server deploy. Yet the live server answers `{"mode":"remote"}`, which requires `CCRC_FLEET`, `CCRC_AGENT_URL` and `CCRC_AGENT_TOKEN` to be reaching it from somewhere this repo does not show — most likely a hand-made drop-in under `~/.config/systemd/user/ccrc.service.d/` on `server-box`.

**Adaptation: this PR does not touch `ccrc.service`, and the mail token is therefore NOT an env var.** Adding `EnvironmentFile=` to a unit whose live environment we cannot read risks flipping a production server to `CCRC_FLEET=local` (the value `ccrc.env.example:12` ships) on the next deploy. The token is read from a **file on the server box** — `~/.ccrc/mail.token`, path configurable via `CCRC_MAIL_TOKEN_PATH` — which `deploy.sh` can ship with no unit-file change at all. `config.ts` stays a pure env→config function; the file read happens at the composition root beside `notifyLog.load()`. The unit-file gap is real and is left as a finding for the operator, not silently patched by an agent that cannot see the box.

### D-5 (correction to the task framing) — run close **releases**, it does not archive

The spec's mapping table is explicit that "a coordinator that archives autonomously runs *through* [the merged-archive] gate, not around it" (`spec:89-95` and the Build 2.5 lifecycle at `spec:214-217`, "releases on final merge"). `FleetWatcher.archiveMerged` (`server/src/watch.ts:795-855`) already archives a merged, unheld workspace on a level-triggered sweep, and it skips a held one. So the honest close is `CCD_ARGV.wsRelease(id)` on the final wave and `CCD_ARGV.wsHold(id, 'program:<slug> wave:N+1/M')` on every other one — the existing sweep does the archiving on its own clock, with its own `archiveSafety` gate.

`CCD_ARGV.wsArchive` is used on **one** path only: an explicit abandon (`state: 'failed'`, `archive: true`), mirroring `POST /api/sessions/:id/archive` (`server/src/server.ts:599-614`) including its `verbSupported` 501.

### D-6 (measurement, carried) — the node floor number must be verified, not remembered

`node:sqlite` shipped in Node 22.5.0 behind `--experimental-sqlite` and stopped needing the flag in a later 22.x. This plan writes `>=22.13.0` into `engines` **and pins it with a test that actually imports `node:sqlite` and asserts `DatabaseSync` exists on the running interpreter** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:31,72`). Measured live while writing this plan: `node v24.14.1` on the fleet host imports it clean; the spec records `v22.22.3` proven on `server-box` (`spec:36-38`).

**Amended after Task 1's implementation review:** the sentence this replaces overstated what the import assertion catches. It is absolute — no `package.json` read, fails on any interpreter below the real flag boundary no matter what `engines.node` says — but it only *fires* when the interpreter CI actually resolves for `node-version: '22'` happens to sit below that boundary. `setup-node` resolves the latest 22.x release, which sits well above any candidate boundary, so the assertion staying green is evidence the interpreter that ran the suite could do it — it is not independent confirmation that `22.13.0` specifically is Node's real number. That confirmation still doesn't exist anywhere in this plan or its tests; the Self-review's unverified-items list already says so (item (b), below) and this amendment stops D-6 from implying otherwise. **The test is a floor-crossing detector, not a historical-accuracy check on the constant `22.13.0`; the number in `engines` is still what moves if assertion 3 ever goes red.**

### D-7 (naming, non-negotiable) — `tasks` is taken, in three packages

`shared/api.ts:82-105` owns `TaskStatus`/`TaskItem`/`TaskProgress`; `SessionStreamMsg` already has a `{type:'tasks'}` arm (`shared/api.ts:1260`); `server/src/tasks/read.ts` and `pwa/src/session/TaskStrip.tsx` consume them. They are Claude Code's TodoWrite plan items and have nothing to do with programs. This build's unit is **`WorkItem`**, its table is `work_items`, and the tally on the wire is `items: { done, total }` — never `tasks`. `server/test/single-definition.test.ts` gains a guard so a second `WorkItem` cannot grow anywhere in the four source roots.

### D-8 (correction, found in Task 2 review) — two of the five v1 enum columns have no we-do-not-know member, and Task 3 as drafted below never gives them one

`schema.ts`'s header comment claimed, verbatim, that "every enum column has a designated we-do-not-know member on the READ side" — the Global Constraint quoted above ("every enum has a designated we-do-not-know member") — for all five enum columns the v1 DDL declares. Two are not covered by anything in this plan: `programs.state` (`active|paused|done|abandoned`) and `mail_deliveries.state` (`queued|delivered|acked|rejected`). Task 3 as drafted below (`CoordStore.programs()` returning `{ slug, title, state: string }`; `MailSummary.state: 'queued' | 'delivered' | 'acked' | 'rejected'` with no `'unknown'` arm) does not close the gap — it reproduces it on the wire, which is what PR J renders.

**Adaptation:** the schema.ts comment is narrowed (Task 2) to name the three columns it actually covers (`runs.state`, `work_items.state`, `mail.kind`, all landing as `RunState`/`WorkItemState`/`MailKind` in Task 3) and to flag the two exempt ones by name rather than claim blanket coverage. **Task 3, when executed, must not copy `programs()`'s and `MailSummary.state`'s shapes verbatim from this document**: `programs.state` needs a `ProgramState` type (`'active' | 'paused' | 'done' | 'abandoned' | 'unknown'`) with an `isProgramState` guard on the same pattern as `isWorkItemState`, and `MailSummary.state` needs an `'unknown'` arm alongside `'queued' | 'delivered' | 'acked' | 'rejected'`, both read through the guard rather than cast — the same rule `:1226` already states for `RunState`/`WorkItemState`/`MailKind`. Until Task 3 lands this, a `programs.state` or `mail_deliveries.state` value written by a newer build and read by this one renders as nothing or is silently never matched by a `WHERE state = …` query — the exact failure `PrPhase`'s `'unchecked'` precedent exists to prevent.

**Closed (Task 3, `d806d3d`):** both gaps are shut — `ProgramState`/`isProgramState` and `MailDeliveryState`/`isMailDeliveryState` both landed with an `'unknown'` arm, and `CoordStore.programs()` reads through the guard rather than casting. `schema.ts`'s header and its two inline DDL comments were left saying the opposite after Task 3 landed (a separate finding, fixed in the Task 2 review this amendment accompanies) — this line is the record that D-8 itself is done, so it does not get re-opened against work already merged.

### D-9 (correction, found in Task 3 review) — `RUN_TRANSITIONS` had no edge into `closing` from where Task 9 actually closes a run

`RUN_TRANSITIONS` as landed only reached `closing` via `merging`, but no writer in this PR ever advances a run to `awaiting-review` or `merging` — Task 9's dispatch route advances only to `dispatched`, and its close route immediately does `advance(id, 'closing')` next. Every close in the build would have 409'd with `bad-transition` the first time it ran; the shipped `coord-store.test.ts` never exercised this because its only transition test stops at `working`.

**Adaptation:** `dispatched` and `working` both gain a direct edge to `closing`. `awaiting-review`/`merging` stay in the table and in `RUN_TRANSITIONS` unchanged — PR J's `POST /api/runs/:id/advance` is what reaches them; PR I never does.

### D-10 (bug, found in Task 3 review) — `dueDeliveries` only ever encoded the `queued` arm; replay-until-ack (spec:174-177) was unreachable

The shipped query was `WHERE state = 'queued' AND nextAttemptAt <= ?`. `markDelivered` moves a row's `state` to `'delivered'`, and nothing in the file ever moves it back — so once a delivery was injected, an unacked recipient could never be re-selected. `deliveredAt`/`ingestedAt` were already in the return shape with no arm that could ever produce a non-null value for them, which was the tell.

**Adaptation:** `dueDeliveries` gains a second parameter, `replayMs`, and a second WHERE arm: `OR (state = 'delivered' AND COALESCE(ingestedAt, deliveredAt) + replayMs <= now)`. `replayMs` is NOT a store-owned constant — `MAIL_REPLAY_MS` stays in `watch.ts` (Task 8) and the caller passes it in, the same way `capsUsage`/`markDispatched` take `now` from the caller rather than reading the clock internally. **Task 8's implementer**: `store.dueDeliveries(now)` in this plan's Step 6 body must become `store.dueDeliveries(now, MAIL_REPLAY_MS)` — a one-line adaptation the shape-authoritative-not-text-authoritative rule already covers, called out here so it is not a surprise. `schema.ts`'s v1 DDL also gains `mail_deliveries_replay(state, deliveredAt)`, the index this arm needs the way `mail_deliveries_due` already covers the `queued` arm — landed in v1 directly rather than a migration 2, on the same reasoning D-1's `clearedAt` amendment already gives (`coord.db` has been observed on no box yet).

### D-11 (bug, found in Task 3 review) — `reconstruct` accepted `registry.held` and never read it; the plan's own Step-4 rule 2 was dropped

Step 4's second rule is explicit: "The last wave in `reconstruct` takes its state from the hold, not from a guess: a held workspace whose ledger shows wave N of M with no handoff commit is `working`." The shipped body derived state purely from `handoffCommit !== null ? 'done' : 'working'` — `held` was typed, accepted, and never read. The shipped reconstruction drill fixture happens to be held, so it could not have told the two cases apart.

**Adaptation:** the last wave in the ledger, when it has no handoff commit, is `working` while `registry.held !== null` and `failed` when the hold is also gone — a released workspace backing a wave with no handoff commit has nothing live to call `working`, and `spec:82-85`'s "nothing is invented" applies to the state column too. Every earlier wave keeps the original `handoffCommit !== null ? 'done' : …` formula (the ledger is append-only, so this only ever matters for the last wave).

### D-12 (bug, found in Task 3 review) — `reconstruct`'s `claimedBy = NULL` rows permanently disarmed `openRun`'s one-coordinator refusal for that program

`openRun`'s guard read `SELECT claimedBy FROM runs WHERE program = ? ORDER BY id LIMIT 1` — the absolute first row. `reconstruct` inserts every rebuilt run with `claimedBy` bound to `NULL` (it has no way to know who will resume the program), so after any disaster rebuild the lowest-id row for that program carried `NULL` forever and the guard's `existing?.claimedBy != null` was false for every subsequent caller — a second coordinator was silently let in.

**Adaptation:** the guard query gains `AND claimedBy IS NOT NULL`, so it finds the first row a real `openRun` actually claimed rather than the first row full stop. A reconstructed-but-not-yet-resumed program is claimable by the first coordinator that calls `openRun` on it, and refuses every one after — the intended behaviour, now reachable.

### D-13 (bug, found in Task 3 review) — `capsUsage().running` counted `planned` runs, wedging the concurrency cap on a botched dispatch

`SELECT count(*) FROM runs WHERE state NOT IN ('done','failed')` counts `planned` — a run with no session, no workspace and no worker, exactly the state Task 9's `ambiguous-dispatch` refusal deliberately leaves a run in. Three botched dispatches, or a coordinator that pre-opens several waves before dispatching wave 1, would pin `running` at the default `maxConcurrentWorkers = 3` against zero live workers, with no route in this PR that can un-wedge it.

**Adaptation:** `running`'s predicate gains `dispatchedAt IS NOT NULL` — the one column only `markDispatched` ever sets, so it names the runs that actually hold a session rather than every non-terminal state.

### D-14 (bug, found in Task 4 review) — `readPrHistory`'s null-read arm could refuse a file that appeared mid-check

The shipped reader did `io.readFile` → (null) → `io.readdir`, and refused whenever the listing named the file — indistinguishable from a file that is genuinely unreadable. But ccd's one append chokepoint (`ccd/ccd:849-859`) fires on a `prnumber` replacement, which lands exactly at a merge, and a run close is exactly what happens at a merge too: a `.prhistory` created in the gap between the read and the listing — by the watcher's 120s sweep or a PR-sheet `GET`, neither serialised against the close — named itself in the listing with nothing behind the first read, and was reported `unreadable` about a file that was present, readable and correct. The module's own docstring cited `registry.ts`'s `HOLD_UNREADABLE` mechanism as its model and even flagged the gap as "a WS round trip wide" in remote mode, but `registry.ts` closes that gap with a **second listing** confirming an ambiguous read (`registry.ts:158-176`); this reader took the first listing alone and skipped the confirming look — the mirror image of the bug `registry.ts`'s comment says it exists to prevent.

**Adaptation:** on the null-read arm, a listing naming the file gets a **second read**, not a second listing — a read is the only op that can produce the actual entries, and refusal is reserved for a file that is still unreadable on that second attempt. Pinned by `coord-prhistory.test.ts`'s stub-`FleetIO` race case (the listing's own callback writes the file, modelling "something else just created it"), which fails against the pre-fix ordering.

### D-15 (correction, found in Task 4 review) — the MALFORMED rung's docstring credited ccd with a ladder ccd does not implement, and the divergence was unrecorded

The docstring said "THE THREE-ANSWER LADDER IS ccd'S OWN (`ccd/ccd:2018-2035`)" and gave MALFORMED as "the lines that parse, in order." Read against the cited lines, ccd's own comment says the opposite by name — "MALFORMED -> `[]`, raw file untouched on disk" (`ccd/ccd:2020-2022`) — and its implementation matches: a list comprehension over every line inside one `try` (`ccd/ccd:2046`), one bad line raises, the `except ValueError` (`ccd/ccd:2060-2061`) discards the whole `rows` down to `[]`. This repo's own `ccd-prhistory.test.ts:105` pins that whole-file behaviour on the ccd side. The plan's own prose at Task 4 (above the Step-3 code block) already said "malformed is `[]` with the raw file untouched," agreeing with ccd; only the code block — followed correctly, per the shape-authoritative rule — implements per-line salvage instead, and nothing recorded that the two had come apart.

Kept as a deliberate divergence rather than reverted to match ccd: ccd's writer only ever appends with `O_APPEND`, so the one line ever at genuine risk of tearing is the LAST one (the scout's own caveat, `coord-prhistory.test.ts:29-31`), and discarding a good record because a later one tore is a worse failure than the one ccd is guarding against. The cost — this reader and ccd's own `ws-archive` fold can disagree about a workspace's PR lineage on a ledger whose bad line isn't the tail — is accepted and now stated in the reader's own docstring, along with the second-order divergence review finding 2 named: ccd performs no shape validation at all, so a line that parses as JSON but fails this reader's `pr`/`branch`/`phase`/`recordedAt` type guards is dropped here the same as an unparseable one, with nothing on `PrHistoryRead` distinguishing a genuinely empty ledger from one that had a line rejected. That gap is left unsignalled for now rather than given a new arm on the return type: the one prospective consumer of such a signal is the run-close route, which Task 9 has not yet built in this tree, and shaping the signal ahead of its actual error contract would be a guess.

**Adaptation:** `prhistory.ts`'s docstring is rewritten to name only ABSENT and UNREADABLE as ccd's own rungs, states the MALFORMED divergence and both its costs explicitly (mixed-file disagreement with ccd; unsignalled shape-rejection), and this Task 4 section above is left as-is with this entry as its correction — the shape-authoritative code block was followed as drafted; the comment above it is what gets corrected, here rather than by re-editing history.

### D-16 (test-coverage, found in Task 4 review) — the `listing === null` refusal arm and the four shape-reject predicates had no test, and Task 11's mutation table credited coverage it didn't have

Every case in the originally-shipped `coord-prhistory.test.ts` ran against a registry directory that existed and was readable, so `io.readdir` never returned `null` anywhere in the suite — the arm production actually takes in remote mode (`README.md:445`: `readdir` is an agent round trip there). Task 11's mutation table (`readPrHistory`: unreadable → `{ok:true, entries:[]}`) named exactly one prhistory mutant, for the `includes` arm alone; mutating the null-check independently (`listing !== null && listing.includes(name)`) reproduced all four then-committed test results byte-for-byte. Separately, the four shape guards (line 50-52 of the original) were pinned only in the accept direction — the suite's one negative case is a torn tail that never reaches them (`JSON.parse` throws first) — so deleting all four guards was also a silent survivor.

**Adaptation:** `coord-prhistory.test.ts` gains three cases — a registry directory that does not exist, a registry directory `chmod 000`'d (both exercising `listing === null` with no root dependency), and a two-line ledger of valid-JSON-wrong-shape records (`{"pr":"577",...}` / `{}`) pinning the reject side of the shape guards. All three were confirmed to kill their respective mutants (measured, isolated copies, sha256-verified restore between) and are added to the mutation table below alongside the existing `includes`-arm and second-read (D-14) rows.

### D-17 (bug, found in Task 5 review) — `verifyDone` re-measured a branch column `markDispatched` freezes once, dead before any `worker_done` could exist

`DoneRun.branch`, as originally typed, read field-for-field like the `runs` row it is built from — but `runs.branch` is written exactly once, by `markDispatched` at dispatch time (`store.ts:138-142`), and `FleetWatcher.sweepNames` (`watch.ts:545-610`) renames the workspace's registry branch autonomously, on the ordinary path, strictly before the first push (`cmd_ws_rename`'s one hard refusal is `has-upstream` — a branch that has been pushed is never renamed) — i.e. before any `worker_done` this claim could be. Every ordinary wave-1 close was measuring a branch ref git had already deleted, with no repair route for `runs.branch` anywhere in this PR: a permanent `tip-unmeasurable`, retried forever.

**Adaptation:** `verifyDone` resolves the branch to re-measure from the LIVE registry (`readRegistry`, keyed on `sessionId`) — the same read `sweepNames`/`sweepPr` already do — and falls back to `run.branch` only when the registry no longer carries a row for that session at all (retired, purged). `DoneRun`'s docstring is rewritten to say so explicitly, so a future reader does not mistake the field for "the run's branch" rather than "the run's branch, if the registry has forgotten it." Pinned by `coord-fingerprint.test.ts`'s "measures the RENAMED branch the registry names, not the born branch frozen on the run row" case, and the fallback case beside it.

### D-18 (bug, found in Task 5 review) — `prPhase`/`prNumber` were typed but never runtime-validated, so a malformed claim could read as agreement

`DoneClaim`'s own docstring says every field is UNTRUSTED input off an HTTP body, and `branchTip`/`handoffCommit` were validated against the `SHA` regex — but `prPhase` and `prNumber` were trusted to be a `PrPhase` and a `number|null` on the strength of a TypeScript annotation alone; no route in this PR, or its plan, names a parse step between the JSON body and this claim. A claim that OMITTED `prPhase`, or spelled it in an out-of-vocabulary string, fell through every arm of `prVerdict` unmatched and read as `ok` on a branch with no PR at all — where the well-formed, honest claim is correctly refused. Separately, the guard read `claim.prNumber !== null` rather than `typeof claim.prNumber === 'number'`, so a non-number `prNumber` reached the mismatch arm and mailed back a detail string naming `PR #undefined`.

**Adaptation:** both fields are validated where `branchTip`/`handoffCommit` already are — `isPrPhase(claim.prPhase)` and a `typeof` check on `claim.prNumber` — refusing `pr-unmeasurable` before either fact is re-measured. Pinned by three new cases: an omitted `prPhase`, an out-of-vocabulary `prPhase` (`'OPEN'`), and a non-number `prNumber`, each checked against a fixture (`pr: 'none'`) whose well-formed baseline case is proven to refuse first, so the malformed cases are not coincidentally refused for some unrelated reason.

### D-19 (bug, found in Task 5 review) — a readable-but-non-SHA loose ref fell through to a STALE `packed-refs` entry, which is not how git resolves

The shipped `readBranchTip` read the loose ref, and only fell through to `packed-refs` when `SHA.test` on its trimmed content failed — folding "loose ref absent" and "loose ref present but not a SHA" (the shape a symref holds) into the same fallthrough. Git does not work this way: a loose ref, once written, SHADOWS the packed entry entirely, and `packed-refs` can hold a stale tip for the same branch after `git pack-refs`. The docstring even said the opposite ("A `.git` this process cannot read, and a symref, are both null here"), while the code fell through for the symref case. On a workspace branch packed at an OLD tip and later moved by a real commit, a torn write, an `EISDIR`, or a `git symbolic-ref` would resolve to the OLD tip from `packed-refs` instead of `null` — and a stale `worker_done` naming OLD would settle the run.

**Adaptation:** a readable loose ref now returns immediately — `SHA.test(v) ? v : null` — never falling through to `packed-refs` whether or not it parses as a SHA. Pinned by "never falls through a PRESENT loose ref to a stale packed-refs entry," a fixture with loose=symref, packed=OTHER, that fails against the pre-fix fallthrough and passes against the fix.

### D-20 (bug, found in Task 5 review) — the project guard's character class admitted `.` and `..`, a one-level escape above `projectsRoot`

`/^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$/.test(project)` accepts `..` — the character class includes `.` with no `includes('..')` companion, unlike the branch guard one line above whose docstring names exactly this class of escape. `path.join(projectsRoot, '..', '.git')` normalises to the parent of `projectsRoot`'s own `.git`; a run opened with `project: '..'` (Task 9's open route takes `project` off the request body) would have its done-claim re-measured against whatever branch of that name exists one level up — a ref outside the run's own repository entirely, satisfying done-authority against the wrong tree.

**Adaptation:** the guard gains an explicit `|| project === '.' || project === '..'`, matching the branch guard's own explicit `..` exclusion one line above. Pinned by "refuses a project name that could climb out of the projects root," which plants a decoy tip at both resolution targets (`root` and `projectsRoot` itself) so a guard-less implementation returns a real, wrong tip rather than merely finding nothing.

### D-21 (test-coverage, found in Task 5 review) — the branch-traversal test's four escape targets did not exist in the fixture, so both path guards could be deleted and the suite stayed green

`refuses a branch name that could climb out of the ref tree` asserted only `toBeNull()` for four names, none of whose escape targets existed in the fixture — `project(TIP, null)` writes exactly one file, so an implementation with NO path guards at all still answers `null` for every row (the loose read misses, `packed-refs` is absent). The plan's own scheduled mutant (`readBranchTip`: drop the `..` guard) survives for the same reason: with `BRANCH_OK` intact, three of the four strings are already rejected by it, and the one string the `..` guard alone owns (`ws/../../x`) resolves to a path with no file behind it either way.

**Adaptation:** two new cases place a 40-hex decoy at the guard's ACTUAL resolution target — `<root>/demo/.git/refs/x` for the `..`-guard-alone mutant, `<root>/loot` (outside the project entirely) for both-guards-deleted — so an unguarded read returns the decoy sha where the guarded read returns `null`. Both confirmed by mutation: reverting each guard individually flips the matching new test from pass to fail. The mutation table's `readBranchTip`: drop the `..` guard row is amended below to point at the discriminating case.

### D-22 (test-coverage, found in Task 5 review) — the mismatch table and `prVerdict` left seven real code paths unguarded; deleting any one of them kept the suite green

Measured against isolated copies, one mutant at a time: (1) the entire `prNumber` mismatch arm, (2) `prVerdict`'s `measured === 'unknown'` clause — which the table's only `pr-state could not be read` row drives through the exec-failure branch, never through `phaseFor`, so it never actually exercised this arm — (3) `verbSupported` being skipped, (4) the `ok` payload's `measured` shape, (5) `prVerdict`'s `claimed === 'merged' && measured !== 'merged'` arm — every table row holds `prPhase: 'open'` fixed, so this arm never ran — (6) the "pr-state answered no full line" refusal, and (7) the `packed-refs` loop's sha/ref-name column check, all survived with the full suite reporting green. Two are the module's stated contract going unpinned: (2) is the one guard whose docstring names the catastrophe it prevents ("a GitHub outage can never read as… agreement"), and (1) is one of the four facts `spec:125-129` names as re-measured.

**Adaptation:** seven cases added — a `prNumber` mismatch against a really-bound PR, a MERGED row with `mergedAt`/`mergeCommit` omitted (`merge-unproven`, the row that actually reaches `prVerdict`'s `unknown` clause through `phaseFor`), a `ccdVerbs: []` fleet state, an assertion on the `ok` rows' `measured` payload (not just `ok: true`), a claim that says `merged` against a branch still `open`, a `ccd pr-state` exit-0 response whose one line is a session-failure shape (no `rows` array, so `isFullLine` is false for every candidate), and a `packed-refs` line with a 41-hex sha that shifts the ref-name column by exactly one place (`.trim()` still lands on the target ref name; the column check is the only thing that tells this apart from a real entry). All seven confirmed by mutation, one construct at a time, restored between. The mutation table below gains the missing rows and amends the two it already had.

### D-23 (bug + stale comments, found in a later Task 2 review) — `mail_deliveries_replay` is a dead index; schema.ts and D-10 both kept asserting it was the index the replay arm needs, after a later fix moved that arm onto a different index entirely

D-10 above added `mail_deliveries_replay(state, deliveredAt)` "the index this arm needs the way `mail_deliveries_due` already covers the `queued` arm" — true of the query D-10 shipped. It stopped being true the moment a later fix ("mail replay honors backoff", store.ts's `dueDeliveries`) added `AND nextAttemptAt <= ?` to the replay arm too, closing an exponential-backoff gap D-10 itself hadn't caught. That one-line addition put BOTH OR arms on `(state, nextAttemptAt)` — exactly what `mail_deliveries_due` already indexes — and left `mail_deliveries_replay` with no query in the build that could ever choose it: its own second column, `deliveredAt`, plays no part in either arm's `nextAttemptAt` predicate, and the replay arm's other clause, `COALESCE(ingestedAt, deliveredAt) + replayMs <= now`, is an expression over two nullable columns that no plain-column index could serve regardless. Neither `schema.ts`'s inline comment nor D-10's own text was updated when the backoff fix landed, so both went on asserting a query plan that measurement contradicts: `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` against the shipped DDL and the shipped `dueDeliveries` query shows `MULTI-INDEX OR` choosing `mail_deliveries_due` for BOTH branches, byte-identical with or without the second index. Every write to `mail_deliveries` (every queued mail, every `markDelivered`/`markIngested`/`backOff`/ack, on the 10s `MAIL_SWEEP_MS` lane) was maintaining a b-tree nothing in the build ever reads, and a maintainer investigating slow replay scans who reads either comment is pointed away from the actual cause.

**Adaptation:** `mail_deliveries_replay` is dropped from the v1 DDL and `schema.ts`'s comment rewritten to say what is actually true — `mail_deliveries_due` alone serves both `dueDeliveries` arms, measured, not assumed. Same v1-widening reasoning D-1's `clearedAt` amendment and D-10 itself already establish (`coord.db` observed on no box yet, at the time of this fix) — dropping a column is additive-only-safe the same direction adding one is, and a migration 2 to drop an index nobody has ever queried through would be pure ceremony against data that does not exist. Pinned by a new `coord-db.test.ts` case asserting the FULL index set on `mail_deliveries` is exactly `['mail_deliveries_due']`, so a stray reintroduction — under this name or another — fails a test rather than silently costing every write on the mail lane a second b-tree maintenance again.

### D-24 (bug, found in the same later Task 2 review) — the 0-byte "never start empty" guard could be tripped by its own process's aborted first migration, turning one transient, already-remedied failure into a permanent refusal over a message asserting data loss that never happened

`openCoordDb`'s guard against adopting a truncated file as a fresh database (rule 2) ran a real check — `existsSync(dbPath) && statSync(dbPath).size === 0` — but the file it was checking could be one THIS OPEN ATTEMPT created: `new DatabaseSync(path)` creates the file eagerly, at 0 bytes, before any migration writes a byte, and the migration loop's own `catch` block only ever `db.close()`s and throws — nothing removed the 0-byte artifact a first-boot migration failure (disk-full, EIO, a SIGKILL in the window, or any later bad migration) left behind. Reproduced end to end (tsx driving the real `openCoordDb`, `MIGRATIONS[0]` forced to fail, then repaired): boot 1 refused correctly, saying "No table data changed" — true, and the failure's underlying cause (disk full, say) may already be fixed by the time anyone reads it. Boot 2, cause fixed, never reached the migration loop at all: the 0-byte guard fired first, over a file that never held a row, with a message asserting "silently erasing whatever this file held before it was truncated" and directing the operator at a markdown-ledger reconstruction for history that never existed. The service is then down permanently — the guard runs before `new DatabaseSync`, so no later boot can migrate past it — until a human manually removes a file the build itself created and never wrote to.

**Adaptation:** `openCoordDb` records `existedBefore = existsSync(dbPath)` before `new DatabaseSync` runs, and the migration-failure `catch` block removes the file, best-effort, when `shouldRemoveMigrationFailureArtifact(existedBefore, sizeAfterRollback)` says so — true only when `!existedBefore && sizeAfterRollback === 0`, i.e. an empty file this call's own aborted attempt created and never got a row into. A file that predates the process is never touched, at any size, so the top guard's own job (refusing a genuinely truncated pre-existing database) is unchanged and un-weakened — pinned by an existing `coord-db.test.ts` case now calling `openCoordDb` on such a file TWICE and asserting it refuses, and the file survives, both times. `shouldRemoveMigrationFailureArtifact` is isolated as a pure function for the same reason `describeMigrationProgress` already is in this file: `existedBefore === true` reaching this decision with a post-rollback size of 0 cannot happen through `openCoordDb` itself — the top guard already refuses any pre-existing 0-byte file before a migration is ever attempted, and SQLite rolls a failed transaction back to its pre-transaction bytes, never to zero, so a pre-existing NON-empty file cannot become 0 bytes via rollback either. A mutant dropping the `!existedBefore` half of that function would therefore pass every `openCoordDb`-level test in the suite; pinned directly against the function instead, the same treatment `describeMigrationProgress`'s otherwise-unreachable branch already gets. A new `coord-db.test.ts` case forces `MIGRATIONS[0]` to invalid SQL against a path with no pre-existing file, confirms the resulting `CoordDbUnmigratable` leaves no file at all (measured directly, not inferred from a second call succeeding), and confirms a second `openCoordDb` call — cause now fixed — migrates clean instead of hitting the guard.

### D-25 (bug, found in a later Task 3 review) — `runs.handoffCommit` had no writer anywhere in the build outside `reconstruct`'s disaster-recovery drill

`grep -rn 'handoffCommit' server/src` named exactly one write in the whole tree before this fix: `reconstruct`'s INSERT (`store.ts`, then :539). `advance()` writes only `state`/`closedAt` (plus `run_events.detail`); `markDispatched` writes the dispatch triple; `foldPrLineage` writes `prLineage`. There was no `markClosed`/`setHandoffCommit` on `CoordStore` and no other `UPDATE` touching the column. Task 9's own close-path prose ("`advance(id, 'closing', 'coordinator')` then `advance(id, state ?? 'done', 'coordinator')`, recording `handoffCommit`") names no method that can do the recording, because none existed. Measured against a temp `coord.db`: open a run, `markDispatched`, `advance('closing')`, `advance('done')` — `store.run(id).handoffCommit` reads `null`. Every wave this build closes would therefore ship `handoffCommit: null` on `{type:'runs'}` and `GET /api/runs` forever, and `handoffCommit` is one of the four facts spec:125-132 names as re-measured — D-2 redefines `no-handoff-commit` around exactly this field, so the claim is checked (`fingerprint.ts:108-111`) and then has nowhere to land.

**Adaptation:** `CoordStore.setHandoffCommit(runId, handoffCommit)` lands now, in Task 3's own file, on `foldPrLineage`'s pattern — a single-column `UPDATE`, called once, at close. Task 9's close route (not yet written in this tree) is the intended caller; this is the write path it needs, landed ahead of it the same way D-1's `clearedAt` amendment landed the column ahead of the route that writes it. Pinned directly: `coord-store.test.ts` writes then reads the column back through `run()`.

**Amended (found in a later Task 9 review, D-51's own review pass):** "not yet written in this tree" stopped being true the moment Task 9 shipped `a4d5207` — the close route's `coord.setHandoffCommit(id, claim.handoffCommit)` call is exactly the intended caller this sentence predicted, landed on schedule. Left uncorrected here for two commits, which is a smaller version of the exact gap D-51 records for this same deviation's sibling below.

### D-26 (bug, found in a later Task 3 review) — `programs.state` had no writer beyond `openRun`'s hardcoded `'active'`, permanently disarming `resolveCoordinator(null)` the moment a second program exists

The only writes to `programs` were the two `INSERT … ON CONFLICT(slug) DO UPDATE SET title = excluded.title` (`openRun`, `reconstruct`) — the conflict arm never touches `state`, and there was no `setProgramState`, no `UPDATE programs` anywhere in `server/src`, and no route in PR I's plan or PR J's route list that can move a program to `paused|done|abandoned`. `resolveCoordinator(null)` requires exactly one row with `state = 'active'` and answers `null` (ambiguous or absent) otherwise, which the mail ingress route turns into a recorded `unknown-recipient` 404. Measured with the real store: open program `p1` (claimed by `coordA`) — `resolveCoordinator(null)` is `coordA`. Open program `p2`, even after every run of `p1` is `done` — `resolveCoordinator(null)` is `null`, permanently: nothing in the build can ever move `p1` out of `active`. A fleet running more than one program over its lifetime is this build's ordinary steady state, not an edge case, so every `toId:'coordinator'` mail with no `runId` after a program's first turn is refused for the rest of the database's life. It also leaves the whole non-`active` half of D-8's `ProgramState` vocabulary unreachable by any writer in the build.

**Adaptation:** `CoordStore.setProgramState(slug, state)` lands now, mirroring `setWorkItemState`'s shape — the writer `programs.state` needed and never had. *Who* calls it and *when* a program should retire is a routing/policy decision belonging to whichever task closes the last run of a program (Task 9, not yet written in this tree) — out of Task 3's own remit, the same boundary D-1's `clearedAt` amendment already draws between "the write path exists" and "the route that drives it." Pinned directly: `coord-store.test.ts` opens two programs, confirms `resolveCoordinator(null)` reads `null` while both are `active`, retires the first with `setProgramState`, and confirms it resolves the second — the failure scenario, demonstrated at the store level, and its fix.

**Amended (found in a later Task 9 review — D-51 below):** Task 9 shipped the close route and never called this writer — "not yet written in this tree" went uncorrected for two commits after it stopped being true, describing a caller that had, by then, been written and had simply never wired the call. D-51 records the measured failure this left live (a second program permanently starving `resolveCoordinator`) and the fix (the close route now calls this method once it knows the run it just closed was the last one). This sentence is left as the plan-writing-time snapshot rather than silently corrected in place, per this section's own "an anchor here is a snapshot at plan-writing time" rule and the D-6/D-9 precedent for amending a deviation's own prior claim by appending to it, not rewriting it.

### D-27 (test-coverage, found in a later Task 3 review) — D-8's record said SHUT everywhere; three of its four guarded read sites had no test able to tell the guard from a cast

D-8's own closing note (`:88` above) and `schema.ts`'s header/DDL comments (then `:7-20`, `:41`, `:109`) all say the we-do-not-know gap is shut, on the strength of the guard existing in the read path. Measured, one mutant at a time, full `coord-store.test.ts` + `mail-routes.test.ts` restored between (sha256-verified): replacing `isProgramState(r.state) ? r.state : 'unknown'` with `r.state as ProgramState` in `programs()`, `isMailDeliveryState(row.state) ? row.state : 'unknown'` with a cast in `delivery()`, and `isRunState(row.state) ? row.state : 'unknown'` with a cast for `advance()`'s own `from` — all three left the full suite passing unchanged before this fix. Only `hydrateRun`'s copy (`run()`/`runs()`) was pinned, by the one existing "reads a state token this build does not know" test; Task 11's mutation table scheduled exactly one store guard mutant (`run(): state as RunState`) and so never scheduled the other three either. `mail-routes.test.ts`'s own `delivery(...)?.state` assertion reads a genuinely-`'queued'` row, which a cast returns identically — not discriminating, and confirmed unaffected by the `delivery()` mutant above.

`advance()`'s case is not merely a silent-render bug like the other three: a run whose `state` a newer build wrote makes `RUN_TRANSITIONS[from]` `undefined`, so `.includes(to)` throws a `TypeError` (confirmed by mutation) — a rollback per spec's own downward-compatibility rule (spec:80-83, "never treats a higher `user_version` as fatal downward") would 500 a future `POST /api/runs/:id/advance` where the contract is a typed `bad-transition` refusal.

**Adaptation:** three cases added to `coord-store.test.ts`, each confirmed by mutation to flip from pass to fail against the guard it pins: `programs()` and `delivery()` write an unrecognised raw token directly (bypassing the store, the same way the existing `run()` case already does) and assert `'unknown'`, never the raw string; `advance()`'s case does the same on `runs.state` and asserts a typed `{ok:false, error:'bad-transition', from:'unknown', ...}` rather than a thrown exception. `schema.ts`'s and D-8's own "SHUT" language is left as-is — the guards themselves were never wrong, only untested — but Task 11's mutation table gains the three rows below so a future mutation sweep credits only what the suite actually discriminates.

### D-28 (test-coverage, found in a later Task 4 review) — `readPrHistory`'s accept-direction assertions checked only `pr`, so an entry-mapping bug at the push site had no test able to catch it

Every accept-direction case in `coord-prhistory.test.ts` asserted `expect.objectContaining({ pr })`, never the full `PrLineageEntry` — three of the four fields the reader exists to carry (`branch`, `phase`, `recordedAt`) went unchecked everywhere in the file. Measured: replacing `prhistory.ts`'s `entries.push` with `entries.push({ pr: o['pr'], branch: '', phase: '', recordedAt: 0 })` left the pre-fix suite at 8/8 green — a clean survivor. Not cosmetic: Task 9's close route folds these entries into `runs.prLineage` via `CoordStore.foldPrLineage` (`store.ts:149-151`), and the disaster-recovery path in `reconstruct` filters `input.prHistory` by `e.branch === input.registry.branch` (`store.ts:550`) — a branch-mapping bug of this shape makes `reconstruct` fold ZERO lineage onto every wave of every rebuilt program, the ledger+registry+`.prhistory` rebuild `spec:82-85` calls ground truth silently answering `prLineage: []`, with the suite green throughout.

**Adaptation:** the `reads JSONL in order, dropping nothing it can parse` case now asserts full-object equality — `{ pr, branch, phase, recordedAt }` — against both entries instead of `objectContaining({ pr })`. Confirmed by mutation: the constant-mapping mutant above now fails this case.

### D-29 (test-coverage, found in a later Task 4 review) — D-16's wrong-shape fixture bundled three fields wrong into one record, so it pinned only the FIRST shape guard and left the other four unreachable in the reject direction

D-16 (above) credited a "two-line ledger of valid-JSON-wrong-shape records… pinning the reject side of the shape guards" (plural), and the Task 11 table carried it as one bundled row. Both of that fixture's lines (`{"pr":"577","branch":null,"phase":7}` and `{}`) die on the very first guard, `typeof o['pr'] !== 'number'` — `branch`/`phase` being wrong on the SAME line as `pr` never gets evaluated, because `continue` fires before the later guard lines run at all. Measured on isolated copies, one mutant at a time, restored between (baseline 8/8 green): dropping `!Number.isInteger(o['pr'])` alone, dropping `typeof o['branch'] !== 'string' || typeof o['phase'] !== 'string'` alone, and dropping `typeof o['recordedAt'] !== 'number'` alone each left the file's assertions byte-identical; only deleting all four guard lines together — the only mutant the table's row actually described — killed it. With `recordedAt`'s guard undetectable, a ledger line `{"pr":1,"branch":"ws/x","phase":"merged","recordedAt":"1786000000"}` would fold a string into a column `PrLineageEntry` types `number`, and `hydrateRun` (`store.ts:240`) hands it back as a number to every consumer.

**Adaptation:** the bundled case is replaced with five, each wrong in exactly one field and every other field well-typed: `pr` the wrong type, `pr` a non-integer number, `branch` the wrong type, `phase` the wrong type, `recordedAt` the wrong type — plus the original bare `{}` kept as a bonus robustness case. All five confirmed by mutation: each construct's own guard-line deletion now fails exactly the case built for it.

### D-30 (correction, found in a later Task 4 review) — the MALFORMED rung's docstring (and D-15) credited `ccd-prhistory.test.ts:105` with pinning ccd's whole-file degrade; that fixture cannot discriminate it from this reader's own per-line policy

D-15 (above) and `prhistory.ts`'s own docstring both cite `ccd-prhistory.test.ts:105` as pinning ccd's whole-file MALFORMED behaviour — "one bad line discards every row, good ones included." That fixture is a ONE-line ledger (`printf 'not json\n'`), and every other malformed fixture in that file is single-line too. For a single bad line, whole-file discard and this reader's per-line salvage answer the identical `[]` — nothing in the repo used a multi-line ledger with one bad line among good ones, so no test anywhere discriminated ccd's policy from this module's deliberate divergence from it, despite the docstring reading as though one did. The substance the docstring argues from is sound (`ccd/ccd:2046`, `:2060-2061` really are whole-file); only the citation was false.

**Adaptation:** a new `ccd-prhistory.test.ts` case (`a corrupt line degrades the WHOLE ledger — a good row ahead of it is lost too`, `:126`) writes a good `{"pr":577,...}` row ahead of a `not json` line and asserts the archived manifest's `prHistory` is `[]` — the good row lost too, which per-line salvage would not do. `prhistory.ts`'s docstring now cites this line instead of the single-line fixture, and notes that a single-line ledger proves nothing about which policy ran.

### D-31 (bug, found in a later Task 5 review) — D-19 closed the readable-non-SHA fallthrough but left the OTHER half open: a PRESENT loose ref that cannot be READ still fell through to a stale `packed-refs` entry, and `verifyDone` settled the stale claim `ok: true`

D-19's own problem statement named three triggers for the stale-packed-tip escape — "a torn write, an `EISDIR`, or a `git symbolic-ref`" — and its adaptation closed exactly one of them (a readable loose file whose content is not a SHA). The `readFile === null` arm was untouched: `FleetIO.readFile` collapses ENOENT (genuinely absent), EACCES, EISDIR, and any remote-mode transport failure to that same `null` (`io.ts:41-43`), so "loose ref absent" and "loose ref present, git's authoritative answer, unreadable right now" still resolved identically — both fell through to `packed-refs`, which may hold the branch's PREVIOUS tip after any `git gc --auto`/`pack-refs` in the main checkout (workspace branches live in the main repo's ref store — this file's own docstring). Measured end to end against the shipped modules before this fix (fixture dirs, `chmod 000` on the loose file and, separately, the loose ref path replaced with a directory for the `EISDIR` trigger; `packed-refs` at an OLD tip): `readBranchTip` returned the OLD tip from `packed-refs` in both cases instead of `null`, and a `verifyDone` claim naming that OLD tip settled `{"ok":true,"measured":{"branchTip":OLD,...}}` while the run's own git ref had already moved to a NEW tip — the exact inversion of spec:127-132, "a stale `worker_done` can never settle a task." The gap was disclosed honestly in `gitref.ts`'s own docstring ("a real, accepted gap in `FleetIO`'s contract... do not read the packed-refs fallback below as proof this case was handled") but nothing above it agreed: D-19 read as closing the class it opened, and the Task 11 mutation row credited "a symref loose ref must not read the stale packed entry" for the whole construct when it only ever exercised the readable-non-SHA half.

**Adaptation:** the house idiom this same PR already applies to the sibling reader (`registry.ts`'s `HOLD_UNREADABLE`, confirming an ambiguous null read with a second, independent read of presence) is applied here too — `io.stat` on the IDENTICAL loose-ref path, after `readFile` returns `null`. `stat` needs only search permission on the parent directory chain, not read permission on the leaf, so it succeeds on a `chmod 000` file and on a directory (`EISDIR`) alike, without ever needing the bytes `readFile` couldn't get. Presence proven -> refuse (`null`, the typed `tip-unmeasurable` refusal `verifyDone` already retries on the next sweep); presence not proven (the ordinary "packed and never re-committed" case) -> `packed-refs` remains git's honest fallback, unchanged. `gitref.ts`'s docstring is rewritten to state the closed contract rather than disclosing an accepted gap that no longer exists. Pinned by four new `coord-fingerprint.test.ts` cases: a `chmod 000` loose ref with a stale packed entry behind it, a directory-shaped loose ref path with a stale packed entry behind it (both at the `readBranchTip` level), and an end-to-end `verifyDone` pair — a STALE claim against an unreadable-but-present loose ref now refuses `tip-unmeasurable` rather than settling `ok:true`, and the HONEST claim against the same fixture is refused too (unreadable means unmeasurable, never "trust whichever claim looks right"). All four fail against the pre-fix code (measured: reverting to the pre-fix `readBranchTip` body reproduces the exact stale-accept the finding described) and pass against the fix.

### D-32 (correction, found in a later Task 5 review) — `verifyDone`'s registry-fallback comment claimed exactly one trigger for `run.branch`, "and only that one"; three other reachable states used the identical fallback for unrelated, often-transient reasons

`verifyDone`'s comment on `record?.branch ?? run.branch` said: "`record` is undefined for a run whose session the registry no longer carries at all (retired, purged); in that one case, and only that one, `run.branch` is the best answer left." `DoneRun`'s own docstring repeated the claim ("falls back to this field only when that registry row is gone entirely"). Measured against the shipped `verifyDone`, five fixture states: (A) a healthy row at the renamed branch resolves correctly, not through the fallback at all; (E) a healthy, readable registry with genuinely no row for this session — the ONE state the comment named, and the only one that is actually "this session retired"; (B) the registry directory itself unreadable (`io.readdir` -> `null`, a single failed agent-WS round trip in remote mode) makes `readRegistry` return `[]` for every session, not just retired ones — `record` is undefined here too, indistinguishable from (E) on the wire, but TRANSIENT rather than terminal; (C) a row present in the directory listing but missing `wrapper`/`workdir`/`uuid` is dropped whole by `readRegistry`'s own `if (!wrapper || !workdir || !uuid) continue` (`registry.ts:123`) — `record` undefined again, also transient; (D) a row that IS found, but whose own `.branch` field reads `null` (absent, or unreadable the same way `field()` collapses any read failure — `registry.ts:77-80`) — reaches the SAME `run.branch` fallback WITHOUT `record` ever being undefined at all, since it is the `??` on `record.branch` that falls back, not the `record === undefined` branch the comment described. Behaviour was already fail-shut throughout every one of B/C/D (a stale `run.branch` still degrades to a typed `tip-unmeasurable` refusal rather than a false accept, per D-31 above), so this is a false comment rather than a settle-on-wrong-facts bug — but a load-bearing one: an operator reading a `tip-unmeasurable` refusal citing a branch git deleted hours ago, on a transient registry read hiccup, is told by the comment that this can only mean the session retired.

**Adaptation:** the comment at the `readRegistry`/`branch` resolution site in `verifyDone`, and `DoneRun`'s own docstring, are rewritten to name all four reachable fallback triggers (B, C, D, E) and say which are transient (B, C — a retry on the next `worker_done` sweep resolves them, spec:174-177/D-10) versus the one terminal state (E), and to call out D's different mechanism (the `??`, not `record === undefined`) explicitly. No behavioural change: the fallback was already safe, only mis-described. Pinned by three new `coord-fingerprint.test.ts` cases, one each for B, C and D — a `chmod 000` registry directory, a row with no `.uuid` (dropped by `readRegistry` entirely), and a row found but with no `.branch` file of its own — each asserting the SAME `ok: true` a healthy fallback already produces (E already had its own case before this fix), which is the point: these are documentation regression guards (proving each trigger is reached at all under the described mechanism), not behavioural discriminators, since nothing about the fallback's behavior changed.

### D-33 (bug, found in a later Task 6 review) — `readMailToken` failed open on an unreadable-but-present token file; fixed in `c845d46` but never recorded as a deviation, so Task 6's own Step 3 recipe and Task 11's mutation table still describe the fail-open version

Task 6 Step 3's own recipe (this plan, verbatim, unedited below per this repo's "an anchor here is a snapshot at plan-writing time" rule) reads:

```ts
export function readMailToken(tokenPath: string): string | null {
  try {
    const v = readFileSync(tokenPath, 'utf8').trim();
    return v === '' ? null : v;
  } catch {
    return null;   // absent is a configuration state, not an error
  }
}
```

That `catch` collapses EVERY read failure — `ENOENT` (genuinely absent, the one state spec:150-155 actually grants a fail-open) alongside `EACCES`, `EISDIR`, `ELOOP`, `EIO` (the token is PRESENT and this box cannot prove it) — into the identical `null`. `checkMailToken(null, …)` returns `'ok'` for every presented value, so a chmod mistake, a bad `chown` after a box rebuild, or a unit that gains a `User=` drop-in disarms the whole gate with nothing red anywhere. `coord-token.test.ts` at the time asserted only the `ENOENT` and the happy-path cases — no fixture ever made `readFileSync` throw anything else, so the collapse was a real, unpinned gap, not a theoretical one. Fixed in `c845d46` (already shipped on this branch before this fix round started): only `code === 'ENOENT'` returns `null`; every other error rethrows, uncaught, the same stance `coord/db.ts`'s `CoordDbUnmigratable` takes for a database that cannot migrate — `index.ts` fails to boot rather than opening the tailnet ingress silently. Pinned by a `chmod 000` case in `coord-token.test.ts` (skipped when the suite runs as root, the same guard `coord-prhistory.test.ts` already uses). Recorded here because the fix, real and already shipped, left no trace in this plan: the Step 3 code block above still reads as the recipe, and nothing named the divergence.

### D-34 (bug, found in a later Task 6 fix pass) — the server's `.trim()` and `notify.sh`'s `tr -d '[:space:]'` normalised the SAME committed token file with different SCOPE, and the documented setup path was the content most likely to expose it

Task 6 Step 3 and Step 6's recipes read the one committed file two different ways: the server `.trim()`s (leading/trailing whitespace only), `notify.sh` ran `tr -d '\r\n'` originally and — per `c845d46` — `tr -d '[:space:]'` (everywhere) after that fix. The character CLASS matched after `c845d46`; the SCOPE never did. Any file content with INTERIOR whitespace produces two different secrets from the one file `deploy.sh`'s `ship_secret` sends unchanged to both boxes. `deploy/ccrc-mail.token.example` — a multi-line `#`-comment block, interior newlines and spaces throughout — is exactly that content, and its own first line taught the `cp *.example ccrc-mail.token && edit` workflow this repo already uses for `ccrc.env.example` (where it is harmless, because dotenv parses comments away; here the file IS the secret, whole). Measured against the shipped `.example` plus one appended `openssl rand -hex 32` line: the server's `.trim()` read 621 characters starting with the comment block's own text; `notify.sh`'s `tr -d '[:space:]'` produced a shorter, differently-shaped string with the comment text and the real value run together. `checkMailToken` calls the resulting length mismatch `'bad'` — every notify and mail request 401s, fleet-wide, with nothing surfacing beyond one journal WARN line neither box's `>/dev/null 2>&1 || true` wrapping lets an operator see easily. `coord-token.test.ts:76-85`'s pre-existing assertions only checked that `notify.sh` CONTAINS the `tr` string — nothing ran both normalisers over the same bytes and compared.

**Adaptation:** both sides now share ONE rule — the first line that is neither blank nor a `#`-comment, whitespace stripped from THAT line everywhere, not just its edges (`coord/token.ts`'s new `extractToken`, factored out of `readMailToken`; `notify.sh`'s `grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#' | grep -v '^[[:space:]]*$' | head -n1 | tr -d '[:space:]'`). Skipping `#`-comment lines is what lets `ccrc-mail.token.example` carry its instructional preamble and still resolve to exactly one value on both boxes. `ccrc-mail.token.example` gains a placeholder value line (`REPLACE-THIS-LINE-WITH-THE-OUTPUT-OF-openssl-rand--hex-32`) below its comments, with an explicit warning not to ship it as-is, so the documented copy-and-edit workflow has a real line to edit rather than nothing. Pinned by a new fixture-driven equivalence suite in `coord-token.test.ts`: the shell extraction snippet is sliced verbatim out of the shipped `notify.sh` (never a hand-copied duplicate, and bounded strictly before the `curl` line so the test never dials the real fleet host) and run over seven fixtures — bare hex, hex + trailing newline, hex + trailing space, CRLF, a value line with an embedded space (the one that discriminates edges-only from everywhere), and the shipped `.example` both unedited and with its placeholder replaced — asserting it agrees with `readMailToken` on every one. All seven agree post-fix; measured pre-fix (the `.example`-plus-value fixture, and separately the embedded-space fixture against an edges-only `extractToken`), the two extractions differed exactly as described above.

### D-35 (bug, found in the same later Task 6 fix pass) — a present-but-EMPTY token file collapsed into the same fail-open `null` D-33's own fix had just refused to grant an unreadable one

`extractToken` (D-34) can return `null` for a file that DID open — 0 bytes, whitespace only, or every line a `#`-comment with no value line at all (a deleted value line, a truncated `openssl rand -hex 32 > deploy/ccrc-mail.token` redirect after a full disk or an interrupted rotation). Before this fix, `readMailToken` answered `null` for that case exactly as it does for `ENOENT` — the same collapse D-33 had just named and fixed for the unreadable-file arm, reopened one line later for the empty-file arm. `checkMailToken(null, …)` again returns `'ok'` for everything: `ship_secret`'s only guard is `[ -f "$local_file" ]`, so a 0-byte file ships to both boxes at the correct 0600/0700 permissions, looking correctly deployed, while every route is unauthenticated with nothing red anywhere — `index.ts`'s boot warning ("no box token at …") reads as "you never shipped one," not "the one you shipped is empty." `coord-token.test.ts:34` (pre-fix) asserted this fail-open as CORRECT behaviour, pinning the gap rather than catching it. Compare Task 2/D-24, where a 0-byte `coord.db` is explicitly a refusal, not a default — the same principle this fix now applies here.

**Adaptation:** `readMailToken` throws a new `MailTokenFileUnusable` when `extractToken` finds no usable line, joining the non-`ENOENT` `catch` arm's fail-shut stance (D-33) rather than the `ENOENT` arm's fail-open one — "never configured" and "configured with nothing usable" are different mistakes and must not read the same. Deliberately uncaught, the same `index.ts`/`CoordDbUnmigratable` precedent D-33 already established. The pre-existing `coord-token.test.ts:34` case (which asserted the fail-open) is corrected to assert the throw instead, and two more fixtures (whitespace-only, comment-only-no-value-line) join it.

### D-36 (bug, found in the same later Task 6 fix pass; lives in Task 7's `routes.ts`, justified by Task 6's `checkMailToken` docstring) — `/api/mail` and `/api/mail/:id/ack` inherited `/api/notify`'s rollout-only `legacy` tolerance, unpinned in both directions

`checkMailToken`'s tri-state return (`'ok' | 'legacy' | 'bad'`) exists so `/api/notify` can grant its one-deploy-generation rollout tolerance (spec:150-155) to an absent token without conflating it with a WRONG one. Task 7's `routes.ts` (landed in `a19cec2`) gated both mail routes with `if (verdict === 'bad')`, which — like `/api/notify` — let `'legacy'` proceed. The route's own comment stated the equivalence outright ("exactly as `/api/notify` already treats them") without noticing the justification does not transfer: `/api/notify` is an existing, already-deployed hook that must not go dark mid-rollout; `/api/mail` is created in THIS build, has no pre-existing caller, and the spec lists `unauthenticated` as one of its own typed, TOTAL rejection codes (spec:136-148) with no tolerance carved out. During the tolerance window (and permanently, if a token is never minted — see D-35), any host that can reach the server's port could POST `/api/mail` with no auth header and reach every gate from 2 through 8, including a full `readRegistry` round trip per request in remote mode. Unlike `/api/notify` (which logs the `legacy` case via `console.warn` because it has no coordination store to write into), the mail routes had a `mail_rejections` table available and used it for `'bad'` but not for the tokenless case, so the spec's own "logged as legacy" requirement (spec:150-155) was unmet on the one path that could actually persist it. `mail-routes.test.ts`'s `send`/`ack` helpers already supported a `null` token (no header) but no case in the file ever called them that way — the accept-direction and reject-direction gap the finding described was real and measured, not theoretical.

**Adaptation:** both routes now refuse `verdict !== 'ok'` — `'legacy'` and `'bad'` both land in `mail_rejections` via the existing `refuse()` helper, with a `detail` string naming which arm fired. `checkMailToken`'s docstring is rewritten to state the scoping explicitly (`'legacy'` is a state, not by itself a license to proceed; exactly one caller is granted tolerance for it) so a future route sharing this function does not repeat the mistake. Pinned by two new tests: a tokenless `POST /api/mail` and a tokenless `POST /api/mail/:id/ack`, both asserting 401 `unauthenticated` and a recorded rejection.

### D-37 (bug, found in a later Task 7 review) — a transient registry-read failure at the mail ingress read as a permanent, recorded `unknown-sender`/`unknown-recipient`, dropping the message rather than deferring it

`readRegistry` collapses `io.readdir` returning `null` (an ordinary transient failure in remote mode — one dropped agent-WS round trip) to `[]` (`registry.ts:104`), and separately drops any individually-listed row whose `wrapper`/`workdir`/`uuid` field read fails (`registry.ts:123`). `routes.ts`'s checks 5/6/7 read either state as fact: `unknown-sender`, `stale-uuid` (via a spurious empty-`uuid` row that could never actually occur, since a dropped row never reaches `sender` at all — the real effect is `unknown-sender`), and `unknown-recipient`, each recorded PERMANENTLY via `refuse()`. Unlike the done-authority checks, an ingress refusal never creates a `mail` row, so there is nothing to replay — a worker's `wave-done` status mail lost this way is gone, and the coordinator never learns the wave finished. This is the same NOT-KNOWING-IS-NOT-`[]` rule D-31/D-32 already spent two rounds establishing on the read side (`verifyDone`'s registry fallback), and `MAIL_REJECT_CODES`'s own docstring already states it for `tip-unmeasurable`/`pr-unmeasurable` — the ingress simply never applied it to itself.

**Adaptation:** a new INGRESS code, `registry-unmeasurable` (502), added to `MAIL_REJECT_CODES`. `routes.ts` reads `io.readdir(cfg.registryDir)` directly (the same fail-shut-on-unlistable-registry idiom Task 8's `sweepMail` and Task 9's dispatch-pause check both use for the same directory) and answers `registry-unmeasurable` — never `unknown-sender`/`unknown-recipient` — when the listing itself fails, or when a `fromId`/`toId` IS listed (its `.uuid` file present) but `readRegistry` still could not produce a row for it. Applied to checks 5, 6 (transitively, since a row that failed check 5 never reaches 6) and 7 on the ingress route, and to the ack route's own sender check. Pinned by `mail-routes.test.ts`'s new `'a transient registry-read failure is NOT a fact about the sender or recipient'` block (three ingress cases) plus one ack-route case.

### D-38 (correction, found in the same later Task 7 review) — `MAIL_REJECT_CODES`'s docstring claimed a both-directions pin the shipped test does not perform, and the narrowing carried no D-number

The docstring read "pinned in BOTH directions by `mail-routes.test.ts` (every code here is emitted somewhere in `server/src/coord/`, and every code emitted there is here)" — but `mail-routes.test.ts`'s own forward-direction scan does `if (code === 'undeliverable') continue;`, because `undeliverable` is emitted by `watch.ts` (Task 8), outside `server/src/coord` entirely. The exclusion is permanent, not temporary (Task 8 lands `undeliverable`'s only emit site outside the scanned directory), so the docstring's parenthetical is false as shipped and was never recorded anywhere but a code comment and a commit message — this build's own standard (D-15/D-30/D-32, and D-33's own reason for existing) says that is not enough.

**Adaptation:** the docstring now states the exception by name, with this D-number, rather than claiming an unqualified total pin. No test or runtime behaviour changes — this is a documentation-and-crediting fix, so Task 11's mutation table does not credit `undeliverable`'s linkage to a test that excludes it by name.

### D-39 (bug, found in the same later Task 7 review) — `/api/mail` and `/api/mail/:id/ack` accepted anonymous POSTs whenever the server box had no token file at all — the unfixed other half of D-36's own hole

`checkMailToken(expected, presented)` returned `'ok'` unconditionally when `expected === null` — a server whose token file was never minted (reachable by omission: `deploy/deploy.sh`'s `ship_secret` only guards `[ -f "$local_file" ]`, and `deploy/ccrc-mail.token` is gitignored) ran both mail routes fully unauthenticated, past the `verdict !== 'ok'` gate, with a single `console.warn` at boot and no `mail_rejections` row — unlike the `'legacy'` and `'bad'` arms D-36 just closed. `token.ts`'s own `readMailToken` docstring mis-sourced the justification, citing spec:150-155 (a rollout tolerance for an ABSENT TOKEN IN A CALLER'S REQUEST, scoped to `/api/notify` by name) as if it also covered a server that was never configured at all — it does not, and spec:136-138 states the mail ingress is "Authenticated by a box token" with no carve-out. The "no pre-existing deployed caller" argument that already ruled out a `'legacy'` tolerance for these two routes applies identically here.

**Adaptation:** `checkMailToken` gains a fourth verdict, `'unconfigured'`, split out of what used to be `'ok'`. `/api/mail`/`/api/mail/:id/ack` read anything but `'ok'` as a refusal, so they now fail shut on it automatically, recorded via the existing `refuse()` path with an honest detail string. `/api/notify` is DELIBERATELY unaffected — it still has a pre-existing deployed caller (`notify.sh`) that must not go dark before a token is ever minted, so its `if (verdict === 'bad' | === 'legacy')` handling (unchanged) simply falls through `'unconfigured'` the same way it used to fall through `'ok'`; `notify-token.test.ts`'s `'accepts everything, unauthenticated, when no token is configured'` case stays green, unedited, as the pin that this route's behaviour did not move. `token.ts`'s docstrings (`readMailToken`'s ENOENT arm and `checkMailToken`'s own) are rewritten to stop mis-sourcing the spec citation. Pinned directly (`coord-token.test.ts`'s new `checkMailToken` block) and through both routes (`mail-routes.test.ts`'s new unconfigured-token cases, ingress and ack).

### D-40 (test-coverage gap, found in the same later Task 7 review) — `renderEnvelope`/`fenceFor` and `resolveCoordinator`, plus the route's whole `'coordinator'`-role arm, were reachable by zero tests in the tree

Measured against the full suite before this fix round: no test imported `renderEnvelope`/`envelope.ts`, and no test called `CoordStore.resolveCoordinator`. Every `mail-routes.test.ts` case that carried `GOOD.toId === 'coordinator'` either refused at a cheaper check (checks 1–6, 8) or was overridden to a literal session id before reaching the role-resolution line (`routes.ts:185`) — so `resolvedToId = toId === 'coordinator' ? coord.resolveCoordinator(runId) : toId` and its `null`-guard refusal were both dead code by test coverage, and `renderEnvelope`'s output, while called on every accept path, was asserted nowhere.

**Adaptation:** this is the umbrella finding D-41/D-42/D-43 below each close their own slice of — recorded with its own number because it is itself a distinct, named observation in the fix round (this build's convention per D-16/D-21/D-22/D-27/D-29: a coverage gap the mutation table had credited without earning gets recorded even when its remedy rides on other deviations' work).

### D-41 (bug, found in the same later Task 7 review) — the envelope published the MAIL id as the ack id; the ack route resolves a DELIVERY id — two independent `AUTOINCREMENT` sequences, aligned only by coincidence

`routes.ts` rendered the envelope with `id: inserted.id` (`mail.id`) and discarded the id `queueDelivery` returned. `envelope.ts` printed that mail id as the `ack:` line's target — the only ack instruction the receiving agent ever sees — but the ack route resolves its `:id` param against `mail_deliveries`, a SEPARATE `AUTOINCREMENT` sequence (`schema.ts`). The two only walked together because the route inserted exactly one delivery per mail; the plan's own Task 7 Step 4 specifies one delivery row PER RESOLVED RECIPIENT, so the first fan-out (or any mail with zero deliveries, or any delivery retention/prune) would desynchronise the counters permanently, and every envelope from then on would teach its recipient a wrong id. `mail-routes.test.ts`'s three original ack tests all took `deliveryId` from `dueDeliveries()[0].id`, never from the envelope or the 202 body — structurally incapable of noticing the two diverge.

**Adaptation:** the route now inserts the delivery row FIRST (with a placeholder empty envelope), renders the envelope against the delivery's own id, then calls a new `CoordStore.setDeliveryEnvelope(id, envelope)` to land it — all inside the same transaction, so no reader ever observes the placeholder. `renderEnvelope` still runs exactly once, at queue time (spec:176-177); `setDeliveryEnvelope` is the second half of that one insert, not a second render. Pinned by a dedicated test that inserts one filler `mail` row directly (desynchronising the two sequences on purpose) before sending the real message, so the mail id and delivery id are provably different numbers and the envelope's `ack:` id is asserted against the delivery id specifically — and by rewriting the three existing ack tests to read their id from the published envelope (`ackIdFromEnvelope`) rather than from `dueDeliveries()[0].id` directly.

### D-42 (test-coverage gap, found in the same later Task 7 review) — the `'coordinator'`-role resolution, the plan's headline "No guessing" decision, was unpinned in both directions
