`routes.ts:185`'s role-resolution line and its `null`-guard refusal had no test reaching either branch (see D-40). Replacing the line with `const resolvedToId = toId;`, or deleting the refusal block entirely, left the full suite green. `CoordStore.resolveCoordinator`'s `runId !== null` arm ("the run's own claim") had zero coverage anywhere in the tree — every existing call passed `null`.

**Adaptation:** no code changed (the implementation was already correct). `mail-routes.test.ts` gained a `'resolving the coordinator role'` block: an accept case (a run claimed by a real session, addressed via `toId: 'coordinator'` + that `runId`, asserting the QUEUED DELIVERY names the real session, never the literal string) and two refusal cases (no active program; two active programs, ambiguous). `coord-store.test.ts` gained a direct `resolveCoordinator(runId)` case against two differently-claimed runs. Verified to actually kill the two named mutants (`resolvedToId = toId`; deleting the refusal block) by temporary local mutation before this deviation was recorded.

### D-43 (test-coverage gap, found in the same later Task 7 review) — `renderEnvelope`/`fenceFor` had zero assertions anywhere in the tree, including the fence-escape arithmetic

No test read `mail_deliveries.envelope`'s contents; `coord-store.test.ts`'s three `queueDelivery` calls passed a literal placeholder string, and `mail-routes.test.ts`'s happy path asserted only the HTTP shape. The entire 67-line module — every header line, the `run:`/`artifacts:` conditionals, the ack instruction, and `fenceFor`'s escape arithmetic — was unverified output; a body containing its own fenced code block (the likely shape for `kind: 'finding'`/`'artifact'` mail) closing the envelope early was not caught by anything.

**Adaptation:** a new `server/test/coord-envelope.test.ts` pins `renderEnvelope` and `fenceFor` directly — header fields, the `run:`/`artifacts:` conditionals in every combination, the three ack-instruction lines (mutation-tested: deleting them fails the suite), and the fence-escape arithmetic across the body, the header, and a longer-than-five backtick run, each mutation-tested against the real `fenceFor` mutant (`return '```'`). `mail-routes.test.ts`'s happy-path test also gained assertions against the stored envelope's real content, closing the route-integration half of the gap.

### D-44 (bug, found in the same later Task 7 review) — the envelope's own COST docstring named a bound (`MAIL_BODY_MAX_BYTES`) that `subject` and `artifacts` were never actually held to

`envelope.ts`'s COST paragraph states "An 8KB body of short lines is a few hundred round trips" as the worst case `sendPrompt`'s per-line injection cost reaches, framed as the reason not to shorten the body. `routes.ts` applied `MAIL_BODY_MAX_BYTES` to `body` only — `subject` was checked for `typeof === 'string'` alone and `artifacts` for `Array.isArray` + per-entry absolute-path shape alone, with no count or length bound on either, and both render into the envelope (`subject` as one line, `artifacts` as one line PER ENTRY). A message with an empty body and tens of thousands of artifact entries stays well under the body cap and Fastify's default 1 MiB body limit (`server.ts` builds `Fastify({ logger: false })` with no `bodyLimit` override) while blowing the stated "a few hundred round trips" worst case by two orders of magnitude, holding the recipient's injection lane the whole time.

**Adaptation:** three new constants in `shared/api.ts` — `MAIL_SUBJECT_MAX_BYTES` (200), `MAIL_ARTIFACTS_MAX` (64), `MAIL_ARTIFACT_PATH_MAX_BYTES` (4096) — enforced at ingress check 4, alongside the existing body cap, all under the `oversize` code. The envelope's COST paragraph is amended to name these caps explicitly rather than implying the body cap alone bounds the cost. Pinned by three new `mail-routes.test.ts` cases (subject length, artifact count, single artifact path length).

### D-45 (test-coverage/documentation gap, found in a later Task 9 review) — Task 9 shipped three `CoordStore` writers and a test-suite allowlist edit outside its own File Structure entry, and cited a deviation number that did not exist

Task 9's own File Structure entry (this plan, above) names exactly two files: `Modify: server/src/coord/routes.ts`, `Create: server/test/run-routes.test.ts`. Its Step 5 `git add` recipe names the same two. The commit that actually shipped Task 9 (`a4d5207`) additionally touched `server/src/coord/store.ts` — adding `CoordStore.setSession` (an OPEN-time writer of `runs.sessionId`, distinct from `markDispatched`'s dispatch-time write of the same column: the open route places a wave N≥2 reclaim's hold immediately, spec:120-123, and the dispatch route then needs `run.sessionId` back off the row to choose `ws-add` vs. `ensure`, D-1), `CoordStore.setClearedAt` (D-1's own amendment, explicitly deferred at the time to "the route that drives it" — this is that route), and a widened `markDispatched(runId, sessionId, workspace, branch, resumed, at?)` signature — and `server/test/mail-routes.test.ts`, whose own totality-scanner test enumerates every `CoordStore` method against an explicit allowlist and needed the three new ones added to it or the scanner itself would have failed the build. Both `routes.ts:486` and `store.ts:135-136` already shipped comments citing "the plan's D-45" for exactly this divergence — the deviation was anticipated and named at write time, but the entry itself was never added here: this section's own count sentence still read "Forty-four" and stopped at D-44, so a reviewer (or Task 11's own mutation table and operator record) following either citation found nothing.

**Adaptation:** none needed to the code — `setSession`/`setClearedAt`/the widened `markDispatched` signature are the correct, minimal writers Task 9's own dispatch route needs (see each method's own docstring in `store.ts`), and the `mail-routes.test.ts` allowlist addition is the totality scanner doing exactly its job. This entry records what shipped, closing the citation both source comments already made. No test changes: `coord-store.test.ts` and `run-routes.test.ts` already exercise all three writers (the open-with-sessionId case, the wave-2 `/clear`-succeeds case, and every `markDispatched` call site in the suite).

### D-46 (bug, found in the same later Task 9 review) — dispatch performed every irreversible fleet act BEFORE the one check that would have refused a second dispatch, so a retried `POST .../dispatch` destroyed a live worker's context and only then answered 409

`RUN_TRANSITIONS.dispatched` is `['working','closing','failed']` — no self-edge, so `dispatched -> dispatched` is illegal — but the only place that fact was ever checked was `advance()`, called at the very END of the dispatch route, after `ccd ensure`/`ccd ws-add`, a possible injected `/clear`, and `ccd ws-hold` had already run. An HTTP client that times out and retries, a PWA double-tap, or a coordinator retrying after a transient 502 from a later step all re-`POST` the same dispatch: pause passes (already dispatched, not paused), caps pass (the run already counts, but the caps check nothing about THIS run's own state). **Measured, not assumed to be a wave-N≥2-only hazard:** even a WAVE-1 run's own retry takes the DANGEROUS branch, because the FIRST dispatch's own `markDispatched` already wrote `sessionId` onto the row — so the SECOND call reads `run.sessionId !== null` and runs `ccd ensure` plus an injected `/clear` into the worker the first call just spawned, mid-turn, before `advance()` finally discovers the transition was always illegal and answers `409 {error:'bad-transition',from:'dispatched',to:'dispatched'}`. Measured directly (mutant: drop the precondition, rerun the same-run-retry test): the call log grows from 2 entries (`ws-add`, `ws-hold`) to 9 — `ws-add`, `ws-hold`, `ensure`, then the full `/clear` injection (`capture-pane`, `send-keys …/clear`, `capture-pane`, `send-keys …Enter`, `capture-pane`), then a second `ws-hold` — before the trailing `advance()` catches it. The caller sees a refusal; the worker has already lost its transcript, on EITHER wave. No test in the pre-fix `run-routes.test.ts` dispatched the same run twice.

**Adaptation:** a precondition, read-only, added immediately after the 404 check and before anything else runs: `if (run.state !== 'planned') return 409 {error:'bad-transition', from: run.state, to: 'dispatched'}`. `advance()` at the end of the route is unchanged and still the only WRITER of `state` — this only answers the legality question early enough that pause/caps/`ccd ensure`/`/clear`/`ws-add`/`ws-hold` never run for a transition that was always going to be refused. An `ambiguous-dispatch` refusal still leaves the run `planned` (unchanged), so a legitimate retry after that specific refusal is still accepted. Pinned by two new `run-routes.test.ts` cases: a same-run double dispatch (asserting zero additional fleet calls and exactly one `run_events` row), and a wave-2 double dispatch specifically (asserting `send-keys` — the `/clear` injection — never fires a second time). Measured: reverting the precondition to `if (false)` fails both new tests; the full pre-existing suite (34 tests) stays green throughout, confirming nothing but the double-dispatch path depended on the old ordering.

### D-47 (bug, found in the same later Task 9 review) — a refused `/clear` still landed dispatch as an unqualified 200 with the refusal discarded, and the wave brief was still queued into the un-cleared context

D-1's own orchestrator-amended text promises two things for a refused `/clear`: the run "leaves the run in `dispatched` with the refusal recorded", and the wave brief is delivered "into a genuinely fresh context." The shipped route kept the first half only in spirit — `clearedAt` stayed honestly `null` — but discarded `clearRes.error` entirely (no `run_events.detail`, despite `advance(runId, to, causedBy, detail?)` already taking one; nothing in the response body but a null column), and queued the wave-brief mail unconditionally regardless of whether `/clear` had verified. On `send.ts`'s `enter-ignored` path (Bug #21's own exact shape, named in the Global Constraints as living on this path) the literal text `/clear` is left sitting, unset, in the worker's own input box. The delivery lane's very next sweep calls `sendPrompt` with no `replaceDraft` for any mail delivery, so it immediately hits `draft-present` and keeps hitting it — parking the wave brief `rejected('undeliverable')` after `MAIL_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, with nothing anywhere surfacing why. A run recorded `dispatched`, a worker resumed into a stale context with an unsent `/clear` sitting in its box, a brief that will never arrive, and a `200 OK` as the only thing the coordinator ever saw.

**Adaptation:** `clearRes.error` is captured (`clearError`) alongside `clearedAt`. `advance(id, 'dispatched', 'coordinator', ...)` now passes `` `clear-refused:${clearError}` `` as `detail` when the clear was refused — the refusal is a fact in `run_events` now, not a silently discarded string. The wave brief is queued only when `!resumed || clearedAt !== null` — wave 1 (nothing else was ever written into that context) or a wave N≥2 whose `/clear` actually verified; a refused clear queues NOTHING, leaving `POST /api/mail` open for a coordinator to send the brief directly once the context is actually fresh. The dispatch response gains `briefQueued: boolean` and, when refused, `clearError` — so the caller does not have to guess. `CoordStore.runEvents` is widened to select `detail` (previously omitted from the SELECT entirely, so nothing had ever needed it back) — additive, every existing caller reads a subset of fields, never the whole row shape. Pinned by rewriting the existing `run-routes.test.ts` refused-`/clear` case: asserts `briefQueued: false`, `clearError: 'dialog-open'`, the exact `run_events.detail` string, and `dueDeliveries()` returning `[]`. Measured: reverting `briefQueued` to an unconditional `true`, or dropping the `detail` argument from the `advance()` call, each fails the rewritten test in isolation; the rest of the suite (35 other tests) stays green.

### D-48 (bug, found in the same later Task 9 review) — close committed the terminal transition before the fleet act, so a 501/502 wedged the run terminally closed with its hold never released and no route able to free it

`advance(id,'closing')`, `advance(id,state)` and `setHandoffCommit` all ran BEFORE the fleet act (`ws-release`/`ws-hold`/`ws-archive`), and every arm of that fleet act can still answer 501 (`verbSupported` false) or 502 (`runCcd` failure). On the final wave, `verifyDone` passing and `.prhistory` folding cleanly, the run advanced to `done` (`closedAt` stamped) and only THEN attempted `ws-release` — a single dropped agent-WS round trip in remote mode is enough. `RUN_TRANSITIONS.done` is `[]`, so a retry of close re-ran `verifyDone`, re-folded `.prhistory`, then 409'd `bad-transition` from `done`: no route in this build could ever release that hold again. The route's own comment claimed this mirrors `POST /api/sessions/:id/archive` "including its 501" — it did not: that route checks `verbSupported` before touching anything, so its 501 is a pure refusal with no state change; here the 501 arrived after the run was already terminally closed. The pre-fix test pinning the 501 asserted only `statusCode === 501`, never `coord.run(id)!.state`, so the suite concealed that the run closed anyway.

**Adaptation:** the fleet act now runs BEFORE the transition commit — `verifyDone` (or its D-49 skip) → `.prhistory` fold → the fleet act → only once it has actually succeeded, `advance('closing')` → `advance(state)` → `setHandoffCommit` (→ D-51's `setProgramState` check). A 501/502 now returns with `run.state` completely untouched, so a retried `POST .../close` simply re-runs the same steps — the "mirrors `POST /api/sessions/:id/archive`" claim is now actually true. Reordering alone would have reintroduced a DIFFERENT wedge — a double-close (or a close on a run still `planned` with `sessionId` set from an open-time reclaim) would now run the fleet act a SECOND time before discovering the transition was illegal — so a second, read-only precondition joins it: `if (!RUN_TRANSITIONS[run.state].includes('closing')) return 409 bad-transition`, checked immediately after the existing `not-dispatched` check and before the fleet act. `advance()` remains the only writer. Pinned by three new/rewritten `run-routes.test.ts` cases: the existing 501 test now also asserts `run.state` stays `'dispatched'` and `closedAt` stays `null`; a new double-close case asserts the second call 409s `bad-transition` from `'done'` with zero additional `ws-release` calls. Measured: reverting the precondition to `if (false)` fails the double-close case (2 `ws-release` calls instead of 1) while leaving the 501 case green; separately, swapping the fleet-act block back to AFTER the transition-commit block reproduces the original bug and fails only the 501 case (`state` reads `'done'`, not `'dispatched'`) while leaving the double-close case green — the two mutants are independent and each is caught by exactly the case that names it.

### D-49 (bug, found in the same later Task 9 review) — an explicit operator ABANDON (`state:'failed'`) was gated on the same done-claim re-measurement a `done` close needs, permanently refusing to close a run whose branch or worktree becomes genuinely unmeasurable

`verifyDone` re-measures a DONE claim (spec:127-129, "never believe a done claim") — but the close route ran it unconditionally, including for `state:'failed'` with `archive:true`, the abandon path. If a dispatched run's worker abandons the branch, the branch is deleted, or the worktree is reaped, `verifyDone` answers `tip-unmeasurable` PERMANENTLY — there is nothing to re-measure against ever again. Every close attempt then 409s, so the run stays wedged in `dispatched` forever, `dispatchedAt` still set, counting against `capsUsage().running` — the identical wedge D-13 was recorded and fixed for (`planned` runs pinning `running`), reached this time through the `dispatched` side. With the default `maxConcurrentWorkers = 3`, three such runs refuse every future dispatch fleet-wide with `cap-concurrency`, and this PR ships no route that can move a run to `failed`, delete it, or edit the caps around it — the only recovery was hand-editing `~/.ccrc/coord.db`.

**Adaptation:** `state:'failed'` is not a claim of doneness — it is the operator saying "this did not complete," which needs no fingerprint to re-measure against. Step 1 of the close route now reads `if (state !== 'failed') { … verifyDone … }`, skipping the call (and its I/O) entirely for an explicit abandon; the `fingerprint` body field stays required (shape-only — `typeof` checks, no SHA validation) so the route's request shape does not fork, and `handoffCommit` is still recorded from whatever the operator claimed. `readPrHistory`'s own refuse-on-unreadable-ledger step is UNCHANGED and still runs for an abandon close — `.prhistory` lives in the registry directory, not the ephemeral worktree the branch-tip measurement can no longer reach, so it is not subject to the same unmeasurability. Pinned by a new `run-routes.test.ts` case: a `state:'failed'` close against the exact stale-tip fixture the FIRST close test in the file proves `verifyDone` refuses for a `done` close, asserting `200`, `state:'failed'`, and `capsUsage().running === 0` afterward. Measured: reverting the `state !== 'failed'` guard to always call `verifyDone` fails this case with a `409 stale-tip` exactly as predicted; the rest of the close suite (11 other cases, including the pre-existing `done` stale-tip refusal) stays green.

### D-50 (test-coverage gap, found in the same later Task 9 review) — the one test of the accept direction for `foldPrLineage` used a fixture that never wrote a real `.prhistory` entry, so deleting the fold call at the close site left the suite green

`run-routes.test.ts`'s `'folds the PR lineage it CAN read, and [] is a measured answer'` test — the only test in the file that closes a run down the accept path — writes NO `.prhistory` file at all, so `readPrHistory` answers `{ok:true, entries:[]}` and the assertion is `expect(coord.run(id)!.prLineage).toEqual([])`. But `hydrateRun` already answers `[]` for a NULL `prLineage` column with nothing ever having folded anything (`row.prLineage ? JSON.parse(...) : []`). Deleting `coord.foldPrLineage(id, history.entries)` at the close route outright left this test — and the whole suite — green, because no test in the file ever closed a run whose `.prhistory` named a real entry (the only other `.prhistory`-touching case is the unreadable-file test, where the fold never runs at all). A mapping bug at the fold site, or the call dropping in a later refactor, would have every closed run read `prLineage: []` on the wire — "this run retired no PRs" — for a workspace whose `.prhistory` names real merges: exactly the forgery `ccd/ccd:2018-2035` and the route's own docstring name, undetected.

**Adaptation:** no code change — the fold call was already correct. A new `run-routes.test.ts` case writes a real, valid `.prhistory` line (`{pr:42, branch, phase:'merged', recordedAt:12345}`) before dispatching and closing the run, and asserts `coord.run(id)!.prLineage` equals that entry exactly, discriminating the fold call's presence from `hydrateRun`'s own `[]` default. Measured: commenting out `coord.foldPrLineage(id, history.entries)` at the close route fails this new case (`prLineage` reads `[]` against an expected non-empty array) while the pre-existing empty-ledger case stays green, confirming the two together — not either alone — cover both the "absent is measured `[]`" and "present is actually folded" facts.

### D-51 (bug, found in the same later Task 9 review) — D-26's own `setProgramState` writer shipped with ZERO callers in the tree Task 9 actually wrote, and this section's own D-26 entry still named Task 9 as "not yet written" two commits after it was

D-26 landed `CoordStore.setProgramState` and explicitly assigned its caller to "whichever task closes the last run of a program (Task 9, not yet written in this tree)". Task 9 shipped the close route and never called it: `grep -rn setProgramState server/src` returned only the method's own definition; the only other hit in the whole tree was `coord-store.test.ts`, which pins the method directly but not that anything routes to it. `openRun`'s `INSERT … ON CONFLICT(slug) DO UPDATE` touches only `title` in its conflict arm, and `reconstruct`'s does the same — so `programs.state` never left `'active'` for anything this build's ordinary steady state actually does. Concretely: a coordinator opens `build4`, dispatches and closes every one of its runs, then opens `build5`. `programs` now holds two rows both `state='active'`. `resolveCoordinator(null)`'s "exactly one active program" guard (`store.ts:392-394`) sees `active.length !== 1` and returns `null` forever, so every `POST /api/mail {toId:'coordinator'}` sent without an explicit `runId` is refused `unknown-recipient` and recorded, for the life of the database — the same failure D-26 measured and fixed at the store level, still fully live at the route level because nothing ever called the fix.

**Adaptation:** the close route now checks `coord.programOpenRunCount(run.program)` — a new `CoordStore` method, `SELECT count(*) FROM runs WHERE program = ? AND state NOT IN ('done','failed')`, `capsUsage().running`'s own predicate scoped to one program — immediately after its own final `advance()` succeeds (D-48's ordering: only once the run being closed is ITSELF already terminal). Zero remaining open runs under that program calls `setProgramState(run.program, state === 'failed' ? 'abandoned' : 'done')` — the program's retirement state mirrors the OUTCOME of the run that closed it out. Stated as a limitation rather than modelled further: a program whose earlier waves failed but whose LAST wave happens to close `done` (or the reverse) retires by the last run's own verdict alone: not more precisely than that, and not blocking `resolveCoordinator` either way, which only ever cares whether the count of ACTIVE programs is exactly one. `setProgramState`'s own docstring (`store.ts`) and this plan's D-26 entry are both corrected to name Task 9 as the actual caller. Pinned by a new `run-routes.test.ts` case: a program with two runs, closing the first (a sibling still open) asserts `programs()` stays `'active'`; closing the second (the last one, as an explicit `state:'failed'` abandon — D-49 lets this close without a real git root) asserts `programs()` reads `'abandoned'`. Measured: reverting the `programOpenRunCount(...) === 0` branch to `if (false)` fails only this new case (`'active'` where `'abandoned'` was expected); the rest of the close suite is unaffected.

### D-52 (bug, found in a later Task 10 review) — five unguarded synchronous `CoordStore` calls sat directly on the watcher tick and the `/ws/fleet` connect path, so one SQLite error killed the whole server

`tick()`'s own design is stated in its neighbouring comments and enforced everywhere except Task 10's own additions: every lane is either `void …().catch(…)` ("one bad sweep must not kill the poll" — `sweepPr`/`sweepNames`/`sweepMail`), `try`/`catch`ed (`saveSnapshot`, "never blocks the poll"), or swallow-by-construction (`readRegistry` answers `[]` when `io.readdir` is null). Task 10 landed five bare, synchronous `CoordStore` calls with no guard between them and the promise: `pushOne`'s `recordFeedEvent` (`watch.ts`, reached from the `done` push, the ask push, and both new lanes), `pushNewMail`/`pushNewRuns`'s own `mailQueuedSince`/`runEventsSince`, the priming tick's `maxMailDeliveryId`/`maxRunEventId`, `emitRuns`'s `coord.runs()`, and — outside `watch.ts` entirely — `server.ts`'s `/ws/fleet` connect handler, which reads `deps.coord.runs()` inside a `void assembleFleet(...).then(…)` chain with no `.catch` anywhere on it. `node:sqlite` throws SYNCHRONOUSLY (measured: a second `DatabaseSync` connection to one file, `db.close()` then `db.prepare(...)`, throws `Error: database is not open` immediately, no retry, no wait) — a full disk (this fleet already ships a 10G floor check) or a second connection racing `tx()`'s `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for coord.db's write lock is enough. `start()` fires `tick()` as `void this.tick()` with no `unhandledRejection` handler anywhere in the tree (`grep -rn "process.on(" server/src/` finds none), so an unguarded throw from any of the four `watch.ts` sites kills the process; the fifth, in `server.ts`, is an unhandled rejection off a browser simply connecting during the fault window — a condition systemd's restart loop cannot clear, because the underlying fault (a full disk, a held lock) is still there after the restart.

**Adaptation:** each of the five sites gets its own `try`/`catch`, in the same idiom the file's other lanes already use — a `console.warn` naming the failed call and stating what still happened (the ring record and the push are unaffected by a lost archive write; a skipped `runs` emission is picked up by the next successful tick) — never a shared silent swallow, so a real fault is still visible in the journal even though it no longer kills the server. `pushNewMail`/`pushNewRuns` are wrapped at their call sites in `tick()` (their own coord reads are the first statement in each, so wrapping the call covers both); the priming tick's two watermark reads share one `try` block; `emitRuns` and `pushOne`'s `recordFeedEvent` call are each wrapped internally, since both have callers beyond the two guarded lanes above (`emitRuns` runs every tick unconditionally; `pushOne` is reached from `ask`/`done`/`merged` too, not just `mail`/`run`). `server.ts`'s `/ws/fleet` handler wraps its own `deps.coord.runs()` read the same way, so a connecting browser during a database fault gets `hello`/`fleet` with no `runs` frame rather than taking the server down for every other socket. Pinned by two new `push-copy.test.ts` cases (closing a real `CoordStore`'s `db` mid-test to reproduce the exact throw class, rather than a mock) — one proving all four `watch.ts` guards fire and warn while the `done` push and its `NotifyLog` record still go through unharmed, one proving a break on the very first (priming) tick degrades instead of crashing — and one new `fleetws.test.ts` case proving the `/ws/fleet` connect handler survives the identical fault and a later frame still reaches the same socket. Measured: reverting any one `try`/`catch` back to a bare call reproduces an uncaught throw (a rejected `tick()` promise, or an unhandled rejection off the `/ws/fleet` `.then()`) against its own new test, with the rest of each suite unaffected.

### D-53 (bug, found in the same later Task 10 review) — `feed_events.kind` was cast into `NotifyEvent['kind']` on an exemption whose own justification was the opposite of what its cited precedent actually does

`coord/schema.ts`'s header comment exempted `feed_events.kind` from the Global Constraint's "every enum has a designated we-do-not-know member" storage rule, on the grounds that `CoordStore.feedEvents` "reads it back with a documented cast — the same stance `mail_rejections.code` below already takes, and for the identical reason." That precedent does not hold: `rejections()`'s own docstring in `store.ts` states, in so many words, that `code` "stays a raw `string`… nothing here re-validates it… stated instead of silently cast" — and the raw string never escapes into a typed wire shape, since nothing beyond this file's own tests reads `rejections()` today. `feedEvents` did the opposite in both halves: `kind: r.kind as NotifyEvent['kind']` was an actual cast, not a stated absence of one, and the result is returned straight out of `GET /api/feed` as `{ events: NotifyEvent[] }`. The exemption's second-order justification — "written only from a value this server itself already typed" — is the identical same-build-wrote-it assumption `user_version`, D-8, and `shared/api.ts`'s own rollback paragraph (`:567-571`, "a rollback breaks even that: `deploy.sh` keeps per-timestamp backups") exist to refuse: a rollback to an older server against a newer store, or a later build that adds a seventh `NotifyEvent.kind`, both put a token in this column that the RUNNING server never wrote and does not recognise. This was also the one enum vocabulary in `shared/api.ts` with no exported predicate to guard with — `isRunState`/`isWorkItemState`/`isProgramState`/`isMailDeliveryState`/`isPrReason` all have one; `NotifyEvent.kind`'s `NOTIFY_KINDS` was a private `const` used only inline inside `reviveNotifyEvent`.

**Adaptation:** `shared/api.ts` gains `isNotifyKind`, exported beside the now-typed `NOTIFY_KINDS: readonly NotifyEvent['kind'][]`, in the same `PR_PHASES`/`isPrPhase` split every sibling vocabulary already uses; `reviveNotifyEvent` is rewritten to call it instead of inlining the same `.includes` check. `CoordStore.feedEvents` reads `kind` through `isNotifyKind(r.kind) ? r.kind : 'unknown'` — a guard, never a cast — and its own docstring, plus `coord/schema.ts`'s header paragraph, are corrected: `feed_events.kind` IS a sixth we-do-not-know column now, the `mail_rejections.code` comparison is removed (it was never the precedent it claimed to be), and the rollback hazard is named explicitly. Pinned by two new `coord-store.test.ts` cases, matching the exact pattern `run()`'s own `reads a state token this build does not know` test already uses: a raw `UPDATE feed_events SET kind = ?` (a value `recordFeedEvent`'s typed signature would never let a caller pass) reads back as `'unknown'`; every known kind still reads back unchanged. Measured: reverting `feedEvents`'s guard to the original cast fails the first new case (`'review'` reads back as `'review'`, not `'unknown'`) while leaving the second green; the rest of `coord-store.test.ts` (69 other cases in the same file, at the pre-fix baseline) is unaffected.

### D-54 (bug, found in the same later Task 10 review) — a run-less mail push decorated with an empty project rendered a dangling ` · ` in any multi-project fleet

`pushNewMail` passed `project: m.project ?? ''`. `mailQueuedSince`'s `project` comes from a `LEFT JOIN runs r ON r.id = m.runId` and is `null` whenever the mail has no run — a fully supported, first-class case: `POST /api/mail` treats `runId` as optional and normalises absent/null to `null` (`coord/routes.ts`), and `mailQueuedSince`'s own docstring names it ("`null` for ad-hoc mail with no run context"). `pushOne` decorates unconditionally on project COUNT: `projects.size > 1 ? \`${e.title} · ${e.project}\` : e.title` — with no check that `e.project` is non-empty. In a fleet with two live projects (the deployed shape — the whole `projects.size > 1` rule exists because multi-project is normal), a run-less `POST /api/mail` produced `✉ finding › cc-a · ` — a separator with nothing after it — written into both `NotifyLog` (the catch-up ring) and `feed_events` (the durable archive), so the malformed string outlives the tick that raised it. No test caught it: every mail case in `push-copy.test.ts` seeded exactly one project, so the decoration branch was never entered on the run-less-mail lane.

**Adaptation:** two-part fix, both halves of the finding's own "either/or." `tick()` builds `sessionProjects`, a `Map<string,string>` off the SAME fleet assembly it already has in scope (`sessions.map((s) => [s.id, s.project])`), and passes it into `pushNewMail`, which now resolves `m.project ?? sessionProjects.get(m.toId) ?? ''` — the recipient session's own project is the honest, informative fallback the finding names as preferred. `pushOne`'s decoration guard is also hardened as a backstop: `projects.size > 1 && e.project !== '' ? … : e.title`, so even the rarer case (a recipient no longer in the registry, so the map lookup also misses) degrades to no decoration at all rather than a dangling separator — never an empty string reaching the title. Pinned by two new `push-copy.test.ts` cases in a two-project fixture: a run-less mail to a live recipient asserts the title carries that session's own project; a run-less mail to a recipient with no fleet entry at all asserts the title carries NO separator. Measured: reverting `pushOne`'s guard to the original unconditional decoration reproduces the dangling `' · '` on the second case; reverting `pushNewMail`'s fallback to `''` alone (guard still in place) collapses the first case to the no-decoration title instead of naming `cc-a`'s actual project — the two new cases discriminate both halves independently.

### D-55 (bug, found in the same later Task 10 review) — every run close fired two pushes, one naming a state (`closing`) that exists for microseconds and that the operator can neither act on nor ever observe

`pushNewRuns` pushes one notification per `run_events` row, unconditionally, and `POST /api/runs/:id/close` commits `advance(id,'closing')` immediately followed by `advance(id,state)` — two adjacent synchronous statements with nothing between them that can fail in practice (`RUN_TRANSITIONS.closing = ['done','failed']`, so the final `advance` cannot legally refuse after the first succeeded) — so the SAME watcher tick always reads both rows. An operator closing a run got `▸ closing › <ws>` and, in the same tick, `▸ done › <ws>` — distinct tags, so the tray does not collapse them, and `closing` is bookkeeping internal to the close route, not a state the operator was ever meant to see land. Half of all run notifications were it — the same "noise dressed as information" `pushOne`'s own docstring rejects two lines above ("a notification for the pane on your screen trains you to dismiss notifications"). `push-copy.test.ts` pinned the behaviour as intended (`expect(sent).toHaveLength(3)`, "one push per transition, not one per run"), so it would not have self-corrected. The feed genuinely does want every transition (`recordAlways` already gets it there); the tray does not, and `pushOne` had no way to say "record, never push" — `recordAlways` only ever exempted the record from the PRESENCE gate, never the push from firing at all.

**Adaptation:** `pushOne` gains `recordOnly?: boolean` — recorded exactly like every other kind (subject to the same presence gate above it), but the actual `push?.notify(...)` call is skipped. `pushNewRuns` sets it to `r.toState === 'closing'`, and only there — every other transition (`dispatched`, `working`, `done`, `failed`, …) is unaffected. Pinned by rewriting the existing `push-copy.test.ts` `run`-lane case (now asserting exactly TWO pushes, `dispatched` and `done`, with `closing` absent from the tray) and a new case that also wires a `NotifyLog` and asserts `log.seq === 3` (all three transitions recorded) alongside `feedEvents()` carrying all three titles including `▸ closing › ccrc-pwa` — proving the suppression is push-only, never record-only. Measured: reverting `recordOnly` to ignored (the early-return path `pushOne` already had before this fix) fails the rewritten case (3 pushes where 2 were expected, `closing` present in `sent`); reverting `pushNewRuns`'s own `recordOnly: r.toState === 'closing'` line to omit the option entirely reproduces the identical failure, confirming both the new `pushOne` parameter and its one caller are load-bearing together.

### D-56 (bug, found in a later Task 11 review) — the Fleet coordination section's own "Run lifecycle" — Task 11 Step 1's whole deliverable — described an API the shipped routes do not implement, in four of its six lines

Checked line-by-line against `server/src/coord/routes.ts` rather than against the route names alone: (1) `POST /api/runs` was described as committing a ledger and placing a hold; the route's own docstring says, verbatim, "THE LEDGER IS NOT WRITTEN HERE, AND IT IS NOT READ HERE," and the `ws-hold` call is inside `if (typeof sessionId === 'string')` — the wave-N≥2 reclaim path only. A wave-1 open places no hold at all; the hold does not exist until dispatch. (2) Wave 1 was named `ccd ws-add`/`start`; `CCD_ARGV.start` is never called anywhere in the coordination lane (`wsHold`, `wsAdd`, `ensure`, `wsArchive`, `wsRelease`, `prStateSession` are the only argv builders this lane uses) — wave 1 is `ws-add` alone, then a separate `ws-hold` at dispatch's own step 5. (3) Step 5 described the coordinator updating the hold reason and "dispatching wave N+1 into the *same* workspace (back to step 2)" — in the shipped machine, updating the hold for wave N+1 is a **non-final** `POST /api/runs/:id/close` (`final:false`), which drives the run being closed to a **terminal** `done`/`failed` (`RUN_TRANSITIONS.done = []`, `.failed = []`); dispatch also refuses any run not in `planned` (D-46). "Back to step 2" on the SAME run id is a 409 — wave N+1 needs a second `POST /api/runs` carrying `sessionId`, opening a NEW run row. (4) Step 6 described an explicit abandon (`state:'failed'`) as archiving immediately; the route's own conditional (`if (state === 'failed' && archive) … else if (final) … else …`) only calls `wsArchive` when `archive:true` is ALSO passed — `state:'failed'` alone (`final:true`, no `archive`) only releases, exactly like a normal close, and `state:'failed'` with `final:false` re-holds the workspace under the next-wave reason even though the run just went terminal, the wedge `run-routes.test.ts:541`'s own case name ("archives on explicit abandon (state:failed, **archive:true**)") already flags by omission.

**Adaptation:** the README's "Run lifecycle" section is rewritten against the route bodies rather than their names — the ledger is named as unwritten/unread here; wave 1's no-hold-at-open and dispatch-time hold are both stated; the verb list drops `start`; step 5 now names the non-final `close` call and the fact that it makes the CURRENT run row terminal, with wave N+1 as a new `POST /api/runs` back to step 1; step 6 states the `archive:true` requirement explicitly and names the `final:false`-abandon wedge as a caution rather than leaving it undiscovered. No code changes — the routes were already correct; only the operator-facing description of them was wrong, and it was Task 11 Step 1's entire deliverable.

### D-57 (bug, found in the same later Task 11 review) — the missing-box-token boot warning names the OPPOSITE polarity for `/api/mail`, the one operator-visible signal the token was never minted

`index.ts`'s boot warning read "no box token at `<path>` — `/api/notify` and `/api/mail` accept unauthenticated callers" — true the day it landed (`eb9c88a`), false since D-39 (Task 7 fix round) made `checkMailToken(null, …)` answer `'unconfigured'`, which both mail gates (`routes.ts`'s ingress and its ack route) treat as `verdict !== 'ok'` and refuse with 401 — their own detail strings say, in so many words, "`/api/mail` fails shut on an unconfigured token, it does not fail open." `/api/notify` is the one route that still passes an unconfigured token through (`server.ts` has no `'unconfigured'` arm on that gate, and — unlike `'bad'`/`'legacy'` — logs nothing on that path). D-39 fixed the gate but never revisited this line, and no test in the tree pins the string (`grep` finds it only in `src`, `dist`, and this plan), which is why the fix never propagated to it. Concretely: a fresh checkout whose `deploy/ccrc-mail.token` was never minted (`ship_secret`'s only guard is `[ -f "$local_file" ]`, so both deploys exit 0 having shipped nothing) boots, prints the one line an operator would grep the journal for, and that line says the opposite of what is wrong — every wave brief, done-claim, and ack gets a silent `401` forever, and the operator is told mail is wide open rather than that the mail bus is entirely dead.

**Adaptation:** the warning is rewritten to state both routes' actual, opposite postures — `/api/notify` still accepts unauthenticated callers (its one-deploy legacy tolerance); `/api/mail` and `/api/mail/:id/ack` fail SHUT and refuse every caller with 401. No test added: `index.ts` is the composition root, run once at process start with no existing test harness anywhere in the tree that imports and asserts against it (`server/test/` has no `index.test.ts`, `boot*.test.ts`, or any precedent for exercising this file directly) — adding one would be a new test-harness shape for this one string, not an adaptation of an existing one. `verify-service.sh`'s journal-tail-on-failed-deploy (Task 8) is the operator's actual net for a boot-time misconfiguration; this fix makes what lands in that journal honest.

### D-58 (test-coverage gap, found in the same later Task 11 review) — Task 11's own mutation table credited "dispatch: check caps before the pause file" to a fixture that cannot discriminate it

The cited case (`refuses while $REG/coordinator-paused exists, before counting anything`) opens exactly one run and touches the pause marker, with the schema defaults still in place (`maxConcurrentWorkers=3`, `maxSessionsPerDay=12`) and `capsUsage()` reading `{running:0, dispatchedIn24h:0}` — under the swapped order, both cap guards (`0 >= 3`, `0 >= 12`) are false regardless, so execution falls through to the pause check either way and the route answers the identical `409 refused:'paused'`. Measured directly against `server/src/coord/routes.ts` (pause block and caps block swapped, sha256-verified restore after): the full pre-fix `run-routes.test.ts` suite (36/36 of the file's then-cases) stays green under the mutant. The sibling cap tests never touch the pause marker, so they cannot discriminate it either.

**Adaptation:** a new case sets BOTH caps to `0` (guaranteeing a caps-first ordering would answer `cap-concurrency`) and ALSO touches `coordinator-paused`, asserting `refused:'paused'` and `calls: []`. Measured: with the pause/caps blocks swapped back to the mutant order, this new case fails (`refused:'cap-concurrency'` where `'paused'` was expected) while the rest of the file (36/37) stays green; restored to the shipped order, the full file (37/37) passes.

### D-59 (test-coverage gap, found in the same later Task 11 review) — Task 11's own mutation table bundled two `capsUsage` mutants behind one case that cannot kill the `>=` half and only kills the calendar-day half 23 hours out of every 24

The cited case (`rolling 24h, not a calendar day`) is the only 24h-window fixture in the tree; `grep -rn '24 \* 3600\|86400' server/test/*.ts` found no exact-boundary fixture anywhere. (a) `dispatchedAt > ?` differs from `>=` only at `dispatchedAt === now - 24h` exactly; every existing fixture sits at `now`, `now - 23h`, or `now - 25h`, none of which cross that boundary. Measured against `server/src/coord/store.ts` (`>` → `>=`, sha256-verified restore after): the full pre-fix suite (`coord-store.test.ts` + `run-routes.test.ts`, 64/64 of the then-cases) stays green under the mutant. (b) The cited case's own fixture used a bare `Date.now() - 23h`; a calendar-day count agrees with the correct rolling-24h answer whenever the real wall clock is within the last hour of the local day (because `now - 23h` then still falls on the same date) — the mutant is caught only the other 23 hours out of 24, a verdict that depends on when the sweep happens to run rather than on the code.

**Adaptation:** two fixtures added. `coord-store.test.ts` gains a case dispatched at exactly `now - 24h`, asserting `dispatchedIn24h: 0` — measured: the `>=` mutant fails it (`dispatchedIn24h: 1`) while the rest of the file (64/65) stays green. `run-routes.test.ts`'s existing calendar-day case now pins `now` with `vi.useFakeTimers({toFake:['Date']})`/`vi.setSystemTime` at a fixed local noon (`2024-06-15T12:00:00Z`) rather than reading the real clock, so `now - 23h` lands on the previous calendar date deterministically regardless of when the suite runs — the same fixed-epoch idiom `mail-sweep.test.ts`/`pr-sweep.test.ts` already use for their own `vi.setSystemTime` fixtures. Measured: a calendar-day rewrite of `capsUsage`'s 24h count (grouping by `toDateString()`) fails this case (`200` where `409 cap-daily` was expected) deterministically, restored to the shipped rolling-window query it passes; the rest of the file (64/65) is unaffected either way.

### D-60 (record gap, found in the same later Task 11 review) — Task 11's own mutation table was never amended for the Task 10 review round; D-52..D-55's nine-odd added constructs had no table rows

Every prior review round appended an amendment block beneath the Step 5 table AND added rows crediting what it found (Task 2, Task 4 twice, Task 5 twice, Task 3, Task 6, Task 9). The Task 10 review commit (`8e38f7b`) landed D-52..D-55 as prose only (this section, above) and stopped there — `grep -n 'recordOnly|isNotifyKind|sessionProjects|feedEvents'` against the table itself hits nothing. The measurements exist (each of D-52..D-55's own paragraphs states what was reverted and what failed), so this was a record gap, not an unmeasured one — but a gate task whose own table is stale by two commits is the exact crediting-drift the table has been amended for six times running to prevent.

**Adaptation:** the amendment block below adds one row per construct D-52..D-55 touched, crediting the measurements already recorded in each deviation's own adaptation paragraph rather than re-running them (the same stance D-33's credit in the Task 6 amendment above takes for a fix and its test that landed together).

### D-61 (process gap, found in the same later Task 11 review, NOT fixed here — see Deviations) — Task 11 Steps 6 and 7 never ran, and Definition-of-Done item bug #21 (`spec:181-183`) is still `pending`

Task 11 landed as one docs-only commit (`23ef195`, README.md) plus Step 8's `git add`. Nothing on the branch records Step 6 (agent-first deploy of the token + `notify.sh`, then the server behind `verify-service.sh`) or Step 7 (the live proof: the token triple against `/api/notify`, `PRAGMA user_version`, a fenced brief landing in a real pane, an ack stopping replay, the four negatives, and Step 7.8's ten-delivery/three-session count closing or formally restating bug #21). Every other task on this branch records its verification in its commit body; `23ef195` carries none.

**Not fixed in this review round.** Steps 6 and 7 require SSH access to two real boxes, a locally-minted `deploy/ccrc-mail.token`, a real deploy (`deploy/deploy.sh`), and live multi-session traffic against a running fleet — every one of which this review round's own safety constraints forbid outright (no `ssh`, no network mutations, no live deploy). Recorded here rather than silently left implicit, and carried into this round's own summary: **PR I must not be represented as complete**, and Bug #21 (task list item #21) stays `pending` until an operator with deploy access runs Step 6, then Step 7 including its 7.8 count, for real.

## Global Constraints (from the spec, verbatim where quoted)

- **The storage rules, and they are deliberately NOT the cache rules.** "`user_version` pragma **is** the schema version. Migrations are forward-only, idempotent, run at open, in a transaction — and a DB that cannot migrate **refuses to start loudly** (rejection-collapses-to-empty is the cache rule; here it would erase a program's history)." "Columns are additive-only; nullable means 'an older build lacked it'; every enum has a designated we-do-not-know member." "An older build meeting a newer schema ignores unknown columns and never treats a higher `user_version` as fatal downward — it may only refuse to *migrate*, never to *read*." "The markdown ledger stays the disaster-recovery ground truth… a test proves the reconstruction path for a representative program." "`engines` fields land in all three package.jsons and CI's node version is asserted against them."
  The code comment on `openCoordDb` must **name** `server/src/fleetstate.ts:47-55` ("the READ is the version negotiation") and `pwa/src/lib/offline.ts:11-16` (v1-forever) and say why neither transfers — otherwise a reviewer will cite them against this file, which is exactly what `spec:66-69` anticipates.
- **"No new ccd verbs in this build."** "`ws-hold` is the claim/veto, `ws-add`/`start` are dispatch, `pr-open`/`pr-state` are the PR lane, `ws-archive` the close. Every one is already granted and gated." If a task finds this impossible, it records a deviation — it does not invent a verb. (Two already exist: **D-1** and **D-5**.)
- **Identity is attribution, not authentication.** "On the fleet host, identity is attribution, not authentication. All sessions share one UNIX user… The spec never pretends otherwise." The box token "authenticates *the box*, which is the honest unit; per-session identity rides as attribution: `{fromId, fromUuid}`, verified server-side against the registry (`fromUuid === $REG/<id>.uuid` read via FleetIO, the hookstate gate's exact shape) — **freshness, not forgery-proofness, and the spec says so**."
- **`/api/notify` adopts the box token in this build** (operator ruling 1, `spec:150-155`): "`notify.sh` gains the token read from `~/.cc-secrets/ccrc-mail.token`; the server accepts the old shape for one deploy generation (absent token → accepted, logged as legacy) so the hook cannot go dark mid-rollout, then the tolerance is removed."
- **Rejection codes are typed and total** — "`unknown-sender`, `stale-uuid`, `unknown-recipient`, `unknown-run`, `oversize`, `bad-kind`, `unauthenticated`, plus the done-authority set (§3). Every rejection is itself recorded."
- **Delivery.** "`MAIL_SWEEP_MS = 10_000`, never awaited… session alive; hookstate fresh and `state ∈ {done, idle}`-equivalent; no dialog/ask pending; quiet ≥ 60s; per-session mail cooldown; a fleet `mail-disabled` marker file as kill-switch (read via FleetIO). Only then does it inject through **`sendPrompt`'s full proof discipline** (echo verified, draft-present refused, dialog-open refused) inside the session's KeyedQueue slot." "**Busy sessions are backed off, not stacked.**" "`attempts` capped with exponential spacing, and a delivery that keeps failing parks as `rejected('undeliverable')` with its mail intact." "Until acked, the delivery replays — **verbatim, never re-rendered** — on later sweeps after cooldown."
- **Nothing in this build teaches `sendPrompt` to retry.** Its two-Enter budget and `submitEnter`'s one-Enter doctrine (`server/src/inject/send.ts:456-460`) are load-bearing: "A human tap does: they looked at the pane first." The escalation for a stuck box is the human.
- **Caps and pause.** "`maxConcurrentWorkers` (running runs), `maxSessionsPerDay` (dispatches per rolling 24h) — rows in `coordinator_state`… **Pause is a file**: `$REG/coordinator-paused` on the fleet host, operator-owned (`touch`/`rm`), read via FleetIO before any dispatch… no verb, no route, no way for the coordinator to unpause itself."
- **The wire is additive.** "A `{type:'runs'}` frame on `/ws/fleet` (additive; old clients drop it)… REST `GET /api/runs` for cold start". New `NotifyEvent` kinds need "a client-side unknown-kind degradation branch (today's closed 3-union has none), tags that do **not** collapse per session… and a **presence-gate exemption**: agent-to-agent mail is a record, not a 'needs your eyes' ping".
- **Restart semantics.** "DB is authoritative; sweeps resume; in-flight deliveries re-verify against hookstate before re-injecting; the primed-quiet rule (no notification storm on boot) extends to the mail lane."
- **Bug #21** (prompt-inject text→Enter race) "lives on this exact path; its formal closure… is a Definition-of-Done item here, not a footnote."
- Run ALL verification **FOREGROUND** in single blocking calls. The server suite is ~200 s and the ccd files alone are ~90 s — use `timeout: 600000` ms. Report REAL printed counts. **Never background a suite.**
- **Mutation sweep the whole diff** — one literal mutant per added construct, full suite per mutant, sha256-verified restore between (Task 11).
- **Never run `ccd` against the live HOME. Never touch tmux, `~/.cc-sessions`, `~/.cc-limits`, `~/.cc-secrets`. No `git push`, no `gh`.** The ccd suites already run the real script against fixture HOMEs; that is the only place ccd executes.

**Read the code before you write it.** Every code block below is **shape-authoritative, not text-authoritative**: it fixes the decision, the comment and the assertion, but where it disagrees with a harness helper, an existing idiom or a neighbouring file's conventions, **the tree wins**. `server/test/helpers.ts` is the authority on `testDeps` (it returns `{cfg, runCcd, tmux, io, queue}` and nothing else); `server/test/tmpHelpers.ts` owns fixture lifetimes (`mkTmp`, with its own `afterAll`); `server/test/name-sweep.test.ts` is the authority on how a `void`-dispatched lane is tested (fake timers, a public sweep method, a `Runner` that records argv); `server/test/fleetws.test.ts` is the authority on driving `/ws/fleet` from a test. Copy from them rather than from here when the two differ.

## Task order and why it is this order

Storage before vocabulary before readers before writers before routes before the wire. Every task ends green except Task 3, which is green on its own suite and leaves `shared/api.ts` carrying types nothing consumes yet — deliberate, and closed by Task 9.

1. The node floor becomes a mechanism
2. `~/.ccrc/coord.db` — open, migrate, or refuse loudly
3. The nouns, once — the shared vocabulary and the run state machine
4. `.prhistory` becomes readable — the first server-side read path
5. Done-authority is a fingerprint the server re-measures
6. The box token, and the anonymous ingress closes
7. `POST /api/mail` and `/api/mail/:id/ack` — the total rejection table
8. `HookState.event`, and the lane that never blind-injects
9. The coordinator API — open, dispatch under caps, close
10. The wire — a `runs` frame, two notify kinds, and an old client that stays safe
11. Gates, mutants, the operator record, and the live proof

---

### Task 1: the node floor becomes a mechanism

Nothing in this repo enforces "Node ≥22" today. It is prose in `README.md:18,26`, CI pins `'22'` in two places, and no `package.json` carries an `engines` field. Every line of Task 2 depends on `node:sqlite` being importable, so the floor stops being prose here — **before** anything relies on it.

The server deploy's post-restart check also becomes the same one the agent path has had since the 113-second lesson. `deploy.sh:106` ends at `sleep 1 && curl -fsS "$HEALTH_URL"`, which does catch a boot failure — but a DB that refuses to migrate (Task 2) is exactly the crash-loop class `verify-service.sh` was written for, and `spec:73-76` names wiring it as part of this build.

**Files:**
- Modify: `server/package.json`, `agent/package.json`, `pwa/package.json`
- Create: `server/test/node-floor.test.ts`
- Modify: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`:29-33`, `:70-74` — comments only)
- Modify: `deploy/deploy.sh` (`:102-107`)

**Interfaces:** none exported. The test reads the three `package.json` files and the running `process.version`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `server/test/node-floor.test.ts`:

```ts
// The Node floor stops being prose here. `node:sqlite` is a built-in whose
// availability is version-sensitive — it shipped behind `--experimental-sqlite`
// and stopped needing the flag in a later 22.x — and `server/src/coord/db.ts`
// imports it unconditionally, so a box or a CI leg below the floor does not
// degrade, it fails to boot.
//
// THREE assertions, and the third is the one that matters: the first two pin
// the DECLARATION (all three packages agree, and they say a number), the third
// pins the FACT (this interpreter can actually do it). If they ever disagree,
// the declaration is what moves — see the plan's deviation D-6. Do not "fix" a
// red third assertion by lowering `engines`.
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

const root = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '../..');
const PACKAGES = ['server', 'agent', 'pwa'];

const enginesOf = (pkg: string): string | undefined => {
  const j = JSON.parse(readFileSync(path.join(root, pkg, 'package.json'), 'utf8')) as
    { engines?: { node?: string } };
  return j.engines?.node;
};

/** `>=22.13.0` -> [22, 13, 0]. Deliberately understands ONE range form: the
 *  moment someone writes `^22 || >=24` this parser must fail loudly rather than
 *  silently accept a floor it did not understand. */
const floorOf = (range: string): [number, number, number] => {
  const m = /^>=(\d+)\.(\d+)\.(\d+)$/.exec(range.trim());
  expect(m, `engines.node must be a single '>=x.y.z' range, got ${JSON.stringify(range)}`).not.toBeNull();
  return [Number(m![1]), Number(m![2]), Number(m![3])];
};

describe('the node floor', () => {
  it('is declared in all three package.jsons, identically', () => {
    const ranges = PACKAGES.map((p) => [p, enginesOf(p)] as const);
    for (const [p, r] of ranges) expect(r, `${p}/package.json has no engines.node`).toBeTruthy();
    expect(new Set(ranges.map(([, r]) => r)).size,
      `the three packages declare different floors: ${JSON.stringify(ranges)}`).toBe(1);
  });

  it('is satisfied by the interpreter running this suite', () => {
    const [maj, min, pat] = floorOf(enginesOf('server')!);
    const [rMaj, rMin, rPat] = process.version.slice(1).split('.').map(Number) as [number, number, number];
    const ok = rMaj > maj || (rMaj === maj && (rMin > min || (rMin === min && rPat >= pat)));
    expect(ok, `running ${process.version}, floor is ${enginesOf('server')}`).toBe(true);
  });

  it('can import node:sqlite and construct a DatabaseSync — the reason the floor exists', async () => {
    const { DatabaseSync } = await import('node:sqlite');
    const db = new DatabaseSync(':memory:');
    db.exec('CREATE TABLE probe (a INTEGER)');
    db.prepare('INSERT INTO probe VALUES (?)').run(1);
    expect((db.prepare('SELECT count(*) AS c FROM probe').get() as { c: number }).c).toBe(1);
    db.close();
  });
});
```

Expected: FAIL — `server/package.json has no engines.node`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Declare the floor**

Add to `server/package.json`, `agent/package.json` and `pwa/package.json`, after `"type": "module"`:

```json
  "engines": { "node": ">=22.13.0" },
```

`22.13.0` is the version at which `node:sqlite` stopped requiring `--experimental-sqlite`. **Verify this against the Node release notes when you run this step**; if it is wrong, the third assertion above is what tells you, and the range is what changes. Measured while the plan was written: `node v24.14.1` (fleet host) imports it clean; `spec:36-38` records `v22.22.3` proven on `server-box`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Name the coupling in CI**

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` keeps `node-version: '22'` in both jobs (`:31`, `:72`) — `actions/setup-node` resolves that to the latest 22.x, which is above the floor. Add above each:

```yaml
          # Above the engines floor in server/agent/pwa package.json
          # (>=22.13.0, where node:sqlite stopped needing --experimental-sqlite).
          # server/test/node-floor.test.ts asserts THIS interpreter satisfies it,
          # so lowering either side turns that suite red rather than shipping a
          # server that cannot open its own database.
          node-version: '22'
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Wire `verify-service.sh` into the server deploy**

`deploy/deploy.sh:102-107`, replace the tail of `REMOTE_CMD`:

```bash
  REMOTE_CMD='cd ~/ccrc/server && npm ci && npm run build \
    && mkdir -p ~/.config/systemd/user && cp ~/ccrc/deploy/ccrc.service ~/.config/systemd/user/ \
    && export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/$(id -u) \
    && systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user enable --now ccrc.service \
    && systemctl --user restart ccrc.service \
    && bash ~/ccrc/deploy/verify-service.sh ccrc.service \
    && curl -fsS '"$HEALTH_URL"
```

Two notes for the diff:

- The `sleep 1` is **gone**, not moved: `verify-service.sh` sleeps `CCRC_VERIFY_SETTLE` (3) then `CCRC_VERIFY_WINDOW` (5) of its own (`deploy/verify-service.sh:54-55, 83, 91`), so a second sleep in front of it buys nothing.
- The `curl` **stays, after**. `verify-service.sh` proves the process stayed up across a window longer than `RestartSec` (`deploy/ccrc.service:10`); it says nothing about whether Fastify is listening. The two answer different questions and the server path is the one place both are cheap. This is what makes a `CoordDbUnmigratable` throw at boot (Task 2) a failed deploy with the journal tail attached, rather than a green deploy in front of a three-second crash loop.

- [ ] **Step 6: Run the gates this task moves**

Run: `cd server && ./node_modules/.bin/vitest run test/node-floor.test.ts test/extraction-manifest.test.ts && cd ../agent && ./node_modules/.bin/vitest run test/deploy-verify.test.ts test/caps.test.ts` (`timeout: 600000`)
Expected: PASS. `agent/test/deploy-verify.test.ts` runs `verify-service.sh` against a stubbed `systemctl` — this task adds a **caller**, not a change to the script, so it must stay green untouched.

Also: `bash -n deploy/deploy.sh` clean.

- [ ] **Step 7: Commit**

```bash
git add server/package.json agent/package.json pwa/package.json \
  server/test/node-floor.test.ts .github/workflows/ci.yml deploy/deploy.sh
git commit -m "build: the node floor is a mechanism, and the server deploy verifies its own service"
```

### Task 2: `~/.ccrc/coord.db` — open, migrate, or refuse loudly

The first artifact in this system whose loss is not free. `spec:62-87` states the rules it lives by and states, in as many words, that the house cache rules do **not** transfer. The code has to say the same thing, in the file, or a reviewer will cite `fleetstate.ts:47` against it — which `spec:66-69` anticipates by name.

**Files:**
- Create: `server/src/coord/schema.ts`, `server/src/coord/db.ts`
- Create: `server/test/coord-db.test.ts`
- Modify: `server/src/config.ts` (`+coordDbPath`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `node:sqlite`'s `DatabaseSync`; `node:fs`'s `mkdirSync` (server-local housekeeping, **never** `FleetIO` — same stance as `fleetstate.ts:26-34`, "Cache lives on THIS box's disk… never proxied through FleetIO/the agent").
- Produces: `openCoordDb(dbPath): DatabaseSync`, `CoordDbUnmigratable`, `tx(db, fn)`, `COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION`, `MIGRATIONS`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `server/test/coord-db.test.ts`:

```ts
// The four properties spec:70-81 names, and one the spec implies: a
// transaction that throws leaves nothing behind.
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import { COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION, CoordDbUnmigratable, openCoordDb, tx } from '../src/coord/db.js';
import { mkTmp } from './tmpHelpers.js';

const dbPathIn = (home: string): string => path.join(home, '.ccrc', 'coord.db');

describe('openCoordDb', () => {
  it('creates the parent directory, the schema and the version, then reopens idempotently', () => {
    const home = mkTmp('ccrc-coord-');
    const p = dbPathIn(home);
    expect(existsSync(path.dirname(p))).toBe(false);   // ~/.ccrc is NOT made by the deploy

    const a = openCoordDb(p);
    expect((a.prepare('PRAGMA user_version').get() as { user_version: number }).user_version)
      .toBe(COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION);
    expect((a.prepare('PRAGMA journal_mode').get() as { journal_mode: string }).journal_mode)
      .toBe('wal');
    // Every table spec:106-117 names, plus the two D-3 adds.
    const names = (a.prepare("SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table'").all() as
      { name: string }[]).map((r) => r.name).sort();
    expect(names).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([
      'coordinator_state', 'mail', 'mail_deliveries', 'mail_rejections',
      'programs', 'run_events', 'runs', 'work_items',
    ]));
    a.prepare("INSERT INTO programs (slug,title,createdAt,state) VALUES ('p','P',1,'active')").run();
    a.close();

    // Reopening at the current version migrates NOTHING and destroys nothing.
    const b = openCoordDb(p);
    expect((b.prepare('SELECT count(*) AS c FROM programs').get() as { c: number }).c).toBe(1);
    b.close();
  });

  it('refuses to start, loudly, on a database it cannot migrate', () => {
    const home = mkTmp('ccrc-coord-');
    const p = dbPathIn(home);
    mkdirSync(path.dirname(p), { recursive: true });
    // A file at a LOWER version whose migration cannot apply: the target table
    // already exists with an incompatible shape, so migration 1 throws.
    const raw = new DatabaseSync(p);
    raw.exec('PRAGMA user_version = 0');
    raw.exec('CREATE TABLE programs (nonsense INTEGER)');
    raw.close();

    expect(() => openCoordDb(p)).toThrow(CoordDbUnmigratable);
    // And the file is UNTOUCHED — the migration ran in a transaction.
    const after = new DatabaseSync(p);
    expect((after.prepare('PRAGMA user_version').get() as { user_version: number }).user_version).toBe(0);
    expect((after.prepare("SELECT count(*) AS c FROM sqlite_master WHERE name='runs'")
      .get() as { c: number }).c).toBe(0);
    after.close();
  });

  it('READS a database from a NEWER build rather than refusing it', () => {
    // spec:78-81 — rollback is real. An older build meeting a higher
    // user_version may refuse to MIGRATE, never to READ.
    const home = mkTmp('ccrc-coord-');
    const p = dbPathIn(home);
    const a = openCoordDb(p);
    a.exec(`PRAGMA user_version = ${COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION + 7}`);
    a.exec('ALTER TABLE runs ADD COLUMN somethingFromTheFuture TEXT');
    a.prepare(`INSERT INTO programs (slug,title,createdAt,state) VALUES ('p','P',1,'active')`).run();
    a.close();

    const b = openCoordDb(p);                       // no throw
    expect((b.prepare('SELECT count(*) AS c FROM programs').get() as { c: number }).c).toBe(1);
    expect((b.prepare('PRAGMA user_version').get() as { user_version: number }).user_version)
      .toBe(COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION + 7);              // never written DOWN
    b.close();
  });

  it('rejects a file that is not a database at all, without eating it', () => {
    const home = mkTmp('ccrc-coord-');
    const p = dbPathIn(home);
    mkdirSync(path.dirname(p), { recursive: true });
    writeFileSync(p, 'this is not a sqlite file\n');
    expect(() => openCoordDb(p)).toThrow();
    expect(existsSync(p)).toBe(true);
  });

  it('tx rolls back everything on a throw, and commits everything otherwise', () => {
    const db = openCoordDb(dbPathIn(mkTmp('ccrc-coord-')));
    tx(db, () => {
      db.prepare("INSERT INTO programs (slug,title,createdAt,state) VALUES ('a','A',1,'active')").run();
    });
    expect(() => tx(db, () => {
      db.prepare("INSERT INTO programs (slug,title,createdAt,state) VALUES ('b','B',1,'active')").run();
      throw new Error('nope');
    })).toThrow('nope');
    const slugs = (db.prepare('SELECT slug FROM programs ORDER BY slug').all() as { slug: string }[])
      .map((r) => r.slug);
    expect(slugs).toEqual(['a']);
    db.close();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the schema**

Create `server/src/coord/schema.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * The v1 DDL, and the ONLY place a migration is written.
 *
 * COLUMNS ARE ADDITIVE-ONLY (spec:77). A column is never repurposed and never
 * dropped; nullable means "an older build lacked it". Every enum column has a
 * designated we-do-not-know member on the READ side (`shared/api.ts`'s
 * `RunState`/`WorkItemState`/`MailKind` each carry one), so a token written by
 * a newer build lands somewhere honest instead of being switched on and
 * rendered as nothing — `PrPhase`'s `'unchecked'` is the precedent
 * (`server/src/registry.ts:133-140`).
 *
 * `run_events` and `mail_rejections` are NOT in spec:106-117's six-table list.
 * They are forced by two of the spec's own sentences: "Every transition records
 * who caused it" (spec:126) is a history, not a column; and "Every rejection is
 * itself recorded" (spec:147) has nowhere to go — a rejected ingress has no
 * `mail` row by construction, since it may carry an unknown recipient or a
 * stale uuid, and writing it into `mail` would put unattributable rows in the
 * table the delivery lane walks. See the plan's deviation D-3.
 *
 * `runs.claimedBy` implements spec:291-292's multi-coordinator non-goal: one
 * coordinator per program, and a second one refuses rather than arbitrating.
 */
export const MIGRATIONS: readonly string[] = [
  // ── 1: user_version 0 -> 1 ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  `
  CREATE TABLE programs (
    slug       TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    title      TEXT NOT NULL,
    createdAt  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    state      TEXT NOT NULL              -- active|paused|done|abandoned
  );

  CREATE TABLE runs (
    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    program       TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES programs(slug),
    wave          INTEGER NOT NULL,
    waveOf        INTEGER,
    project       TEXT NOT NULL,
    sessionId     TEXT,                   -- null until dispatch mints the workspace
    workspace     TEXT,
    branch        TEXT,
    state         TEXT NOT NULL,          -- see RUN_TRANSITIONS
    claimedBy     TEXT,                   -- the one coordinator; a second refuses
    resumed       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,   -- deviation D-1: wave N>=2 resumes
    openedAt      INTEGER NOT NULL,
    dispatchedAt  INTEGER,
    closedAt      INTEGER,
    handoffCommit TEXT,
    prLineage     TEXT                    -- JSON, folded from .prhistory at close
  );
  CREATE INDEX runs_by_state   ON runs(state);
  CREATE INDEX runs_by_program ON runs(program, wave);

  CREATE TABLE run_events (
    id        INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    runId     INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
    at        INTEGER NOT NULL,
    fromState TEXT NOT NULL,
    toState   TEXT NOT NULL,
    causedBy  TEXT NOT NULL,              -- 'coordinator' | 'operator' | <session id>
    detail    TEXT
  );
  CREATE INDEX run_events_by_run ON run_events(runId, at);

  CREATE TABLE work_items (
    id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    runId           INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
    title           TEXT NOT NULL,
    state           TEXT NOT NULL,        -- pending|claimed|done|failed|abandoned
    claimedBy       TEXT,                 -- a session id
    blockedBy       TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]',  -- JSON array of work_item ids
    doneFingerprint TEXT                  -- JSON, as reported then re-measured
  );
  CREATE INDEX work_items_by_run ON work_items(runId);

  CREATE TABLE mail (
    id        INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    at        INTEGER NOT NULL,
    fromId    TEXT NOT NULL,
    fromUuid  TEXT NOT NULL,
    toId      TEXT NOT NULL,              -- 'coordinator' | a session id
    runId     INTEGER REFERENCES runs(id),
    kind      TEXT NOT NULL,              -- finding|question|answer|status|artifact
    subject   TEXT NOT NULL,
    body      TEXT NOT NULL,              -- <= MAIL_BODY_MAX_BYTES
    artifacts TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'  -- JSON array of PATHS, never payloads
  );

  CREATE TABLE mail_deliveries (
    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    mailId        INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES mail(id),
    toId          TEXT NOT NULL,          -- the resolved SESSION id
    state         TEXT NOT NULL,          -- queued|delivered|acked|rejected
    attempts      INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    nextAttemptAt INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    -- The rendered envelope, stored at QUEUE time. spec:174-177 requires a
    -- replay to be verbatim and never re-rendered; a render deferred to each
    -- sweep would silently change with any later edit to envelope.ts.
    envelope      TEXT NOT NULL,
    lastError     TEXT,
    deliveredAt   INTEGER,
    ingestedAt    INTEGER,                -- the UserPromptSubmit edge (spec:178-180)
    ackedAt       INTEGER,
    rejectCode    TEXT
  );
  CREATE INDEX mail_deliveries_due ON mail_deliveries(state, nextAttemptAt);

  CREATE TABLE mail_rejections (
    id       INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    at       INTEGER NOT NULL,
    code     TEXT NOT NULL,
    fromId   TEXT, fromUuid TEXT, toId TEXT, runId INTEGER, kind TEXT, subject TEXT,
    detail   TEXT
  );

  CREATE TABLE coordinator_state (
    id                   INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id = 1),
    maxConcurrentWorkers INTEGER NOT NULL,
    maxSessionsPerDay    INTEGER NOT NULL,
    updatedAt            INTEGER NOT NULL
  );
  INSERT INTO coordinator_state (id, maxConcurrentWorkers, maxSessionsPerDay, updatedAt)
    VALUES (1, 3, 12, 0);
  `,
];

/** The version this build writes. `MIGRATIONS.length` and nothing else: a
 *  hand-maintained constant beside a growing array is a pair that goes out of
 *  step, and the failure is silent (a migration that never runs). */
export const COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION = MIGRATIONS.length;
```

**Note on the caps counters.** `coordinator_state` holds the two **limits**. The two **counts** are queries — running runs, and dispatches in a rolling 24 h from `runs.dispatchedAt` — not stored columns. `spec:201` says "rows in `coordinator_state`"; a stored counter would be a second copy of a fact the `runs` table already holds, and the one that drifts is always the copy. Say this in the store.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the opener**

Create `server/src/coord/db.ts`:

```ts
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';
import { mkdirSync } from 'node:fs';
import { homedir } from 'node:os';
import path from 'node:path';
import { COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION, MIGRATIONS } from './schema.js';

export { COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION } from './schema.js';

/** Thrown when the database on disk cannot be brought to this build's schema.
 *  Deliberately NOT caught anywhere: `index.ts` lets it kill the process, and
 *  `deploy.sh`'s `verify-service.sh ccrc.service` turns that into a failed
 *  deploy with the journal tail attached. */
export class CoordDbUnmigratable extends Error {}

/** `~/.ccrc/coord.db` on the SERVER box — the same directory `push-subs.json`
 *  (`index.ts:23`), `notify-log.json` (`index.ts:30`) and `state-cache.json`
 *  (`fleetstate.ts:32`) already live in, and the same stance: this is local-box
 *  housekeeping, never proxied through FleetIO. The fleet host has no copy and
 *  cannot read this file; every cross-check against a `.hold`/`.prhistory`/
 *  registry fact is a FleetIO read in the other direction. */
export function defaultCoordDbPath(home: string = homedir()): string {
  return path.join(home, '.ccrc', 'coord.db');
}

/**
 * Open the coordination database, migrating it forward if needed, or refuse to
 * start.
 *
 * THE HOUSE CACHE RULES DO NOT APPLY HERE, and this paragraph is why — because
 * without it a reviewer will cite them, correctly, against this file:
 *
 *  - `fleetstate.ts:47-55` says "the READ is the version negotiation", and that
 *    a cache which cannot be revived is treated as ABSENT. `offline.ts:11-16`
 *    stays at v1 forever for the same reason. Both govern artefacts whose loss
 *    is FREE: the degraded-mode snapshot is re-assembled live, the offline
 *    snapshot is replaced by the next frame.
 *  - This is the first artefact in ccrc whose loss is NOT free. Collapsing an
 *    unreadable database to "empty" would answer "that program never happened"
 *    — the exact shape of `ccd`'s SIXTEENTH FORGERY, a manifest that lies
 *    pristine (`ccd/ccd:2018-2035`).
 *  - And the reason those files could not do better is absent here: they had
 *    NOWHERE to put a version key. This file has `PRAGMA user_version`, which
 *    IS the key, so the negotiation is a real one and does not have to be
 *    smuggled into the read.
 *
 * The four rules, from spec:70-81:
 *   1. Forward-only, idempotent, at open, EACH IN A TRANSACTION.
 *   2. Cannot migrate -> refuse to start LOUDLY. Never start empty.
 *   3. A HIGHER user_version is not fatal: a rollback (`~/ccrc-backups/`, and
 *      `shared/api.ts:566-575` on why rollback is a real scenario) must be able
 *      to READ. It may only refuse to MIGRATE. `user_version` is never written
 *      downward, and unknown columns are ignored because every read names its
 *      columns explicitly — `SELECT *` is banned in this directory.
 *   4. The markdown ledger stays the disaster-recovery ground truth
 *      (`docs/superpowers/programs/<slug>.md` + the registry + `.prhistory`);
 *      `coord-store.test.ts`'s reconstruction drill proves the path.
 */
export function openCoordDb(dbPath: string): DatabaseSync {
  // `~/.ccrc` is not created by the deploy except incidentally (`deploy.sh:34`
  // runs `mkdir -p ~/.ccrc` only inside `ship_env`), so every writer makes its
  // own parent — `fleetstate.ts:40`, `push.ts:50`, `notifylog.ts:85`.
  mkdirSync(path.dirname(dbPath), { recursive: true });
  const db = new DatabaseSync(dbPath);
  // WAL first: a reader must never be blocked by the sweep's write, and the
  // journal mode is a property of the FILE, so it survives every later open.
  db.exec('PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL');
  db.exec('PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON');
  db.exec('PRAGMA busy_timeout = 5000');

  const current = (db.prepare('PRAGMA user_version').get() as { user_version: number }).user_version;
  if (current > COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION) {
    // Rule 3. Loud, because it is worth knowing; not fatal, because refusing
    // here would make a rollback unable to read the history it rolled back to.
    console.warn(
      `ccrc-server: coord.db is at schema ${current}, this build knows ${COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION} — ` +
      'reading it as-is and migrating nothing (a rollback may only refuse to migrate, never to read)',
    );
    return db;
  }

  for (let v = current; v < COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION; v++) {
    try {
      tx(db, () => {
        db.exec(MIGRATIONS[v]!);
        // Inside the same transaction as the DDL it describes: SQLite's DDL is
        // transactional, so a crash between the two would otherwise leave a
        // migrated schema claiming to be unmigrated and re-run it on next boot.
        db.exec(`PRAGMA user_version = ${v + 1}`);
      });
    } catch (err) {
      db.close();
      throw new CoordDbUnmigratable(
        `ccrc-server: ${dbPath} is at schema ${v} and migration ${v + 1} failed: ` +
        `${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}. REFUSING TO START. ` +
        'Nothing was changed (the migration ran in a transaction). Restore from ~/ccrc-backups/ ' +
        'or move the file aside — starting empty would erase a program\'s history.',
      );
    }
  }
  return db;
}

/**
 * Every write in a transaction (spec:60). `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, not the default
 * DEFERRED: under WAL a deferred transaction takes its write lock at the first
 * write, so a busy database fails in the MIDDLE of a multi-statement unit
 * instead of at its start.
 *
 * SYNCHRONOUS by construction — `DatabaseSync` has no async surface — and that
 * is a property worth naming rather than apologising for: a whole transaction
 * runs without yielding the event loop, so no route, sweep or socket can
 * interleave inside one. `fn` must therefore never await, and nothing in this
 * directory does.
 */
export function tx<T>(db: DatabaseSync, fn: () => T): T {
  db.exec('BEGIN IMMEDIATE');
  try {
    const out = fn();
    db.exec('COMMIT');
    return out;
  } catch (err) {
    // A rollback that itself throws must not replace the real error: the caller
    // needs to know what failed, not that the recovery also did.
    try { db.exec('ROLLBACK'); } catch { /* the transaction is already gone */ }
    throw err;
  }
}
```
