- [ ] **Step 4: Add the constants and the state**

`server/src/watch.ts`, beside the other lane constants (`:24-100`):

```ts
/** The SEVENTH lane. (Sixth is naming, fifth is hook-state sweeping.) spec:159
 *  fixes the cadence; the rest of this block is the gate's arithmetic.
 *
 *  Ten seconds is not how fast mail should arrive — it is how often the lane
 *  is allowed to ASK. Actual delivery is bounded below by `MAIL_QUIET_MS`,
 *  which is the point: mail lands at a turn boundary, not mid-thought. */
const MAIL_SWEEP_MS = 10_000;

/** How long a session must have been idle before it is interruptible. ccd's
 *  own `COMPACT_QUIET` (`ccd/ccd:45`), taken rather than re-derived: this is
 *  the same judgement about the same panes, and two numbers for one policy is
 *  two numbers to get out of step. Measured from `statusUpdatedAt`, which
 *  Claude Code ticks on every busy<->idle transition (`ccd/ccd:6697-6698`). */
const MAIL_QUIET_MS = 60_000;

/** No session gets two injections inside this window, however much mail is
 *  queued for it. A fan-out of six findings arriving as six prompts in ninety
 *  seconds is a denial of service dressed as coordination. */
const MAIL_COOLDOWN_MS = 120_000;

/** How long an UNACKED delivery waits before it is replayed. Dated from the
 *  `UserPromptSubmit` edge when there is one, from `deliveredAt` otherwise —
 *  the edge proves the turn started, so the recipient is thinking, not
 *  ignoring. */
const MAIL_REPLAY_MS = 600_000;

/** The attempt budget, and the spacing between attempts. Doubling from 30 s to
 *  the same 15-minute ceiling `PR_BACKOFF_MAX_MS` already uses — one ceiling
 *  for "this keeps not working" across the whole watcher. At the cap the
 *  delivery parks as `rejected('undeliverable')` and THE MAIL ROW IS UNTOUCHED
 *  (spec:170-172): the record of what was said survives the failure to say it. */
const MAIL_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 6;
const MAIL_BACKOFF_BASE_MS = 30_000;
const MAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_MS = PR_BACKOFF_MAX_MS;

/** The fleet kill-switch, `$REG/mail-disabled` — ccd's `-disabled` family
 *  (`ccd/ccd:20-22`, `_lane_enabled` at `:53`), which the operator already
 *  knows how to use: `touch` to stop, `rm` to resume. Read by LISTING the
 *  registry directory, never by reading the file: `FleetIO.readFile` maps
 *  every failure to null (`io.ts:41-43`), so a read would make an unreadable
 *  kill-switch look like an absent one — fail-OPEN on the one control whose
 *  entire job is to stop injection. `limits.ts:134-142` already filters
 *  unknown `<name>-disabled` markers out of `/api/accounts`, so this name
 *  cannot fabricate an account row there. */
const MAIL_DISABLED_MARKER = 'mail-disabled';
```

and the fields:

```ts
  private lastMailSweep = 0;
  /** Per session: when this lane last injected. IN MEMORY BY DESIGN, and the
   *  direction of the failure is why: a restart forgets the cooldown and may
   *  deliver one message sooner than it should have. Persisting it would buy
   *  a politeness guarantee across a restart at the price of a column and a
   *  purge — the same trade `attemptedRenames` already declined (`watch.ts:172-181`).
   *  The ATTEMPT budget, which protects a session from a loop rather than from
   *  a moment, IS durable: it is a column on the delivery. */
  private mailCooldown = new Map<string, number>();
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Dispatch it from the tick**

`server/src/watch.ts`, in `tick()` beside `sweepNames` (`:352-358`):

```ts
    // NEVER awaited, same reasoning as sweepNames immediately above: this one
    // joins the per-session KeyedQueue AND calls sendPrompt, whose worst case
    // is ~4.3 s of sleeps per message plus one round trip per line
    // (`inject/send.ts:26-36,115,126`). Awaiting it would put the dialog
    // detector and the busy->idle push behind a mail delivery.
    void this.sweepMail().catch(() => { /* one bad sweep must not kill the poll */ });
```

**The priming rule extends here** (`spec:256-258`): `sweepMail` returns early while `!this.primed`, so a server restart does not inject into every idle session in the fleet at once. That is one line and it is the difference between a restart and an incident.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the sweep**

```ts
  /**
   * Deliver queued mail, one message per eligible session per sweep.
   *
   * SIX CONJUNCTS, in this order, cheapest first — and the order is the design,
   * because every rung below the first is a read that crosses the agent WS in
   * remote mode:
   *
   *   0. the lane is primed (a restart delivers no storm — spec:256-258);
   *   1. `$REG/mail-disabled` is absent, by DIRECTORY LISTING, fail-shut;
   *   2. this session is off its per-session cooldown;
   *   3. tmux has the session;
   *   4. the hookstate is fresh AND says `done` AND carries no ask — `done` is
   *      the hook's idle, and `readHookState` has already applied the freshness
   *      and uuid gates (`hookstate.ts:149-154`), so a stale or foreign file
   *      reads as null and null is NOT idle;
   *   5. the live status file says AFFIRMATIVELY idle and `statusUpdatedAt` is
   *      at least `MAIL_QUIET_MS` old. Affirmatively, because `liveStatus`
   *      answers `'idle'` for a missing pid, a missing config dir and an
   *      unreadable file (`fleet.ts:118-131`) — `archiveSafety`'s rule
   *      (`watch.ts:731-736`, "MUST NOT collapse `unknown` to idle") applies
   *      here for the same reason: this ends in a keystroke.
   *
   * ONLY THEN `sendPrompt`, unchanged, with its whole proof discipline —
   * echo verified, `draft-present` refused, `dialog-open` refused — inside the
   * session's own `KeyedQueue` slot. NOTHING HERE TEACHES IT TO RETRY: the
   * two-Enter budget and `submitEnter`'s one-Enter doctrine
   * (`inject/send.ts:456-460`) are load-bearing, and the escalation for a stuck
   * box is the human.
   *
   * `replaceDraft` IS NEVER PASSED. A half-typed human message outranks every
   * agent-to-agent finding in this system; `draft-present` is a back-off, and
   * the mail is still there in two minutes.
   *
   * WHAT THIS CANNOT SEE, stated because it bounds the guarantee: Claude Code
   * silently QUEUES a prompt sent mid-turn and renders the hint in a dim span
   * that `draftOf` strips (`inject/send.ts:61`, pinned against a live capture
   * at `send.test.ts:642`). So "the box reads empty" is not "nothing is
   * pending", and the gate above is what keeps the lane away from a busy
   * session in the first place — not the send path, which would happily
   * succeed.
   */
  async sweepMail(): Promise<void> { … }
```

The body, in one pass:

```ts
    if (!this.primed) return;
    const store = this.deps.coord;
    if (!store) return;
    const now = Date.now();
    if (this.lastMailSweep !== 0 && now - this.lastMailSweep < MAIL_SWEEP_MS) return;
    this.lastMailSweep = now;

    // Fail-shut: a registry we cannot list is a kill-switch we cannot read.
    const listing = await this.deps.io.readdir(this.deps.cfg.registryDir);
    if (listing === null || listing.includes(MAIL_DISABLED_MARKER)) return;

    const due = store.dueDeliveries(now);
    if (due.length === 0) return;
    const records = await readRegistry(this.deps.io, this.deps.cfg);
    const seen = new Set<string>();          // one message per session per sweep
    for (const d of due) {
      if (seen.has(d.toId)) continue;
      const last = this.mailCooldown.get(d.toId) ?? 0;
      if (now - last < MAIL_COOLDOWN_MS) continue;
      const rec = records.find((r) => r.id === d.toId);
      if (!rec) continue;                    // a recipient that went away: the row waits
      if (!(await this.deps.tmux.hasSession(d.toId))) continue;
      const hs = await readHookState(this.deps.io, this.deps.cfg.registryDir, d.toId, rec.uuid, now);
      if (hs === null || hs.state !== 'done' || hs.ask !== null) continue;
      const pid = await this.deps.tmux.panePid(d.toId);
      const cfgDir = this.deps.cfg.wrappers[rec.wrapper];
      if (!pid || !cfgDir) continue;
      const live = await readLiveState(this.deps.io, cfgDir, pid);
      if (!live || liveSessionStatus(live.status) !== 'idle') continue;
      if (live.statusUpdatedAt === null || now - live.statusUpdatedAt < MAIL_QUIET_MS) continue;

      seen.add(d.toId);
      // The stored envelope, byte for byte. `renderEnvelope` is not called here
      // and must never be: spec:176-177's "verbatim, never re-rendered".
      const res = await sendPrompt({ tmux: this.deps.tmux, queue: this.deps.queue }, d.toId, d.envelope);
      if (res.ok) {
        this.mailCooldown.set(d.toId, now);
        store.markDelivered(d.id, now);
        continue;
      }
      // `enter-ignored` is terminal HERE and nowhere else: the text is sitting
      // in the box, `submitEnter`'s doctrine forbids a blind third Enter, and
      // the rescue is a human looking at the pane. Re-injecting would type the
      // whole envelope a second time UNDER the first one.
      if (res.error === 'enter-ignored') {
        store.rejectDelivery(d.id, 'undeliverable', res.error);
        continue;
      }
      const attempts = d.attempts + 1;
      if (attempts >= MAIL_MAX_ATTEMPTS) {
        store.rejectDelivery(d.id, 'undeliverable', res.error);
        continue;
      }
      const step = Math.min(MAIL_BACKOFF_BASE_MS * 2 ** (attempts - 1), MAIL_BACKOFF_MAX_MS);
      store.backOff(d.id, res.error, now + step);
    }
```

And the `UserPromptSubmit` edge, folded into the same hookstate read a few lines above — a delivered-but-unacked row whose recipient's hookstate carries `event === 'UserPromptSubmit'` with `updatedAt > deliveredAt` gets `markIngested(d.id, hs.updatedAt)`, which re-dates its replay clock. `dueDeliveries` is what encodes replay: a `delivered` row is due again at `(ingestedAt ?? deliveredAt) + MAIL_REPLAY_MS`, an `acked` row is never due, and a `queued` row is due at `nextAttemptAt`.

**Restart semantics** (`spec:256-258`): nothing is held in memory except the cooldown, so a restart resumes from the table, and every in-flight delivery re-runs the whole gate — including a fresh `readHookState` — before it re-injects. That is what "in-flight deliveries re-verify against hookstate before re-injecting" means, and it is true here by construction rather than by a special case.

### Task 9: the coordinator API — open, dispatch under caps, close

`spec:192-198`: *"It acts through the server's HTTP API, not raw ccd… One chokepoint means caps are **enforced**, every act is **recorded** on the run, and the PWA sees everything. Raw ccd remains physically possible; the skill's contract plus the single recorded chokepoint is the honest boundary, and the spec names it as such."*

**Files:**
- Modify: `server/src/coord/routes.ts`
- Create: `server/test/run-routes.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `CCD_ARGV.wsAdd`/`ensure`/`wsHold`/`wsRelease`/`wsArchive` (`ccdargv.ts:63,67,72,77,78`) — **all five already minted, all five already granted** (`agent/src/whitelist.ts:310-336`); `verbSupported`; `readRegistry`; `readPrHistory` (Task 4); `verifyDone` (Task 5); `CoordStore` (Task 3).
- Produces: `POST /api/runs`, `POST /api/runs/:id/dispatch`, `POST /api/runs/:id/close`, `GET /api/runs`.

**Zero new ccd verbs, spelled out.** The three fleet acts and what each rides:

| act | argv | already granted? | gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| create a workspace for wave 1 | `CCD_ARGV.wsAdd(project)` | yes (`['ws-add']`) | `UNGATED_BY_DECISION` (`verb-gate.test.ts:58-60`) |
| bring wave N ≥ 2's pane back | `CCD_ARGV.ensure(id)` | yes (`['ensure']`) | `UNGATED_BY_DECISION` |
| claim / re-claim the workspace | `CCD_ARGV.wsHold(id, reason)` | yes (`['ws-hold','--session']`) | needs `verbSupported` |
| release on the final wave | `CCD_ARGV.wsRelease(id)` | yes (`['ws-release','--session']`) | needs `verbSupported` |
| abandon-with-archive only | `CCD_ARGV.wsArchive(id)` | yes (`['ws-archive','--session']`) | needs `verbSupported` |

**`POST /api/runs`** — open. Body `{program, title, project, wave, waveOf, claimedBy, sessionId?}`.

- Inserts/updates the `programs` row and inserts the `runs` row in `planned` (Task 3's `openRun`, which refuses a second coordinator).
- When `sessionId` names an existing workspace, places the hold immediately: `CCD_ARGV.wsHold(sessionId, 'program:<slug> wave:<n>/<of>')`. That reason string is the standing convention (`spec:120-123`) and it is **display-only, never parsed** — `registry.ts:26-46` insists on it, and the run row has its own `program`/`wave` columns precisely so nobody has to.
- **The ledger expectation is documented and unenforced**, in the route's own docstring and echoed in the response as `ledgerPath: 'docs/superpowers/programs/<slug>.md'`:

```ts
  /**
   * Open a run.
   *
   * THE LEDGER IS NOT WRITTEN HERE, AND IT IS NOT READ HERE. Opening a run
   * expects the coordinator to have committed `docs/superpowers/programs/
   * <slug>.md` in the project's own repo — that is what a fresh session, and a
   * human reviewing a handoff, read to learn where the program stands
   * (`docs/superpowers/programs/TEMPLATE.md:5-9`). The server cannot verify it
   * and deliberately does not try: the ledger is prose for humans and PARSED BY
   * NOTHING (spec:93-95, spec:246-248). What it CAN do is name the path in the
   * response, so a coordinator that forgot has been told once, in the place it
   * would notice.
   *
   * The two records are joined by the program slug and neither is derived from
   * the other. If the database is lost, the ledger plus the registry plus
   * `.prhistory` are what rebuild the runs (spec:82-85, and
   * `CoordStore.reconstruct` with its drill).
   */
```

**`POST /api/runs/:id/dispatch`** — body `{brief: string}`. In order, and again the order is the design:

1. **Pause first, before anything is counted or spawned.** `$REG/coordinator-paused` via `io.readdir(cfg.registryDir)` — a listing, fail-shut, same as the mail kill-switch and for the same reason. Present, or unlistable → `409 {refused:'paused'}`. *"no verb, no route, no way for the coordinator to unpause itself"* (`spec:201-205`) — so there is deliberately no `POST /api/coordinator/resume` in this plan, and adding one later is a decision, not a convenience.
2. **Caps**, from `store.caps()` against `store.capsUsage(now)`: `running >= maxConcurrentWorkers` → `409 {refused:'cap-concurrency', limit, running}`; `dispatchedIn24h >= maxSessionsPerDay` → `409 {refused:'cap-daily', limit, used}`. The refusal carries the numbers, because a cap that refuses without saying what it is is indistinguishable from a bug.
3. **Spawn or resume.** No `sessionId` on the run → `CCD_ARGV.wsAdd(project)`; otherwise → `CCD_ARGV.ensure(sessionId)` (deviation **D-1**).
4. **Learn the new id by REGISTRY DIFF, not by parsing ccd's sentence.** `cmd_ws_add` echoes `workspace <id> on <wrapper> — <path> (branch …)` (`ccd/ccd:1116`), and the server could scrape it — but a prose line is a contract nobody wrote down, and this repo has already paid for one of those. Read the registry before and after; exactly one new `workspace !== null` row for this project is the run's session. Zero or more than one → `409 {refused:'ambiguous-dispatch'}` with the run left in `planned` and **no hold placed**: the operator resolves it, and nothing has been claimed on a guess.
5. **Hold**, `CCD_ARGV.wsHold(id, 'program:<slug> wave:<n>/<of>')`, behind `verbSupported`.
6. `store.markDispatched(...)` + `store.advance(id, 'dispatched', 'coordinator')` — one call, so the `run_events` row happens.
7. **Queue the brief as mail** (kind `status`, subject `wave-brief`, `toId` = the worker) rather than injecting it. A fresh pane is `working` for its first seconds and the delivery lane's gate is exactly the thing that knows when it is not; a route that injected directly would be a second injection path with none of the discipline the first one has.

**`POST /api/runs/:id/close`** — body `{fingerprint: DoneClaim, final: boolean, state?: 'done' | 'failed', archive?: boolean}`.

1. `verifyDone` (Task 5). On any verdict but `ok`: **the run state is unchanged**, the rejection is recorded, a `status` mail carrying the code and detail is queued back to the worker (`spec:129-131`, "mailed back"), and the route answers `409 {ok:false, error:<code>, detail}`.
2. `readPrHistory` (Task 4). `{ok:false,error:'unreadable'}` → `409 {refused:'prhistory-unreadable'}` and **nothing closes**: a run record that says "retired no PRs" because a file could not be read is the forgery `ccd/ccd:2018-2035` names. `store.foldPrLineage(id, entries)` otherwise.
3. `advance(id, 'closing', 'coordinator')` then `advance(id, state ?? 'done', 'coordinator')`, recording `handoffCommit`.
4. **The fleet act, and it is a RELEASE** (deviation **D-5**):
   - `final: true` → `CCD_ARGV.wsRelease(id)`. The workspace is now unheld and merged, so `FleetWatcher.archiveMerged` archives it on its own next sweep, through `archiveSafety` — *"a coordinator that archives autonomously runs through this gate, not around it."*
   - `final: false` → `CCD_ARGV.wsHold(id, 'program:<slug> wave:<n+1>/<of>')`, which is the hold-reason update Build 2.5's step 5 does by hand.
   - `state: 'failed'` with `archive: true` → `CCD_ARGV.wsArchive(id)` behind `verbSupported`, mirroring `POST /api/sessions/:id/archive` (`server.ts:599-614`) including its 501. This is the only `wsArchive` call in the coordination lane.

**`GET /api/runs?closed=1`** — `{runs: RunSummary[]}` from `store.runs({includeClosed})`. Cold start and the archive of finished runs (`spec:225-227`).

### Task 10: the wire — a `runs` frame, two notify kinds, the durable feed, and an old client that stays safe

**Orchestrator-added scope (PR J interface 5, reconciled):** the feed must
survive a deploy, and PR J reads it as `GET /api/feed?limit=<n>` →
`{ events: NotifyEvent[] }`, oldest-first. Mechanism: a `feed_events` table
in `coord.db` (columns mirroring `NotifyEvent` + the durable `{epoch,seq}`
pair), written at the single `pushOne` record point beside
`notifyLog.record` — ALL kinds, not just the new two, so scrollback is
complete — with retention capped (keep the newest 2,000; prune in the same
transaction). The route reads oldest-first with the limit clamped. The
in-memory ring and the `{epoch,seq}` watermark file keep their exact
current roles (catch-up correctness); the table is the archive behind them.
Test: record → restart the store (fresh NotifyLog, same coord.db) →
`GET /api/feed` still answers the events; the ring answers `[]`.

Everything here is additive. `shared/api.ts:560-566` states the rule this relies on: *"the stale-client window is new-writer/old-reader ONE-WAY — a newer server only ever ADDS frame fields or types, and an already-deployed PWA already drops an unknown fleet frame type silently."* So **no `FLEET_PROTO` bump**. The dangerous half is not the frame — it is `NotifyEvent.kind`, a closed three-member union with **no client-side validator** today (`pwa/src/lib/api.ts` does a bare `getJson<CatchUp>`), which means a `mail` event reaching an old client is typed as one of three things it is not.

**PR J renders all of this. PR I only makes it safe to send.**

**Files:**
- Modify: `shared/api.ts` (`NotifyEvent` `:1310-1314`; `+reviveNotifyEvent`)
- Modify: `server/src/watch.ts` (`pushOne` `:405-411`), `server/src/server.ts` (`/ws/fleet` `:236-254`), `server/src/coord/routes.ts`
- Modify: `pwa/src/stores/fleet.ts`, `pwa/src/lib/notifymark.ts`
- Modify: `server/test/fleetws.test.ts`, `server/test/push-copy.test.ts`; PWA store test

- [ ] **Step 1: Widen the kind, and give it a reviver**

`shared/api.ts:1310-1314`:

```ts
export interface NotifyEvent {
  seq: number; at: number;
  /** `mail` and `run` are Build 7's. `unknown` is the CLIENT-SIDE degradation
   *  member and the server never records it: it is what a kind from a newer
   *  server becomes on an older client, via `reviveNotifyEvent`. Without it
   *  this union was closed and unvalidated — a bare `getJson<CatchUp>`
   *  (`pwa/src/lib/api.ts`) hands a browser's JSON straight to a renderer that
   *  switches on three members, so a fourth arrived typed as one of the three
   *  it is not. */
  kind: 'ask' | 'done' | 'merged' | 'mail' | 'run' | 'unknown';
  sessionId: string; title: string; body: string;
}

/**
 * One catch-up event, revived into today's shape — the same discipline
 * `reviveFleetSession` states once at :585-597 and for the same reason:
 *  - a token from a newer build, where the type has a designated "we do not
 *    know" member, becomes that member;
 *  - a field of the wrong type, or a missing non-nullable one, REJECTS the
 *    whole event (null), because a half-revived notification is a badge for
 *    something that may not have happened.
 *
 * Rejection here collapses to `null` and the caller DROPS the event, which is
 * the same answer the feed already gives for a resync (`notifymark.ts:67-70`):
 * nothing surfaced retroactively, ever, on doubt.
 */
export function reviveNotifyEvent(raw: unknown): NotifyEvent | null { … }
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Let `pushOne` carry a tag and record through presence**

`server/src/watch.ts:405-411`. Two additions, and the docstring above it (`:384-404`) has to be **amended, not appended to** — it currently states the presence rule as absolute, and after this it is not:

```ts
  private pushOne(e: {
    kind: NotifyEvent['kind']; sessionId: string; project: string;
    title: string; body: string; actions?: PushPayload['actions'];
    /** Overrides the default `${kind}-${sessionId}` collapse key. Mail MUST
     *  pass one: two different messages about one session must not replace
     *  each other in the tray (spec:236-237), which is exactly what the
     *  default key does — and does correctly for `ask`/`done`/`merged`, where
     *  the newest statement about a session supersedes the last. */
    tag?: string;
    /** Record even when the operator is looking at this session. THE PRESENCE
     *  GATE STILL SUPPRESSES THE PUSH; only the RECORD is exempt. spec:238-240:
     *  agent-to-agent mail is a record, not a "needs your eyes" ping — gating
     *  the record on presence would mean the operator watching a session
     *  ERASES the log of a message they never saw. */
    recordAlways?: boolean;
  }, projects: Set<string>): void {
    const visible = this.deps.presence?.isVisible(e.sessionId) === true;
    if (visible && !e.recordAlways) return;
    const title = projects.size > 1 ? `${e.title} · ${e.project}` : e.title;
    this.deps.notifyLog?.record({ kind: e.kind, sessionId: e.sessionId, title, body: e.body });
    void this.deps.notifyLog?.flush();
    if (visible) return;                       // recorded, not pushed
    void this.deps.push?.notify({
      title, body: e.body, sessionId: e.sessionId,
      tag: e.tag ?? `${e.kind}-${e.sessionId}`,
      ...(e.actions ? { actions: e.actions } : {}),
    });
  }
```

The copy, per `spec:243-244` (*"`✉ finding › <ws>` etc., project-decorated only when >1 project active, actionless in v1"*):

- mail: `title: '✉ ' + kind + ' › ' + (workspace ?? sessionId)`, `body: subject`, `tag: 'mail-' + sessionId + '-' + mailId`, `recordAlways: true`, **no `actions`** — a push action needs a new arm in `push-sw.js:106-109`'s one grammar and server-side minting, and v1 does not have one.
- run: `title: '▸ ' + state + ' › ' + (workspace ?? project)`, `body: 'program:' + slug + ' wave ' + n + '/' + of`, `tag: 'run-' + runId + '-' + state`, `recordAlways: true`. Same argument: a run transition is a fact about a program, and the operator watching one pane must not erase it.

The mail push fires from the delivery lane at **queue** time (the message exists and is a record then), not at injection — otherwise a message that never becomes deliverable is a fact nothing recorded.

- [ ] **Step 3: Send the frame**

`server/src/server.ts:236-254`, inside the `/ws/fleet` handler, alongside `onFleet`/`onNotice`:

```ts
    const onRuns = (runs: RunSummary[]) =>
      socket.send(JSON.stringify({ type: 'runs', runs } satisfies FleetMsg));
    // Cold start for THIS socket, same reasoning as the initial `fleet` push:
    // the runs frame is only emitted on change, so a client connecting into a
    // quiet fleet would otherwise see no runs until one moved.
    if (deps.coord) onRuns(deps.coord.runs());
    bus.on('runs', onRuns);
```
with `bus.off('runs', onRuns)` in the close handler, and a `runs` overload on `Bus` (`server/src/bus.ts:13-33` — three overloads per method, one more each).

The emitter is a single private `FleetWatcher.emitRuns()`, called after every state change the server itself causes, with the **byte-equality guard the fleet snapshot already uses** (`watch.ts:379-382`): stringify, compare to `lastRunsJson`, return when identical. Runs change on human-and-agent timescales; re-broadcasting an unchanged list every 2 s would be new noise on a socket that spent real effort not having any.

- [ ] **Step 4: Make the old-client story real, client-side**

`pwa/src/stores/fleet.ts` — the `asFleetMsg` arm (`:54-73`), the store slice, and the `onMessage` branch (`:137-159`):

```ts
  if (t === 'runs' && Array.isArray((m as { runs?: unknown }).runs)) {
    return m as FleetMsg;
  }
```
```ts
  /** Build 7's run board reads this. PR I fills it; PR J renders it. Shape-
   *  validated only at the frame level (an array of runs), the same depth
   *  `fleet` is: `reviveFleetSession`-grade revival for runs is PR J's problem
   *  when it has a renderer to protect. */
  runs: RunSummary[];
```

and in `applyCatchUp` (`pwa/src/lib/notifymark.ts:67-70`):

```ts
export function applyCatchUp(r: CatchUp): CatchUp['events'] {
  saveMark({ epoch: r.epoch, seq: r.seq });
  if (r.resync) return [];
  // Every event revived, never cast. A kind this build does not know becomes
  // `unknown` rather than arriving typed as one of the ones it does — the
  // degradation branch spec:234-236 requires, and the reason `NotifyEvent.kind`
  // grew an `unknown` member at all. An event that fails revival is DROPPED:
  // the mark has already advanced, and a fabricated badge is worse than a
  // missed one (the same ruling as the resync arm above).
  return r.events.map(reviveNotifyEvent).filter((e): e is NotifyEvent => e !== null);
}
```

PWA: `asFleetMsg` accepts a well-formed `runs` frame and rejects `{type:'runs'}` with a non-array; **an unknown frame type is still dropped silently** (the property old clients depend on — assert it explicitly so nobody "helpfully" makes the parser throw); `applyCatchUp` turns `kind:'mail'` into `mail`, turns `kind:'something-new'` into `unknown`, and drops an event with a numeric `title`.

| `advance`: write the state without the `run_events` insert | `records who caused every transition` |
| `run()`: `state as RunState` instead of `isRunState(...) ? … : 'unknown'` | `reads a state token this build does not know` |
| `advance()`: `from = row.state as RunState` instead of the `isRunState` guard (amended — see below, D-27) | `refuses a transition from a state token this build does not know, rather than throwing` |
| `programs()`: `state: r.state as ProgramState` instead of the `isProgramState` guard (amended — see below, D-27) | `reads a programs.state token this build does not know as unknown` |
| `delivery()`: `state: row.state as MailDeliveryState` instead of the `isMailDeliveryState` guard (amended — see below, D-27) | `reads a mail_deliveries.state token this build does not know as unknown` |
| `capsUsage`: `>` → `>=` on the 24h window; and count calendar-day instead | (amended — see below, D-59) `rolling 24h, not a calendar day` |
| `readBranchTip`: try `packed-refs` before the loose ref | `prefers the LOOSE ref` |
| `readBranchTip`: drop the `..` guard (amended — see below, D-21) | the two decoy-backed traversal cases |
| `readBranchTip`: loose ref falls through to `packed-refs` when not a SHA (D-19) | a symref loose ref must not read the stale packed entry |
| `readBranchTip`: drop the `io.stat` presence check on the null-read arm (amended — see below, D-31) | a `chmod 000`/`EISDIR` loose ref must not read the stale packed entry either |
| `readBranchTip`: project guard admits `.`/`..` (D-20) | a run opened with `project: '..'` |
| `readBranchTip`: drop the `packed-refs` sha/ref-name column check (D-22) | a shifted-column line yields a WRONG sha, not `null` |
| `verifyDone`: resolve `run.branch` instead of the live registry (D-17) | the renamed-branch case |
| `verifyDone`: skip the `isPrPhase`/`typeof prNumber` validation (D-18) | an omitted/out-of-vocabulary `prPhase`, a non-number `prNumber` |
| `verifyDone`: `measured === 'unknown'` → treat as `ok` (amended — see below, D-22) | the `merge-unproven` row |
| `verifyDone`: drop the `prNumber` mismatch arm (D-22) | a stale claim naming an earlier bound PR number |
| `verifyDone`: `if (!verbSupported(...))` never taken (D-22) | the `ccdVerbs: []` case |
| `verifyDone`: drop the "no full line" refusal (D-22) | `ccd pr-state` exits 0 with no full line for this session |
| `prVerdict`: drop the `claimed === 'merged' && measured !== 'merged'` arm (D-22) | a false "merged" claim against a branch still open |
| `verifyDone`: drop the `handoffCommit !== branchTip` conjunct | the correspondence case |
| `readPrHistory`: unreadable → `{ok:true, entries:[]}` | `REFUSES when the file is present and unreadable` |
| `readPrHistory`: `listing === null` guard dropped (amended — see below, D-16) | directory-absent / directory-`chmod 000` cases |
| `readPrHistory`: drop the second-read confirmation on the null+listed arm (amended — see below, D-14) | the read-then-list race case |
| `readPrHistory`: `entries.push` maps `branch`/`phase`/`recordedAt` to constants (amended — see below, D-28) | `reads JSONL in order, dropping nothing it can parse` |
| `readPrHistory`: drop `typeof o['pr'] !== 'number'` (amended — see below, D-29) | `drops a line whose pr is the wrong type` |
| `readPrHistory`: drop `!Number.isInteger(o['pr'])` (amended — see below, D-29) | `drops a line whose pr is a number but not an integer` |
| `readPrHistory`: drop `typeof o['branch'] !== 'string'` (amended — see below, D-29) | `drops a line whose branch is the wrong type` |
| `readPrHistory`: drop `typeof o['phase'] !== 'string'` (amended — see below, D-29) | `drops a line whose phase is the wrong type` |
| `readPrHistory`: drop `typeof o['recordedAt'] !== 'number'` (amended — see below, D-29) | `drops a line whose recordedAt is the wrong type` |
| `checkMailToken`: `legacy` → `ok` for a WRONG token | `refuses a WRONG token` |
| `checkMailToken`: `timingSafeEqual` → `===` | nothing (state this: it is a timing property, not a behavioural one, and no test can discriminate it — the mutant SURVIVES by construction and that is a disclosure, not a gap) |
| `readMailToken`: `code === 'ENOENT'` → always `return null` (D-33) | `THROWS when the file is present and unreadable` |
| `extractToken`: drop the `t.startsWith('#')` skip (D-34) | the `.example`-plus-value agreement case (server would read the whole comment blob, not the value line) |
| `extractToken`: `t.replace(/\s+/g, '')` → `t` (edges-only, the pre-D-34 `.trim()` scope) (D-34) | the embedded-space agreement case: the mutant keeps the interior space (measured: `"aaaa… bbbb…"`, 65 chars) where the shell side's `tr -d '[:space:]'` strips it (64 chars joined) — `shellExtract(p) !== readMailToken(p)` |
| `readMailToken`: `token === null` guard dropped, `extractToken`'s `null` returned as-is (D-35) | the three present-but-empty cases (0 bytes, whitespace-only, comment-only) all stop throwing |
| `deploy/notify.sh`: drop the `grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#'` stage (D-34) | the `.example`-plus-value agreement case (shell would carry the comment text into `$tok` too) |
| mail ingress: `verdict !== 'ok'` → `verdict === 'bad'` (restores the pre-D-36 legacy pass-through) (D-36) | the tokenless `POST /api/mail` case |
| mail ack: `verdict !== 'ok'` → `verdict === 'bad'` (restores the pre-D-36 legacy pass-through) (D-36) | the tokenless `POST /api/mail/:id/ack` case |
| mail ingress: `Buffer.byteLength` → `.length` | `measures the 8KB cap in UTF-8 BYTES` |
| mail ingress: drop the `fromUuid` comparison | `refuses stale-uuid` |
| mail ingress: reorder so oversize is checked after the registry read | no test fails — **a finding**: add an ordering assertion or accept and document |
| `MAIL_QUIET_MS` 60_000 → 0 | `does not deliver until the session has been quiet` |
| `MAIL_COOLDOWN_MS` → 0 | `does not deliver twice inside the cooldown` |
| `sweepMail`: `hs.state !== 'done'` → `=== 'working'` | `does not deliver while an ask is pending` |
| `sweepMail`: `readdir` null → treat as absent marker | `an unreadable kill-switch is a PAUSE` |
| `sweepMail`: pass `{replaceDraft: true}` | `never passes replaceDraft` |
| `sweepMail`: `enter-ignored` joins the backoff arm | `parks IMMEDIATELY on enter-ignored` |
| `sweepMail`: re-render instead of using `d.envelope` | `replays the SAME BYTES` |
| `sweepMail`: drop the `!this.primed` guard | `does not fire on the priming tick` |
| dispatch: check caps before the pause file | (amended — see below, D-58) `refuses while paused, before counting anything` |
| dispatch: `CCD_ARGV.start` instead of `ensure` for wave ≥ 2 | `runs ensure — never start` |
| close: archive instead of release on `final` | `releases the hold and archives NOTHING itself` |
| `pushOne`: `tag` override ignored | the non-collapsing mail-tag case |
| `pushOne`: `recordAlways` ignored (early return restored) | the presence-exemption case |
| `applyCatchUp`: `r.events` returned uncast | `turns an unknown kind into unknown` |
| `asFleetMsg`: accept `{type:'runs'}` with a non-array | the malformed-frame case |
| `watch.ts`: drop the `try`/`catch` around `pushOne`'s `recordFeedEvent` call (D-52) | (amended — see below, D-60) `recordFeedEvent, pushNewMail and pushNewRuns each swallow the throw, warn, and let the push through` |
| `watch.ts`: drop the `try`/`catch` around `pushNewMail`'s call in `tick()` (D-52) | (amended — see below, D-60) same case, `pushNewMail failed` warned |
| `watch.ts`: drop the `try`/`catch` around `pushNewRuns`'s call in `tick()` (D-52) | (amended — see below, D-60) same case, `pushNewRuns failed` warned |
| `watch.ts`: drop the shared `try`/`catch` around the priming tick's two watermark reads (D-52) | (amended — see below, D-60) `a break on the very first (priming) tick leaves the watermarks at 0 instead of crashing` |
| `watch.ts`: drop the `try`/`catch` around `emitRuns`'s `coord.runs()` call (D-52) | (amended — see below, D-60) same case, `emitRuns failed` warned |
| `server.ts`: drop the `try`/`catch` around `/ws/fleet`'s `deps.coord.runs()` read (D-52) | (amended — see below, D-60) new `fleetws.test.ts` case: the socket survives a broken db and a later frame still reaches it |
| `feedEvents`: `kind: r.kind as NotifyEvent['kind']` instead of `isNotifyKind(r.kind) ? r.kind : 'unknown'` (D-53) | (amended — see below, D-60) `reads a feed_events.kind token this build does not know as unknown` |
| `pushNewMail`: `m.project ?? sessionProjects.get(m.toId) ?? ''` reverted to `m.project ?? ''` (D-54) | (amended — see below, D-60) `falls back to the RECIPIENT session's own project for run-less mail in a multi-project fleet` |
| `pushOne`: drop the `e.project !== ''` decoration guard (D-54) | (amended — see below, D-60) `suppresses the decoration entirely … when even the recipient's project is unknown` |
| `pushOne`: `recordOnly` parameter ignored (D-55) | (amended — see below, D-60) `records the closing transition into the log and the feed, but never pushes it` |
| `pushNewRuns`: drop the `recordOnly: r.toState === 'closing'` argument (D-55) | (amended — see below, D-60) same case, both mutants independently reproduce the failure |
| mail ingress: the listed-but-unreadable arms (checks 5/6/7) collapsed to the terminal code (D-37) | `registry row … is listed but unreadable — transient, not a fact about the sender/recipient` cases |
| `checkMailToken`: `expected === null` returns `'ok'` instead of `'unconfigured'` (restores pre-D-39) (D-39) | `/api/mail`/`/api/mail/:id/ack` "fails shut on an unconfigured token" cases |
| `setDeliveryEnvelope`: render against `mail.id` instead of the delivery id, or skip the second write (D-41) | the ack-by-delivery-id case (`mail.id` and `delivery.id` walk apart the moment a mail resolves to a second delivery) |
| mail ingress: drop the `subject`/`artifacts` byte/count caps (D-44) | the oversize-subject / too-many-artifacts / oversize-artifact-path cases |
| `resolveCoordinator`: `toId === 'coordinator'` resolved to the literal string instead of the run's/program's claim (D-42) | `resolves toId:'coordinator' to the NAMED RUN's own claim, never the literal string` |
| dispatch: `run.state !== 'planned'` precondition dropped, or read outside the request's own serialisation (D-46, review findings 4/11/23) | `refuses a second dispatch before touching the fleet at all` AND `two dispatch requests fired CONCURRENTLY for the same run never both spawn a workspace` |
| dispatch: `clearedAt`/`clearError` not recorded, or the brief queued unconditionally regardless of whether `/clear` verified (D-47, review finding 12) | `leaves clearedAt null when the injected /clear is refused … the brief is NOT queued` AND `refuses dispatch with worker-busy when the hookstate says the session is still working` |
| close: the fleet act (`ws-release`/`ws-hold`/`ws-archive`) run AFTER the transition commit instead of before (D-48) | `answers 501 when the fleet ccd does not support ws-release, and the run stays RETRYABLE` |
| close: `state:'failed'` still runs `verifyDone` (D-49) | `closes state:failed WITHOUT re-measuring the claim — an operator abandon is not a done claim` |
| close: `coord.foldPrLineage` call dropped (D-50) | `folds real PR lineage read from a real .prhistory file — [] alone cannot discriminate the fold call being dropped` |
| close: `programOpenRunCount`/`setProgramState` never called, or called outside the close transaction (D-51, review finding 25) | `retires the program only once ALL its runs are terminal, mirroring whichever run closes it out` |

A survivor is a finding, not a pass.

**Amendment (whole-branch review, review finding 29): D-37..D-51 had no table rows at all** — D-60 amended this table for the Task 10 review round (D-52..D-55's rows, above) but the same gap over D-37..D-51 (spanning Tasks 6 through 9's own fix rounds) was never closed, so the table's own coverage claim was false for eleven deviations covering roughly a third of the branch's diff. The eleven rows immediately above close it: each names the construct, and each "must fail" column names a test that exists in the tree today (`mail-routes.test.ts`, `run-routes.test.ts`) and was confirmed, by inspection of what each case actually asserts, to discriminate the named mutant — not re-measured mutant-by-mutant here (that remains Task 11's own job before merge), but no longer a bare gap with zero rows either.

**Amendment (found in Task 2 review, three rows above):** the table as originally drafted paired
two `openCoordDb`/`tx` mutants with tests that provably cannot kill them, and asserted the third's
coverage on an assumption no test actually checked. Measured, one mutant at a time, full
`coord-db.test.ts`/`coord-store.test.ts` per mutant, sha256-verified restore between:

- **`current > VERSION` → `>=` (row 1 above).** The two operators differ only when
  `current === COORD_SCHEMA_VERSION`: the original enters the migration loop, whose guard
  `v < VERSION` is immediately false, and falls through to the same return as the mutant's
  rollback-read branch — identical database, identical return value. The *only* observable delta
  is the `console.warn` at `db.ts`'s rule-3 branch, which no test asserted on before this review
  (grepped: no `vi.spyOn`/`console.warn` anywhere in `coord-db.test.ts`). **This was a real gap,
  now closed**: `coord-db.test.ts`'s `READS a database from a NEWER build` case asserts
  `warnSpy` was called once, with the schema-mismatch text; its `reopens idempotently` case
  (`current === VERSION`, the boundary the mutant actually turns on) asserts `warnSpy` was
  **not** called. Confirmed by mutation: with the assertions in place, the `>=` mutant now fails
  both cases (2 failed of 10); reverting to the original comparison restores 10 passed.
- **`move PRAGMA user_version OUT of the migration tx` (row 2 above).** Ambiguous as drafted —
  moved *before* `tx(...)` it is catchable (the pragma would commit outside any transaction ahead
  of a migration that then fails, leaving `user_version` bumped on a database the `the file is
  UNTOUCHED` case catches); moved *after* `tx(...)`, still inside the same `try`, it is not: on
  the failure path execution never reaches the pragma statement in either variant (the throw from
  `tx()` propagates past it identically), and on the success path both variants leave the same
  schema and the same stamped version. The only place they differ is a crash *between* the tx
  commit and the now-unguarded pragma statement — the exact crash-window `db.ts`'s own comment
  names — which is not reachable from any synchronous, single-process, in-memory test in this
  suite. Measured: the "moved after" mutant leaves the full `coord-db.test.ts` +
  `coord-store.test.ts` run at 18 passed, unchanged. **This one is left surviving, disclosed
  rather than silently claimed covered** — the same treatment `checkMailToken`'s `timingSafeEqual`
  row below already gets. Closing it for real needs either a process-level crash-injection harness
  (out of scope for this suite's style) or accepting the disclosure.
- **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` → `BEGIN` (row 4 above, split out from the `ROLLBACK` row it was originally
  bundled with).** `DEFERRED` and `IMMEDIATE` differ only in *when* the write lock is taken —
  observable only with a second concurrent connection racing the first for the lock. Every test in
  `coord-db.test.ts` uses one connection, so `tx rolls back everything` (which the `ROLLBACK`-drop
  half of the original mutant genuinely fails) cannot discriminate the `BEGIN` half. Measured: the
  isolated mutant leaves the same 18-passed run unchanged. Disclosed for the same reason as the
  pragma-placement mutant above, not claimed caught.

This is the same finding class `aac518b` already made once against this file (a mutant with `tx()`
deleted from the migration loop producing byte-identical post-conditions) — reappearing in table
rows that were asserted rather than re-checked. The house precedent (`34605c8` amending D-6 for
overclaiming what an assertion catches) applies here too: the table now says what the suite
actually pins, not what the construct's *intent* was.

**Amendment (found in Task 4 review, D-14/D-16 above): three `readPrHistory` rows added, all
confirmed by mutation.** The table originally credited exactly one prhistory mutant (the
`includes`-arm row above), and Task 4 review measured that this left two more constructs
unguarded. All three added rows below were confirmed on isolated copies of
`prhistory.ts`/`coord-prhistory.test.ts`, one mutant at a time, restored between:

- **`listing === null` guard dropped.** Before the new directory-absent / directory-`chmod 000`
  cases existed, every case in this file used a registry directory that existed and was readable,
  so `io.readdir` never returned `null` anywhere in the suite. Measured: the mutated guard
  (`listing !== null && listing.includes(name)`) reproduced all four then-committed test results
  byte-for-byte — a clean survivor. With the two new cases in place, the mutant now throws inside
  `readPrHistory` on both (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'includes')`), which vitest
  reports as two failing tests.
- **The second-read confirmation dropped (reverting to read-then-list-and-refuse).** The new
  stub-`FleetIO` race case (`readdir`'s own callback writes the file mid-check) fails against this
  reversion with `{ok:false,error:'unreadable'}` where `{ok:true,entries:[…pr:1…]}` is expected —
  this is D-14's fix, pinned.
- **The four shape-reject guards deleted.** Measured pre-fix: deleting all four lines
  (`typeof o['pr'] !== 'number' …` through `typeof o['recordedAt'] !== 'number'`) left the
  suite's four original cases byte-identical, because the only negative case was a torn tail that
  never reaches the guards (`JSON.parse` throws first). The new wrong-shape case
  (`{"pr":"577","branch":null,"phase":7}` / `{}`) fails against the mutant: `{ok:true,entries:[]}`
  expected, `{ok:true,entries:[{pr:'577',…},{}]}` received.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 4 review, D-28..D-30 above): the entries-mapping field and
four of the five shape-reject guard constructs were still unpinned or bundled into one
undiscriminating mutant, and the MALFORMED-rung docstring's own test citation could not tell
ccd's whole-file policy apart from this reader's per-line one.** Measured, one construct at a
time, on isolated copies of `prhistory.ts`/`coord-prhistory.test.ts`/`ccd-prhistory.test.ts`,
restored between:

- **`entries.push` mapped to constants (D-28).** Every accept-direction assertion in the file was
  `expect.objectContaining({ pr })`, so `entries.push({ pr: o['pr'], branch: '', phase: '',
  recordedAt: 0 })` left the suite at 8/8 green — three of the four fields the module exists to
  carry were never checked. The `reads JSONL in order` case now asserts full-object equality
  against both entries; the mutant now fails it.
- **Four of the five shape-guard constructs (D-29).** The bundled wrong-shape fixture the Task 4
  amendment above credited (`{"pr":"577","branch":null,"phase":7}` / `{}`) dies on the FIRST
  guard, so the `Number.isInteger` conjunct and the `branch`/`phase`/`recordedAt` guards were
  never exercised in the reject direction. Measured: dropping `!Number.isInteger(o['pr'])` alone,
  dropping `typeof o['branch'] !== 'string' || typeof o['phase'] !== 'string'` alone, and dropping
  `typeof o['recordedAt'] !== 'number'` alone each left the bundled fixture byte-identical; only
  deleting all four guard lines together — the one mutant the row above actually names — killed
  it. Five new cases, each wrong in exactly one field, replace the bundled one; each construct's
  own deletion now fails exactly the case built for it.
- **The MALFORMED docstring's test citation (D-30).** `ccd-prhistory.test.ts:105`'s ledger is one
  line; a single bad line answers `[]` under both whole-file discard and per-line salvage, so it
  cannot tell ccd's own policy apart from this reader's deliberate divergence from it, despite the
  docstring's claim that it "pins exactly that." A new case (`ccd-prhistory.test.ts:126`) puts a
  good row ahead of a bad one — the shape that discriminates the two policies — and the docstring
  now cites it instead.

**Amendment (found in Task 5 review, D-17..D-22 above): the two Task-5 rows were false credits,
and seven more constructs had no row at all.** All measured on isolated copies of
`fingerprint.ts`/`gitref.ts`/`coord-fingerprint.test.ts`, one mutant at a time, restored between:

- **`readBranchTip`: drop the `..` guard (row above).** The scheduled must-fail case, `refuses a
  branch name that could climb out`, used escape targets that do not exist in the fixture — an
  implementation with NO path guards at all still answers `null` for all four (D-21). With the two
  new decoy-backed cases in place, dropping the `..` guard alone now returns
  `<root>/demo/.git/refs/x`'s planted sha instead of `null`, and dropping both guards returns
  `<root>/loot`'s.
- **`verifyDone`: `measured === 'unknown'` → treat as `ok` (row above).** The scheduled must-fail
  case, `pr-state could not be read`, models an exec failure (`res.ok === false`) and never reaches
  `prVerdict` at all — `phaseFor` was never made to return `phase: 'unknown'` anywhere in the
  original suite (D-22). The new `merge-unproven` row (a MERGED gh row with a failed `isMergedRow`
  conjunct) reaches this clause through `phaseFor`; deleting it now settles that row `ok` instead
  of refusing `pr-unmeasurable`.
- **Nine rows added** (D-17, D-18, D-19, D-20, D-22), one per newly-added test: the loose-ref-
  shadows-packed-refs fix, the live-registry branch resolution, the `prPhase`/`prNumber` runtime
  validation, the project guard's `.`/`..` exclusion, the `prNumber` mismatch arm,
  `verbSupported`-false, the "no full line" refusal, `prVerdict`'s `claimed === 'merged'` arm, and
  the `packed-refs` sha/ref-name column check. Each confirmed to flip its matching test from pass
  to fail under mutation, restored to the fixed source between — the `ok`-payload `measured`
  assertion (also D-22) has no row of its own here because it pins a *value*, not a branch a
  single deleted construct can flip; it is credited in the deviation entry instead.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 5 review, D-31/D-32 above): one row added, and the D-19 row's
must-fail case is corrected to say which half of the fallthrough it actually covers.** Measured on
isolated copies of `gitref.ts`/`fingerprint.ts`/`coord-fingerprint.test.ts`, restored between:

- **`readBranchTip`: drop the `io.stat` presence check on the null-read arm (D-31).** Before this
  fix, `readFile === null` fell straight through to `packed-refs` with no presence check at all —
  the mutant described here is that same pre-fix behavior, and it reproduced every test in the file
  byte-for-byte (D-19's own must-fail case, "a symref loose ref must not read the stale packed
  entry," exercises only the READABLE-but-non-SHA arm, never this one — the readable-loose-ref
  branch returns before the mutated line is ever reached). With the new `chmod 000` and
  directory-shaped-loose-ref cases in place, deleting the `io.stat` check now returns the stale
  `packed-refs` sha in both instead of `null`; the paired end-to-end `verifyDone` case (a STALE
  claim naming that same stale sha) now settles `ok: true` instead of refusing `tip-unmeasurable`.
  The D-19 row above is corrected to name its actual coverage rather than the whole construct.
- **`verifyDone`'s `run.branch`-fallback comment (D-32) has no mutation row of its own.** It is a
  documentation fix — the fallback's *behaviour* was already correct and unchanged (fail-shut
  throughout, per D-31's own retry argument) — so there is no construct a mutant could delete to
  make a test fail; the three new cases pinned in D-32 are regression guards on the mechanism the
  corrected comment now describes, not discriminators of a behavioural change. Recorded here rather
  than left unlisted, the same way D-4 and the `checkMailToken`/`timingSafeEqual` row above disclose
  a deliberately uncredited case instead of silently omitting it.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 2 review, D-23/D-24 above): two rows added.** Both measured on
isolated copies of `schema.ts`/`db.ts`/`coord-db.test.ts`, sha256-verified restore between:

- **`schema.ts`: re-add `mail_deliveries_replay` (D-23).** With the index dropped and the new
  `mail_deliveries carries exactly the index dueDeliveries can use` case in place, restoring the
  dead index flips that case from pass to fail (`toEqual(['mail_deliveries_due'])` receives both
  names back). Without that case, re-adding it is a silent survivor — no functional query in the
  suite ever touches it, by construction (EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN never selects it either way).
- **`shouldRemoveMigrationFailureArtifact`: drop the `!existedBefore` guard (D-24).** Isolated the
  same way `describeMigrationProgress` already is in this file, and for the matching reason: the
  combination the guard exists for (`existedBefore === true`, post-rollback size `0`) cannot be
  produced through `openCoordDb` itself — the top-of-function guard already refuses any
  pre-existing 0-byte file before a migration is attempted, so the mutant passes every
  `openCoordDb`-level test in the suite unchanged (measured: full `coord-db.test.ts` run
  unaffected). Pinned directly against the function: `shouldRemoveMigrationFailureArtifact(true, 0)`
  goes from `false` to `true` under the mutant, flipping the matching unit test.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 3 review, D-27 above): three rows added, all confirmed by
mutation.** D-8's own closing note and `schema.ts`'s header/DDL comments say the we-do-not-know
gap is shut for all five v1 enum columns; the table before this amendment scheduled exactly one
guard mutant (`run()`'s copy) to check that. Measured, one mutant at a time, full
`coord-store.test.ts` + `mail-routes.test.ts`, restored between (sha256-verified):

- **`advance()`: `from = row.state as RunState` instead of the `isRunState` guard.** Before the
  new "refuses a transition from a state token this build does not know" case existed, nothing
  in the suite ever wrote an out-of-vocabulary `runs.state` and then called `advance` on it — the
  existing unknown-state case (`run()`'s own test) only ever calls `run()`, never `advance()`, on
  such a row. Measured: the mutant leaves the full suite passing unchanged. With the new case in
  place, the mutant makes `RUN_TRANSITIONS['reconciling']` `undefined`, so
  `.includes(to)` throws a `TypeError` where `{ok:false, error:'bad-transition', from:'unknown',
  to:'working'}` is expected — vitest reports it as a failing test, not merely a wrong value.
- **`programs()`: `state: r.state as ProgramState` instead of the `isProgramState` guard.**
  Measured: the mutant leaves the full suite passing unchanged (no prior case ever wrote an
  out-of-vocabulary `programs.state`). The new case writes `'archived'` directly and asserts

  the mutant the assertion receives `'archiving'` back and fails.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 6 fix pass, D-33..D-36 above): eight rows added, seven
confirmed by mutation.** The table before this amendment carried the two original `checkMailToken`
rows and nothing for `readMailToken`, `extractToken`, `notify.sh`'s extraction pipeline, or the
mail routes' token gate — every construct D-33..D-36 touched. Measured, one mutant at a time,

in full per mutant):

  '\n')` returns the 65-character string with the interior space intact; the shell side's `tr -d
  '[:space:]'` on the identical bytes returns the 64-character string with it removed. The new
  "value line with an embedded space" fixture in `coord-token.test.ts` catches exactly this via the
  same `shellExtract`/`readMailToken` equality assertion.

  three `it.each` cases added for D-35 (`expect(() => readMailToken(p)).toThrow(...)`) each fail

- **Mail ack: the same mutant on the ack route's gate (D-36).** Measured in the same run: the
  ack-route case in "applies the SAME token and attribution gate as the ingress" (the `noToken`
  assertion) observes 200 `{ok:true, already:false}` instead of 401 — the tokenless ack succeeds
  outright, since attribution behind the gate has nothing else to refuse it on. Both mutants

  diff before re-applying the fix.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 9 review, D-46..D-51 above): eight rows added, all confirmed by
mutation.** The base table's three Task-9 rows ("dispatch: check caps before the pause file",
"dispatch: `CCD_ARGV.start` instead of `ensure`", "close: archive instead of release on `final`")
covered none of the ordering/gating fixes this review found and closed. Measured, one mutant at a
time against `server/src/coord/routes.ts`, sha256-verified restore between, `run-routes.test.ts`
run in full per mutant:

- **dispatch: drop the `run.state !== 'planned'` precondition (D-46).** Measured: reverting the
  check to `if (false)` leaves the base suite green (30/30) but fails both new double-dispatch
  cases. The same-run (wave-1) retry observes `calls.length` grown from 2 to 9 — the second call
  reads `run.sessionId` already set by the FIRST dispatch's own `markDispatched` and takes the
  wave-N≥2 branch regardless: `ws-add`/`ws-hold` (the first call) then `ensure`, a full `/clear`
  injection (`capture-pane`/`send-keys …/clear`/`capture-pane`/`send-keys …Enter`/`capture-pane`),
  and a second `ws-hold`, before the trailing `advance()` finally 409s — where the fixed code runs
  none of that. The wave-2 retry case observes a SECOND `send-keys` call (a second `/clear`
  injected into the already-resumed worker) where none should fire.
- **dispatch: drop the `detail` argument on the post-`/clear` `advance()` call (D-47).** Measured:
  the refused-`/clear` case's `runEvents` assertion receives `detail: null` where
  `'clear-refused:dialog-open'` was expected; every other case (which never refuses a `/clear`)
  stays green.
- **dispatch: `briefQueued` computed as an unconditional `true` (D-47).** Measured: the same
  refused-`/clear` case's response-shape assertion receives `briefQueued: true` where `false` was
  expected; the wave-1 and successful-wave-2 cases (where `true` is correct either way) stay green,
  confirming the mutant is caught only by the case that actually discriminates it.
- **close: drop the `RUN_TRANSITIONS[run.state].includes('closing')` precondition (D-48).**
  Measured: the double-close case observes a SECOND `ws-release` call (`calls.filter(...).length`
  grown to 2) where the retry should have refused before touching the fleet at all; the lone-501
  case is unaffected (it never closes twice), confirming the two D-48 mutants are independent and
  each caught by exactly the case that names it.
- **close: the fleet act reverted to running AFTER `advance('closing')`/`advance(state)` (D-48).**
  Measured by swapping the two blocks back to their pre-fix order: the 501-unsupported case now
  reads `coord.run(id)!.state === 'done'` where `'dispatched'` was expected — the run committed
  terminal before the fleet act that then failed — while the double-close case stays green (the
  transition precondition above still fires correctly regardless of act ordering).
- **close: `state !== 'failed'` gate on `verifyDone` reverted to always calling it (D-49).**
  Measured: the state:`'failed'`-against-a-stale-tip case observes `409 stale-tip` where `200` was
  expected — the abandon path is refused by the same re-measurement a `done` claim needs; the
  `done`-path stale-tip case (the first test in the file) is unaffected, confirming the mutant only
  breaks the abandon path the fix specifically opened.
- **close: the `programOpenRunCount(...) === 0` branch reverted to `if (false)` (D-51).** Measured:
  the two-runs-one-program case observes `programs()` still reading `'active'` after the SECOND
  (last) run closes, where `'abandoned'` was expected; the sibling-still-open assertion earlier in
  the same case (which expects `'active'` regardless) stays green, confirming the mutant is caught
  specifically by the retirement half of the test, not the non-retirement half.
- **close: drop `coord.foldPrLineage(id, history.entries)` at the call site (D-50, test-coverage
  gap — no code changed by this fix, only the test).** Measured: with the NEW real-`.prhistory`
  case in place, commenting out the call leaves `coord.run(id)!.prLineage` reading `[]` where the
  real folded entry was expected; the PRE-EXISTING absent-ledger case (asserting `[]` for a run
  with no `.prhistory` file at all) stays green under the same mutant, which is exactly the
  discrimination gap D-50 records: before this fix, ONLY the absent-ledger case existed, and it
  cannot tell the fold call's presence from its absence.

**Amendment (found in a later Task 11 review, D-58/D-59 above): two rows corrected, one split.**
Both false credits, measured directly against the shipped modules, sha256-verified restore after

  (`dispatchedIn24h: 1`) while the rest of the file (64/65) stays green.

  (grouping dispatches by `toDateString()`) now fails this case deterministically (`200` where
  `409 cap-daily` was expected) on every run, not just 23 hours out of 24; the rest of the file

- [ ] **Step 6: Deploy — agent first, then server**

**STATUS (D-61, later Task 11 review): NOT RUN.** This step and Step 7 below require SSH to two
real boxes, a locally-minted `deploy/ccrc-mail.token`, a real deploy, and live multi-session
traffic — all of which a review round bound by "no `ssh`, no network mutations" cannot perform.
`23ef195` shipped Step 1's README correction only; Steps 6-8 remain exactly as drafted below,
unexecuted. **PR I is not complete until an operator with deploy access runs both.**

This ships `deploy/notify.sh` (fleet host) **and** the server, and the ordering is the standing discipline (`README.md:417-423`, `spec:277-280`: *"agent-first is trivially satisfied — no ccd changes"*). It is also what makes the legacy tolerance unnecessary in practice:

1. `CCRC_SSH_KEY=~/.ssh/your-key-b bash deploy/deploy.sh agent you@198.51.100.7` — ships the token to `~/.cc-secrets/ccrc-mail.token` and the new `notify.sh`.
2. `CCRC_SSH_KEY=~/.ssh/your-key-b bash deploy/deploy.sh` — ships the token to `~/.ccrc/mail.token` and the server, now behind `verify-service.sh ccrc.service`.

`deploy/ccrc-mail.token` must exist locally first (`openssl rand -hex 32`), or `ship_secret` silently skips and both boxes stay unauthenticated — which is the same behaviour `ship_env` already has, and the reason the boot warning in `index.ts` exists.

- [ ] **Step 7: Verify the real thing**

Behavioural, demonstrated, not inferred. **No destructive verbs, no touching live sessions beyond the one deliberate delivery.**

1. `curl -fsS .../health` and `/api/fleet/health` — still `{"ok":true}`, still `{"mode":"remote"}`.
2. `POST /api/notify` **without** the header → 200, and `journalctl --user -u ccrc` shows the `legacy` line. **With** a wrong token → 401. With the right one → 200 and the notice reaches a connected PWA.
3. `ls -l ~/.ccrc/coord.db*` on the server box: the database and its `-wal` exist, owned by the service user. `PRAGMA user_version` reads `1`.
4. `GET /api/runs` → `{"runs":[]}` on a fresh database.
5. Open a run against a scratch workspace, dispatch it, and watch: `ccd ls` shows a new workspace, its `.hold` reads `program:<slug> wave:1/N`, `GET /api/runs` shows `dispatched`, and **within one `MAIL_SWEEP_MS` after the pane goes quiet for a minute, the wave brief appears in the pane inside its fence** — typed, not pasted, with the ack instructions visible.
6. Ack it from that session with `curl`; confirm the delivery stops replaying (wait past `MAIL_REPLAY_MS`, or read the row).
7. **The negatives that matter most:**
   - `touch ~/.cc-sessions/coordinator-paused` → dispatch answers `409 refused:paused`. `rm` it.
   - `touch ~/.cc-sessions/mail-disabled` → a queued message is not delivered on any sweep. `rm` it.
   - Type a half-finished sentence into a recipient's box and leave it: the next sweep must **not** clobber it, and the delivery must back off.
   - Post a done-claim with a `branchTip` that is one commit stale: `409 stale-tip`, the run state unchanged, and the code mailed back.
8. **Bug #21 (`spec:181-183`), formally closed or formally restated.** Deliver at least ten messages across at least three sessions and count typed-but-unsent outcomes. Zero → record the run and close #21 with the evidence, naming `sendPrompt`'s two-Enter proof (`inject/send.ts:98-112, 380-396`) as what covers it. Any → **do not close it**; record the pane tail and the `SendResult`, and say so in the PR. Closing it on the strength of "the send path's own pins largely cover it" without the count is exactly the kind of claim this repo has had to retract before.

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit and open the PR**

```bash
git add README.md
git commit -m "docs: ccrc has a database, runs on two boxes, and both sentences that said otherwise are corrected"
```

- **The zero-new-verbs claim was checked, not assumed.** `agent/src/whitelist.ts:310-336` grants `start`, `enable`, `ensure`, `stop`, `swap`, `ws-add`, `pr-state --session|--project`, `pr-open --session`, `ws-archive --session`, `ws-restore --session`, `ws-audit --session`, `ws-reap --expect`, `ws-attic --session`, `ws-hold --session`, `ws-release --session`, `ws-rename --session`. Every act Task 9 needs is on that list, and `CCD_ARGV` (`ccdargv.ts:56-85`) already mints each one. **`whitelist-subset.test.ts` passing untouched is the proof**, because it enumerates the two tables against each other in both directions.
- **`verb-gate.test.ts` was read before the gates were placed.** `UNGATED_BY_DECISION` is `{start, enable, ensure, ws-add, stop, swap}` (`:58-60`), so `wsAdd`/`ensure` need no `verbSupported` and `wsHold`/`wsRelease`/`wsArchive`/`prStateSession` do — which is why `verifyDone` asks the question above its call rather than beside it, and why the dispatch route's hold is gated and its spawn is not.
- **The `tasks` collision was measured, not assumed:** `shared/api.ts:82-105` (`TaskStatus`/`TaskItem`/`TaskProgress`), the `{type:'tasks'}` arm at **`:1260`**, `server/src/tasks/read.ts`, `pwa/src/session/TaskStrip.tsx`. Hence `WorkItem`, `work_items`, `items` — and a structural guard rather than a comment.
- **`node:sqlite` was executed on this box while writing the plan**, not recalled: `node v24.14.1`, `require('node:sqlite')` exports `DatabaseSync, StatementSync, Session, constants, backup` with the experimental warning, and a create/insert/select round trip returns 1. The `22.13.0` floor is the one number in this plan taken from memory rather than measurement — **D-6** says so. Task 1's third assertion catches the interpreter that actually runs CI falling below whatever the real boundary is; it does not independently confirm `22.13.0` is that boundary's exact value (D-6, amended).
- **The `~/.cc-secrets` unreadability was checked against the whitelist, not inferred from the scouts:** `checkPath`'s read arm (`agent/src/whitelist.ts:82-88`) allows `.cc-sessions`, `.cc-limits`, `.cc-clips`, the projects root and `$HOME/.claude*`. `.cc-secrets` matches none, which is the structural reason the token exists in two copies rather than one.
- **The `ccrc.service` EnvironmentFile gap is a real contradiction I could not resolve read-only** (`deploy/ccrc.service` has no such line, `deploy.sh:103` overwrites the installed unit every deploy, and the live server is nonetheless in remote mode). **D-4** records it and routes around it rather than guessing; resolving it needs a look at `~/.config/systemd/user/ccrc.service.d/` on `server-box`, which this plan does not do.
- **The delivery-cost arithmetic is the scout's measurement, carried and attributed:** `sendPrompt` costs one `sendLiteral` per line plus an `M-Enter` between lines (`inject/send.ts:300-305`), ≈4.3 s of sleeps worst case from the constants at `:26-36`, and every tmux call is an agent round trip in remote mode. It is stated in `envelope.ts`'s docstring as a cost, not designed around, because the cap is the spec's.
- **`limits.ts:134-142` was read before `mail-disabled`/`coordinator-paused` were named**: it filters `<name>-disabled` markers that are not known wrappers out of `/api/accounts`, so neither new marker can fabricate an account row. `coordinator-paused` does not even end in `-disabled`, and that is deliberate — it is not a lane kill-switch and should not read as one.
- **Not verified, and stated as such:** (a) that `server-box` runs a Node satisfying the floor — the spec records `v22.22.3` and this plan did not ssh there; (b) the exact Node version at which `node:sqlite` lost its flag (**D-6**); (c) whether `packed-refs` is present in the live project repos — `readBranchTip` handles both and the tests cover both, but which path fires in production is unmeasured; (d) that ten deliveries is enough evidence to close bug #21 — it is the count Task 11 Step 7.8 asks for, and if the failure is rarer than 1-in-10 that step will close it wrongly. If the implementer can afford more, take more.
